, but we are leaving this implicit to avoid complicated notation.
In Section
IV.4
we shall restrict
K
and
\Lambda
so that
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
does not depend any longer on the choice of
\Lambda
. There we shall use the following observation:

Lemma II.5.6
.

Let
a,b\in K
be such that
a-b\in(\mathcal{O}^{\times})^{\dagger}
. Then for all
\gamma
we have
\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{a}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{b}}(\gamma)
; in particular,
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{a})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{b})
.

Proof.

Use that if
u\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}
and
a-b=u^{\dagger}
, then
A_{a}=(A_{b})_{\ltimes u}
.
∎

Corollary II.5.7
.

Let
\Lambda^{*}
be a complement of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear subspace
K^{\dagger}
of
K
and let
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{*}\colon\Lambda\to\Lambda^{*}
be the group isomorphism with
\lambda-\lambda^{*}\in K^{\dagger}
for all
\lambda
. If
\lambda-\lambda^{*}\in(\mathcal{O}^{\times})^{\dagger}
for all
\lambda
, then
\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)=\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda^{*}}}(\gamma)
for all
\gamma
, so
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)=\bigcup_{\lambda}\,{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda^{*}})
.

Remark II.5.8
.

For
a\in K^{\times}
we have

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(aA)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)

and
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(Aa)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)-va
.
Note also that
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{0})\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.
Let
\phi\in K^{\times}
be active in
K
, and set
\lambda^{\phi}:=\phi^{-1}\lambda
.
Then
\Lambda^{\phi}:=\phi^{-1}\Lambda
is a complement of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear subspace
(K^{\phi})^{\dagger}=\phi^{-1}K^{\dagger}
of
K^{\phi}
,
and
(A^{\phi})_{\lambda^{\phi}}=(A_{\lambda})^{\phi}
. Hence
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{K}(A)
agrees with the set
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{K^{\phi}}(A^{\phi})
of ultimate exceptional values of
A^{\phi}
with respect to
\Lambda^{\phi}
.

Remark II.5.9
.

Suppose
L
is an
H
-asymptotic extension of
K
with asymptotic integration and algebraically closed constant field
C_{L}
such that

L^{\dagger}
is divisible, and
\Psi
is cofinal in
\Psi_{L}
or
K
is
\uplambda
-free.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})\cap\Gamma
, by Lemma
I.4.1
and Corollary
I.7.10
. Hence if
\Lambda_{L}\supseteq\Lambda
is a complement of the subspace
L^{\dagger}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
L
, and
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{L}(A)
is the set of ultimate exceptional values of
A
(viewed as an element of
L[\der]
) with respect to
\Lambda_{L}
, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{L}(A)
. (Note that such a complement
\Lambda_{L}
exists iff
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
.)

In the rest of this subsection we equip
{\operatorname{U}}
with the gaussian extension
v_{\operatorname{g}}
of the valuation of
K
.
Recall that we have a decomposition
\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=\bigoplus_{\lambda}(\ker A_{\lambda})\operatorname{e}(\lambda)
of the
C
-linear space
\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A

as an internal direct sum of subspaces,
and hence

(II.5.2)

v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\ =\ \bigcup_{\lambda}\,v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})\ \subseteq\ \bigcup_{\lambda}\,{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A).

Here are some consequences:

Lemma II.5.10
.

Suppose
K
is
r
-linearly newtonian. Then
v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.

Proof.

By Proposition
I.4.2
we have
v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})
for each
\lambda
. Therefore
v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
by (
II.5.2
).
∎

Lemma II.5.11
.

Suppose
K
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued. Then
\lvert v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\rvert\leqslant\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A\leqslant r
.

Proof.

By [ADH, 5.6.6(i)] applied to
A_{\lambda}
in place of
A
we have

\lvert v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})\rvert\ =\ \dim_{C}\ker A_{\lambda}\ =\ \operatorname{mult}_{\lambda}(A)\qquad\text{ for all~$\lambda$}

and thus by (
II.5.2
),

\lvert v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\rvert\ \leqslant\ \sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})\rvert\ =\ \sum_{\lambda}\,\operatorname{mult}_{\lambda}(A)\ =\ \dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A\ \leqslant\ r

as claimed.
∎

Lemma II.5.12
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
r=1
. Then

v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker^{\neq}_{{\operatorname{U}}}A)\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A),\qquad\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\rvert\ =\ 1.

Proof.

Arrange
A=\der-g
,
g\in K
, and take
f\in K^{\times}
and
\lambda
such that
g=f^{\dagger}+\lambda
. Then
u:=f\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\in{\operatorname{U}}^{\times}

satisfies
A(u)=0
, hence
\ker^{\neq}_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=Cu
and thus
v_{\text{g}}(\ker^{\neq}_{{\operatorname{U}}}A)=\{vf\}
.
By Lemma
I.4.9
we have
v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})
for all
\lambda
and
hence
v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker^{\neq}_{{\operatorname{U}}}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
by (
II.5.2
).
∎

Corollary II.5.13
.

If
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
a\in K^{\times}
, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(\der-a^{\dagger})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(\der-a^{\dagger})=\{va\}
.

Proposition
II.5.15
below partly extends Lemma
II.5.12
.

Spectral extensions and ultimate exceptional values

In this subsection
K
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued of
H
-type with small derivation, asymptotic integration, and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Also
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
has order
r
and
\gamma
ranges over
\Gamma
.

Suppose
\Omega
is equipped with a spectral extension
v
of the valuation of
K
. Let
{g\in K^{\times}}
with
vg=\gamma
.
The Newton weight of
A_{\lambda}g\in K[\der]
does not change in passing from
K
to
\Omega
, since

\Psi
is cofinal in
\Psi_{\Omega}
by Lemma
II.5.3
; see [ADH, 11.1].
Thus

\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)\ =\ \operatorname{nwt}(A_{\lambda}g)\ =\ \operatorname{nwt}\!\big(Ag\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\ =\ \operatorname{nwt}_{A}\!\big(v(g\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\ =\ \operatorname{nwt}_{A}\!\big(\gamma+v(\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\big).

In particular, using
\Gamma_{\Omega}=\Gamma\oplus v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big)
,

(II.5.3)

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)\ =\ \bigcup_{\lambda}\,{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\qquad\text{(a disjoint union)}.

Thus
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)=\pi\big({\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)\big)
where
\pi\colon\Gamma_{\Omega}\to\Gamma
is given by

\pi\big({\gamma+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\lambda)}\big)\big)=\gamma
.

Lemma II.5.14
.

We have
\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A\leqslant\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert
, and

\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A\ =\ \sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert\ \Longleftrightarrow\ v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\text{ for all $\lambda$.}

Moreover, if
\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=\sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert
, then
v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.

Proof.

Clearly,
\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A\leqslant\dim_{C}\ker_{\Omega}A
.
Equip
\Omega
with a spectral extension of the valuation of
K
.
Then
\dim_{C}\ker_{\Omega}A=\lvert v(\ker_{\Omega}^{\neq}A)\rvert
and
v(\ker_{\Omega}^{\neq}A)\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
by [ADH, 5.6.6(i)] and [ADH, p. 481], respectively, applied to
\Omega
in the role of
K
.
Also
|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)|=\sum_{\lambda}|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})|
(a sum of cardinals) by the remarks preceding the lemma. This yields the first claim of the lemma.

Next, note that
v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})
for all
\lambda
.
Hence from (
II.5.3
) and

v(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\ =\ \bigcup_{\lambda}v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\qquad\text{(a disjoint union)}

we obtain:

v(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad v(\ker^{\neq}A_{\lambda})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\text{ for all $\lambda$.}

Also

\lvert v(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\rvert=\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A
by [ADH, 2.3.13],
and

v(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\ \subseteq\ v(\ker_{\Omega}^{\neq}A)\ \subseteq\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A),\qquad|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)|\ =\ \sum_{\lambda}|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})|.

This yields the displayed equivalence.

Suppose
\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert
;
we need to show
v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.
We have
\pi\big({\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)\big)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
for the above projection map
\pi\colon\Gamma_{\Omega}\to\Gamma
, so it is enough to show

\pi\big(v(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\big)=v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)
.
For that, note that for
\mathcal{B}\subseteq K^{\times}\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)
in Corollary
II.5.4
we have

\pi\big(v(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\big)\ =\ \pi(v\mathcal{B})\ =\ v_{\operatorname{g}}(\mathcal{B})\ =\ v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A),

using for the last equality the details in the proof of Corollary
II.5.4
.
∎

Proposition II.5.15
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free.
Then
\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)=0
for all but finitely many pairs
(\gamma,\lambda)
and

\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\rvert\ \leqslant\ \sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert\ =\ \sum_{\gamma,\lambda}\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)\ \leqslant\ r.

If
\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=r
, then
\sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert=r
and
v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.

Proof.

Equip
\Omega
with a spectral extension
v
of the valuation of
K
.
Then
\Omega
is
\upomega
-free, so
\sum_{\lambda}|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})|=|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)|\leqslant r
by the remarks preceding Lemma
II.5.14
and Corollary
I.4.5
applied to
\Omega
in place of
K
. These remarks also give

\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)=0
for all but finitely many pairs
(\gamma,\lambda)
, and so

\sum_{\gamma,\lambda}\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)\ =\ \sum_{\gamma,\lambda}\operatorname{nwt}_{A}\!\big(\gamma+v(\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\ =\ \lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)\rvert\ \leqslant\ r.

Corollary
I.4.5
applied to
A_{\lambda}
in place of
A
yields
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert=\sum_{\gamma}\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)
and so
\sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert=\sum_{\gamma,\lambda}\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)
. This proves the first part (including the display). The rest follows from this and Lemma
II.5.14
.
∎

In the next lemma (to be used in the proof of Proposition
III.1.27
), as well as in Corollary
II.5.21
,
L
is a
\operatorname{d}
-valued
H
-asymptotic extension of
K
with
algebraically closed constant field and asymptotic integration (so
L
has small derivation), such that
L^{\dagger}
is divisible,
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
, and
\operatorname{I}(L)\subseteq L^{\dagger}
. We also fix there a complement
\Lambda_{L}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear subspace
L^{\dagger}
of
L
with

\Lambda\subseteq\Lambda_{L}
. Let
{\operatorname{U}}_{L}=L\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda_{L})\big]
be
the corresponding universal exponential extension
of
L
containing
{\operatorname{U}}=K\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big]
as a differential subring, as
described in the remarks following Corollary
II.2.13
,
with differential fraction field
\Omega_{L}
.

Lemma II.5.16
.

Assume
C_{L}=C
. Let
\Omega_{L}
be equipped with a spectral extension of the valuation of
L
, and take
\Omega
as a valued subfield of
\Omega_{L}
; so the valuation of
\Omega
is a spectral extension of the valuation of
K
. Suppose
\Psi
is cofinal in
\Psi_{L}
or
K
is
\uplambda
-free.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega_{L}}(A)\cap\Gamma_{\Omega}\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
range over
\Lambda_{L}
.
We have

\Gamma_{\Omega_{L}}\ =\ \Gamma_{L}\oplus v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda_{L})\big),\qquad\Gamma_{\Omega}\ =\ \Gamma\oplus v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big)

by Lemma
II.5.3
and

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega_{L}}\ =\ \bigcup_{\mu}{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\mu})+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\mu)\big),\qquad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}\ =\ \bigcup_{\lambda}{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)

by (
II.5.3
). Hence

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega_{L}}(A)\cap\Gamma_{\Omega}\ =\ \bigcup_{\lambda}\big({\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})\cap\Gamma\big)+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\ =\ \bigcup_{\lambda}{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})+v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\lambda)\big)\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A),

where we used the injectivity of
\mu\mapsto v\big(\!\operatorname{e}(\mu)\big)

for the first equality and Remark
II.5.9
for the second.
∎

The material in the rest of this section is not used later in this monograph.

Terminal operators

In this subsection we continue to work in the setting of the previous subsection.

Call
A

terminal
with respect to
\Lambda

if
\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert=r
. We omit “with respect to
\Lambda
” if it is clear from the context what
\Lambda
is.
In Section
IV.4
we shall restrict
K
,
\Lambda
anyway so that this dependence on
\Lambda
disappears.
Recall also that for a given spectral extension of the valuation of
K
to
\Omega
we have
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}_{\Omega}(A)\rvert=\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert
.
If
A
is terminal and
\phi\in K^{\times}
is active in
K
, then
A^{\phi}\in K^{\phi}[\derdelta]
is terminal
with respect to
\Lambda^{\phi}
(cf. remarks after Corollary
II.5.7
). If
A
is terminal and
a\in K^{\times}
, then
aA
is terminal.
If
r=0
, then
A
is terminal.

Lemma II.5.17
.

If
r=1
, then
A
is terminal.

Proof.

Assume
r=1
. Then
\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=1
, so
\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert\geqslant 1

by Lemma
II.5.14
. Equip
\Omega
with a spectral extension of the valuation of
K
. Then

\Omega
is ungrounded by Lemma
II.5.3
, and
r=1
gives
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}_{\Omega}(A)\rvert\leqslant 1
. Now use

\lvert{\mathscr{E}}_{\Omega}(A)\rvert=\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert
.
∎

Lemma II.5.18
.

Suppose
A
and
B\in K[\der]^{\neq}
are terminal, and each operator
B_{\lambda}
is asymptotically surjective. Then
AB
is terminal.

Proof.

Use that
(AB)_{\lambda}=A_{\lambda}B_{\lambda}
, and that

\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda}B_{\lambda})\rvert=\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert+\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(B_{\lambda})\rvert
by Corollary
I.4.19
.
∎

Thus if
A
is terminal and
a\in K^{\times}
, then
aA,Aa
, and
A_{\ltimes a}
are terminal. From
Lemmas
II.5.17
,
II.5.18
, and Corollary
I.4.25
we conclude:

Corollary II.5.19
.

If
K
is
\uplambda
-free and
A
splits over
K
, then
A
is terminal.

Corollary II.5.20
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free and
B\in K[\der]^{\neq}
. Then
A
and
B
are terminal iff
AB
is terminal.

Proof.

The “only if” part follows from Lemma
II.5.18
and Corollary
I.4.26
.
For the “if” part, use Corollary
I.4.19
and Proposition
II.5.15
.
∎

Corollary II.5.21
.

Suppose
A
is terminal,
\Psi
is cofinal in
\Psi_{L}
or
K
is
\uplambda
-free, and
L
is
\upomega
-free. Then, with respect to the complement
\Lambda_{L}
of
L^{\dagger}
in
L
, we have:

(i)

as an element of
L[\der]
,
A
is terminal;

(ii)

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\mu})=\emptyset
for all
\mu\in\Lambda_{L}\setminus\Lambda
;

(iii)

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})
for all
\lambda
; and

(iv)

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{L}(A)
.

Proof.

By the remarks after Corollary
II.5.7
we have
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})
for each
\lambda
,
and so with
\mu
ranging over
\Lambda_{L}
, by Proposition
II.5.15
applied to
L
in place of
K
,
we have
r=\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert\leqslant\sum_{\mu}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\mu})\rvert\leqslant r
. This yields (i)–(iv).
∎

In
[
8
]
we shall study other situations where
A
is terminal.

The real case

In this subsection
H
is a real closed
H
-field with small derivation, asymptotic integration, and
H^{\dagger}=H
;
also
K=H[\imag]
,
\imag^{2}=-1
, for our valued
differential field
K
. We also assume
\operatorname{I}(H)\imag\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.

Then
K
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued of
H
-type with small derivation, asymptotic integration,
K^{\dagger}=H+\operatorname{I}(H)\imag
,
and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Note that
H
and thus
K
is
\uplambda
-free by [ADH, remark after 11.6.2, and 11.6.8].
Let
A
in
K[\der]^{\neq}
have order
r
and let
\gamma
range over
\Gamma
.

Lemma II.5.22
.

If the real closed
H
-asymptotic extension
F
of
H
has asymptotic integration
and convex valuation ring, then
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
for the algebraically closed

H
-asymptotic field extension
L:=F[\imag]
of
K
.

Proof.

Use Corollary
I.2.15
and earlier remarks in the same subsection.
∎

Corollary II.5.23
.

The
H
-field
H
has an
H
-closed extension
F
with
C_{F}=C_{H}
, and for any such
F
, the algebraically closed

\operatorname{d}
-valued field extension
L:=F[\imag]
of
H
-type of
K
is
\upomega
-free with
C_{L}=C
,
\operatorname{I}(L)\subseteq L^{\dagger}
, and

L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Use [ADH, 16.4.1, 9.1.2] to extend
H
to an
\upomega
-free
H
-field with the same constant field as
H
, next use [ADH, 11.7.23] to pass to its real closure, and then use [ADH, 14.5.9] to extend further to an
H
-closed
F
, still with the same constant field as
H
.
For any such
F
, the
\operatorname{d}
-valued field

L:=F[\imag]
of
H
-type is
\upomega
-free by [ADH, 11.7.23] and newtonian by (
0.12
). Hence
\operatorname{I}(L)\subseteq L^{\dagger}

by Lemma
I.2.9
,
and
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}

by Lemma
II.5.22
.
∎

This leads to a variant of Proposition
II.5.15
:

Proposition II.5.24
.

The conclusion of Proposition
II.5.15
holds. In particular:

\dim_{C}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=r\ \Longrightarrow\ A\text{ is terminal}.

Proof.

Corollary
II.5.23
gives an
H
-closed extension
F
of
H
with
C_{F}=C_{H}
, so
L:=F[\imag]
is
\upomega
-free,

C_{L}=C
,
\operatorname{I}(L)\subseteq L^{\dagger}
, and
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
. Take a complement
\Lambda_{L}\supseteq\Lambda
of the subspace
L^{\dagger}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
L
.
By Remark
II.5.9
we have

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})\cap\Gamma
. Hence
Proposition
II.5.15
applied to
K
,
\Lambda
replaced by
L
,
\Lambda_{L}
, respectively, and
A
viewed
as element of
L[\der]
, yields

\sum_{\lambda}\,\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert\leqslant r
.
Corollary
I.7.10
applied to
A_{\lambda}
in place of
A
gives
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert=\sum_{\gamma}\operatorname{nwt}_{A_{\lambda}}(\gamma)
.
This yields the conclusion of Proposition
II.5.15
as in the proof of that proposition.
∎

Let now
F
be a Liouville closed
H
-field extension of
H
and suppose

\operatorname{I}(L)\subseteq L^{\dagger}
where
L:=F[\imag]
.
Lemma
II.5.22
yields
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
, so
L
is as described just before Lemma
II.5.16
, and we have
a complement
\Lambda_{L}\supseteq\Lambda
of the subspace
L^{\dagger}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
L
.
Note that if
A
splits over
K
, then
A
is terminal by Corollary
II.5.19
.

Corollary II.5.25
.

Suppose
A
is terminal. Then, with respect to the complement
\Lambda_{L}
of
L^{\dagger}
in
L
, the conclusions

(i)–(iv)
of Corollary
II.5.21
hold.

Proof.

By the remarks after Corollary
II.5.7
we have
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})
for all
\lambda
,
and so with
\mu
ranging over
\Lambda_{L}
, Proposition
II.5.24
applied to
L
in place of
K
,
we have
r=\sum_{\lambda}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})\rvert\leqslant\sum_{\mu}\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\mu})\rvert\leqslant r
. This yields (i)–(iv).
∎

Part III
Normalizing Holes and Slots

In this introduction
K
is an
H
-asymptotic field with small derivation and rational asymptotic integration.
In Section
III.2
we introduce
holes
in
K
: A
hole in
K
is a triple
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
with
P\in K\{Y\}\setminus K
,
{\mathfrak{m}}\in K^{\times}
, and
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
for some immediate asymptotic extension

\widehat{K}
of
K
, such that
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
and
P(\widehat{a})=0
.
The main goal of Part
III
is a normalization theorem, namely Theorem
III.3.33
, that allows us to transform
under reasonable conditions a hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
into a “normal” hole; this helps to pin down the location of

\widehat{a}
relative to
K
.
The notion of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
being
normal
involves the linear part of
the differential polynomial
P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
, in particular the
span
of this linear part. We introduce the span in the preliminary Section
III.1
.
In Section
III.4
we study
isolated
holes
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
, which under reasonable conditions ensure the uniqueness of the
isomorphism type of
K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle
as a
valued differential field over
K
; see Proposition
III.4.9
. In Section
III.5
we focus on holes
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
where
\operatorname{order}P=\deg P=1
.
For technical reasons we actually work in Part
III
also with
slots
in
K
, which are a bit more general than holes in
K
.

First some notational conventions. Let
\Gamma
be an ordered abelian group. For
\gamma,\delta\in\Gamma
with

\gamma\neq 0
the expression “
\delta=o(\gamma)
” means “
n|\delta|<|\gamma|
for all
n\geqslant 1
” according to [ADH, 2.4], but here we find it convenient to extend this to
\gamma=0
, in which case “
\delta=o(\gamma)
” means “
\delta=0
”.
Suppose
\Gamma=v(E^{\times})
is the value group
of a valued field
E
and
{\mathfrak{m}}\in E^{\times}
. Then we denote the archimedean class

[v{\mathfrak{m}}]\subseteq\Gamma
of
v{\mathfrak{m}}\in\Gamma
by just
[{\mathfrak{m}}]
. Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}\not\asymp 1
. Then we have a
proper convex subgroup

\Delta({\mathfrak{m}})\ :=\ \big\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\,\gamma=o(v{\mathfrak{m}})\big\}\ =\ \big\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\,[\gamma]<[{\mathfrak{m}}]\big\},

of
\Gamma
.
If
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{m}})}{\mathfrak{n}}\in E
, then
0\neq{\mathfrak{n}}\not\asymp 1
and
\Delta({\mathfrak{m}})=\Delta({\mathfrak{n}})
.
In particular, if
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp{\mathfrak{n}}\in E
, then
0\neq{\mathfrak{n}}\not\asymp 1
and
\Delta({\mathfrak{m}})=\Delta({\mathfrak{n}})
. Note that for
f,g\in E
the meaning of “
f\preccurlyeq_{\Delta({\mathfrak{m}})}g
” does not change in passing to a valued field extension of
E
, although
\Delta({\mathfrak{m}})
can increase as a subgroup of the value group of the extension.

III.1.
The Span of a Linear Differential Operator

In this section
K
is a valued differential field with small derivation and
\Gamma:=v(K^{\times})
. We let
a
,
b
, sometimes subscripted, range over
K
, and
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K^{\times}
.
Consider a linear differential operator

A\ =\ a_{0}+a_{1}\der+\cdots+a_{r}\der^{r}\in K[\der],\qquad a_{r}\neq 0.

We shall use below the quantities
\operatorname{dwm}(A)
and
\operatorname{dwt}(A)
defined in [ADH, 5.6]. We also introduce a measure
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
for the “lopsidedness” of
A
as follows:

{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\ :=\ a_{r}/a_{m}\in\ K^{\times}\qquad\text{where $m:=\operatorname{dwt}(A)$.}

So
a_{r}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\preccurlyeq 1
, with

{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\asymp 1\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\operatorname{dwt}(A)=r\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\mathfrak{v}}(A)=1.

Also note that
{\mathfrak{v}}(aA)={\mathfrak{v}}(A)
for
a\neq 0
. Moreover,

{\mathfrak{v}}(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}\ \asymp\ a_{r}\ \asymp\ {\mathfrak{v}}(A)A

since
A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}=a_{r}\der^{r}+\text{lower order terms in $\der$}
.

Example
.

{\mathfrak{v}}(a+\der)=1
if
a\preccurlyeq 1
, and
{\mathfrak{v}}(a+\der)=1/a
if
a\succ 1
.

We call
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
the
span
of
A
.
We are mainly interested in the valuation of
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
.
This is related to the gaussian valuation
v(A)
of
A
: if
A
is monic, then
v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big)=-v(A)
.
An important property of the span of
A
is that its valuation is not affected by small additive perturbations of
A
:

Lemma III.1.1
.

Suppose
B\in K[\der]
,
\operatorname{order}(B)\leqslant r
and
B\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A
. Then:

(i)

A+B\sim A
,
\operatorname{dwm}(A+B)=\operatorname{dwm}(A)
, and
\operatorname{dwt}(A+B)=\operatorname{dwt}(A)
;

(ii)

\operatorname{order}(A+B)=r
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(A+B)\sim{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
.

Proof.

From
B\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\preccurlyeq 1
we obtain
B\prec A
, and thus (i).
Set
m:=\operatorname{dwt}(A)
, let
i
range over
\{0,\dots,r\}
, and let
B=b_{0}+b_{1}\der+\cdots+b_{r}\der^{r}
.
Then
a_{i}\preccurlyeq a_{m}
and
b_{i}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A\asymp a_{r}\preccurlyeq a_{m}
.
Therefore, if
a_{i}\asymp a_{m}
, then
a_{i}+b_{i}\sim a_{i}
, and if
a_{i}\prec a_{m}
, then
a_{i}+b_{i}\prec a_{m}
. Hence

{\mathfrak{v}}(A+B)=(a_{r}+b_{r})/(a_{m}+b_{m})\sim a_{r}/a_{m}={\mathfrak{v}}(A)
.
∎

For
b\neq 0
, the valuation of
{\mathfrak{v}}(Ab)
only depends on
vb
; it is enough to check this for
b\asymp 1
. More generally:

Lemma III.1.2
.

Let
B\in K[\der]^{\neq}
and
b\asymp B
. Then
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}(Ab){\mathfrak{v}}(B)
.

Proof.

Let
B=b_{0}+b_{1}\der+\cdots+b_{s}\der^{s}
,
b_{s}\neq 0
. Then

AB\ =\ a_{r}b_{s}\der^{r+s}+\text{lower order terms in}\ \der,

so by [ADH, 5.6.1(ii)] for
\gamma=0
:

\displaystyle v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\big)\

\displaystyle=\ v(a_{r}b_{s})-v(AB)\ =\ v(a_{r}b_{s})-v(Ab)

\displaystyle=\ v(a_{r}b)-v(Ab)+v(b_{s})-v(B)

\displaystyle=\ v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(Ab){\mathfrak{v}}(B)\big).\qed

Corollary III.1.3
.

Let
B\in K[\der]^{\neq}
. If
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)=1
, then
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)={\mathfrak{v}}(B)=1
. The converse holds if
B
is monic.

This is clear from from Lemma
III.1.2
, and in turn gives:

Corollary III.1.4
.

Suppose
A=a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r})
. Then

{\mathfrak{v}}(A)=1\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\preccurlyeq 1.

Remark
.

Suppose
K=C(\!(t)\!)
with the
t
-adic valuation and derivation
\der=t\frac{d}{dt}
. In the literature,
A
is called
regular singular
if
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)=1
, and
irregular singular
if
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1
; see
[
20
, Definition 3.14]
.

Lemma III.1.5
.

Let
B\in K[\der]^{\neq}
. Then
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(B)
, and if
B
is monic, then
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
.

Proof.

Lemma
III.1.2
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(Ab)\preccurlyeq 1
for
b\neq 0
yields
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(B)
.
Suppose
B
is monic, so
v(B)\leqslant 0
. To show
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
we arrange that
A

is also monic. Then
AB
is monic, and
{\mathfrak{v}}(AB)\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
is equivalent to
v(AB)\leqslant v(A)
. Now

v(AB)\ =\ v_{AB}(0)\ =\ v_{A}\big(v_{B}(0)\big)\ =\ v_{A}\big(v(B)\big)\ \leqslant\ v_{A}(0)\ =\ v(A)

by [ADH, 4.5.1(iii), 5.6.1(ii)].
∎

Corollary III.1.6
.

If
A=a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r})
, then

b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\ \preccurlyeq\ {\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-1}.

Let
\Delta
be a convex subgroup of
\Gamma
, let
\dot{\mathcal{O}}
be the valuation ring of the coarsening
v_{\Delta}
of the valuation
v
of
K
by
\Delta
, with maximal ideal
\dot{\smallo}
,
and
\dot{K}=\dot{\mathcal{O}}/\dot{\smallo}
be the
valued differential residue field of
v_{\Delta}
.
The residue morphism
\dot{\mathcal{O}}\to\dot{K}
extends to the ring morphism
\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]\to\dot{K}[\der]
with
\der\mapsto\der
. If
A\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]
and
\dot{A}\neq 0
,
then
\operatorname{dwm}(\dot{A})=\operatorname{dwm}(A)
and
\operatorname{dwt}(\dot{A})=\operatorname{dwt}(A)
.
We set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)
.

Lemma III.1.7
.

If
A\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]
and
\operatorname{order}(\dot{A})=r
, then
{\mathfrak{v}}(\dot{A})=\dot{\mathfrak{v}}
.

Behavior of the span under twisting

Recall that
o(\gamma):=0\in\Gamma
for
\gamma=0\in\Gamma
.
With this convention, here is a consequence of [ADH, 6.1.3]:

Lemma III.1.8
.

Let
B\in K[\der]^{\neq}
. Then
v(AB)=v(A)+v(B)+o\big(v(B)\big)
.

Proof.

Take
b
with
b\asymp B
. Then

v(AB)=v_{AB}(0)=v_{A}\big(v_{B}(0)\big)=v_{A}(vb)=v(Ab)

by [ADH, 5.6.1(ii)]. Moreover,
v(Ab)=v(A)+vb+o(vb)
, by [ADH, 6.1.3].
∎

We have
{\mathfrak{v}}(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})={\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}})
, so
v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})=v(A)+o(v{\mathfrak{n}})

by Lemma
III.1.8
. Moreover:

Lemma III.1.9
.

v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}})\big)=v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big)+o(v{\mathfrak{n}})
.

Proof.

Replacing
A
by
a_{r}^{-1}A
we arrange
A
is monic, so
A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}
is monic, and thus

v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}})\big)\ =\ v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})\big)=-v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})=-v(A)+o(v{\mathfrak{n}})=v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big)+o(v{\mathfrak{n}})

by remarks preceding the lemma.
∎

Recall: we denote the archimedean class

[v{\mathfrak{n}}]\subseteq\Gamma
by
[{\mathfrak{n}}]
. Lemma
III.1.9
yields:

Corollary III.1.10
.

\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \big[{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}})\big]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]
.

Under suitable conditions on
K
we can say more about the valuation of

{\mathfrak{v}}(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})
: Lemma
III.1.12
below.

Lemma III.1.11
.

Let
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{0},\dots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{r}\in K^{\times}
be such that

v({\mathfrak{m}}_{i})+v(A)\ =\ \min_{i\leqslant j\leqslant r}v(a_{j})+(j-i)v({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}).

Then with
m:=\operatorname{dwt}(A)
we have

{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\ \succcurlyeq\ \cdots\ \succcurlyeq\ {\mathfrak{m}}_{r}\quad\text{and}\quad({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{m}\ \preccurlyeq\ {\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\ \preccurlyeq\ ({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{r}.

(
In particular,
[{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]
, with equality if
m>0
.
)

Proof.

From
v({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\leqslant 0
we obtain
v({\mathfrak{m}}_{0})\leqslant\cdots\leqslant v({\mathfrak{m}}_{r})
.
We have
0\leqslant v(a_{j}/a_{m})
for
j=0,\dots,r
and so

rv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\ \leqslant\ \min_{0\leqslant j\leqslant r}v(a_{j}/a_{m})+jv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\ =\ v({\mathfrak{m}}_{0})\ \leqslant\ mv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})

as required.
∎

Lemma III.1.12
.

Suppose
\der\mathcal{O}\subseteq\smallo
. Then

{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq 1\ \Longrightarrow\ v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})=v(A),\qquad{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succ 1\ \Longrightarrow\ \lvert v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})-v(A)\rvert\leqslant-rv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}).

Proof.

Let
R:=\operatorname{Ri}A
. Then
v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})=v(R_{+{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}})
by [ADH, 5.8.11].
If
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq 1
, then
v(R_{+{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}})=v(R)

by [ADH, 4.5.1(i)], hence
v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})=v(R)=v(A)
by [ADH, 5.8.10].
Now suppose
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succ 1
.
Claim
:
v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})-v(A)\geqslant rv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
.
To prove this claim we
replace
A
by
a^{-1}A
, where
a\asymp A
, to arrange
A\asymp 1
.
Let
i
,
j
range over
\{0,\dots,r\}
. We have

R_{+{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}}=\sum_{i}b_{i}R_{i}
where

b_{i}\ =\ \sum_{j\geqslant i}{j\choose i}a_{j}R_{j-i}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}).

Take
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}\in K^{\times}
as in Lemma
III.1.11
.
By Lemma
I.1.17
we have
R_{n}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\sim({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{n}
for all
n
;
hence
v(b_{i})\geqslant v({\mathfrak{m}}_{i})
for all
i
. Thus

v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})-v(A)\ =\ v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})\ =\ v(R_{+{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}})\ \geqslant\ \min_{i}v(b_{i})\ \geqslant\ v({\mathfrak{m}}_{0})\ \geqslant\ rv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})

by Lemma
III.1.11
, proving
our claim.
Applying this claim with
A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}
in place of
A
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
also yields

v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})-v(A)\leqslant-rv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
, thus
\lvert v(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})-v(A)\rvert\leqslant-rv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
.
∎

Remark
.

Suppose that
\der\mathcal{O}\subseteq\smallo
and
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succ 1
. Then Lemma
III.1.12
improves on Lemma
III.1.9
, since
v({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})=o(v{\mathfrak{n}})
by [ADH, 6.4.1(iii)].

Lemma III.1.13
.

Suppose
\der\mathcal{O}\subseteq\smallo
and
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-1}
.
Let
B\in K[\der]
and
s\in{\mathbb{N}}
be such that
\operatorname{order}(B)\leqslant s
and
B\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{s+1}A
. Then
B_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})A_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}
.

Proof.

We may assume
B\neq 0
and
s=\operatorname{order}(B)
. It suffices to show
B_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A
.
If
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq 1
, then Lemma
III.1.12
applied to
B
in place of
A
yields

B_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}\asymp B\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A
.
Suppose
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succ 1
. Then Lemma
III.1.12
gives

\lvert v(B_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}})-v(B)\rvert\leqslant-sv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\leqslant sv({\mathfrak{v}}(A))
and hence

B_{\ltimes{\mathfrak{n}}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-s}B\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)A
.
∎

If
\der\mathcal{O}\subseteq\smallo
, then we have functions
\operatorname{dwm}_{A},\operatorname{dwt}_{A}\colon\Gamma\to{\mathbb{N}}
as defined in [ADH, 5.6].
Combining Lemmas
III.1.1
and
III.1.13
yields a variant of [ADH, 6.1.7]:

Corollary III.1.14
.

Suppose
\der\mathcal{O}\subseteq\smallo
and
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-1}
.
Let
B\in K[\der]
be such that
\operatorname{order}(B)\leqslant r
and
B\prec{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{r+1}A
.
Then
\operatorname{dwm}_{A+B}(v{\mathfrak{n}})=\operatorname{dwm}_{A}(v{\mathfrak{n}})
and
\operatorname{dwt}_{A+B}(v{\mathfrak{n}})=\operatorname{dwt}_{A}(v{\mathfrak{n}})
. In particular,

v{\mathfrak{n}}\in{\mathscr{E}}(A+B)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ v{\mathfrak{n}}\in{\mathscr{E}}(A).

About
A({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})
and
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}^{l}=\pm{\mathfrak{n}}^{k}
where
k,l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
l\neq 0
. Then

{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}=q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
with
q=k/l\in{\mathbb{Q}}
. In particular, if
K
is real closed or algebraically closed, then for any
{\mathfrak{n}}
and
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
we have
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}=q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
for some
{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Below in this subsection
K
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued and
{\mathfrak{n}}
is such that for all

q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
we are given an element of
K^{\times}
, denoted by
{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
for suggestiveness, with
({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})^{\dagger}=q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
; then
v({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})=qv({\mathfrak{n}})
: to see this we may arrange that
K
is algebraically closed
by [ADH, 10.1.23], and hence contains an
{\mathfrak{m}}
such that
v{\mathfrak{m}}=q\,v{\mathfrak{n}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}=q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}=({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})^{\dagger}
, and thus
v({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})=v{\mathfrak{m}}=q\,v{\mathfrak{n}}
.

Lemma III.1.15
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
. Then for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
,

v\big(A({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\big)\ =\ v({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})+\min_{j}v(a_{j})+jv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}).

Proof.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
and take
b_{0},\dots,b_{r}\in K
with
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}=b_{0}+b_{1}\der+\cdots+b_{r}\der^{r}
.
Then

b_{0}\ =\ A({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\ =\ {\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\big(a_{0}R_{0}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})+a_{1}R_{1}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})+\cdots+a_{r}R_{r}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\big).

Let
i
,
j
range over
\{0,\dots,r\}
.
By Lemma
I.1.17
,
R_{i}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\sim q^{i}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{i}
for all
i
. Take
{\mathfrak{m}}
(independent of
q
) such that

v({\mathfrak{m}})=\min_{j}v(a_{j})+jv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
, and let
I
be the nonempty set of

i
with
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp a_{i}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{i}
. For
i\in I
we take
c_{i}\in C^{\times}
such that
a_{i}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{i}\sim c_{i}{\mathfrak{m}}
, and set
R:=\sum_{i\in I}c_{i}Y^{i}\in C[Y]^{\neq}
.
Therefore, if
R(q)\neq 0
, then

\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}R_{i}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{m}}R(q).

Assume
R(q)\neq 0
in what follows. Then

\sum_{i=0}^{r}a_{i}R_{i}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\ \sim\ \sum_{i\in I}a_{i}R_{i}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{m}}R(q)\ \asymp\ {\mathfrak{m}},

hence
b_{0}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
, in particular,
b_{0}\neq 0
.
∎

Lemma III.1.16
.

Assume
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
and
[{\mathfrak{v}}]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]
for

{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)
. Then

\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\big]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]
for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
, and for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}

we have

{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}
, and thus
[{\mathfrak{v}}]\leqslant\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\big]
.

Proof.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
. Then
[{\mathfrak{v}}]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]=[{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}]
, so
[{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})]<[{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}]=[{\mathfrak{n}}]
by Corollary
III.1.10
.
To show the second part, let
m=\operatorname{dwt}(A)
.
Replacing
A
by
a_{m}^{-1}A
we arrange
a_{m}=1
, so
a_{r}={\mathfrak{v}}
,
A\asymp 1
.
Take
b_{0},\dots,b_{r}
with
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}=b_{0}+b_{1}\der+\cdots+b_{r}\der^{r}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
III.1.15
we obtain an
{\mathfrak{m}}
and a polynomial
R(Y)\in C[Y]^{\neq}
(both independent of
q
)
such that
v({\mathfrak{m}})=\min_{j}v(a_{j})+jv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
, and
b_{0}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
if
R(q)\neq 0
.
Assume
R(q)\neq 0
in what follows; we show that then
{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}
. For
n:=\operatorname{dwt}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})
,

b_{0}{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\ \preccurlyeq\ b_{n}{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\ =\ b_{r}\ =\ {\mathfrak{n}}^{q}{\mathfrak{v}},

hence

{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}/{\mathfrak{m}}
.
It remains to note that

{\mathfrak{m}}\succcurlyeq a_{m}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{m}=({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{m}\succcurlyeq 1
.
∎

Lemma III.1.17
.

Assume
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
and
{\mathfrak{m}}
satisfies

v{\mathfrak{m}}+v(A)\ =\ \min_{0\leqslant j\leqslant r}v(a_{j})+jv({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}).

Then
[{\mathfrak{m}}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]
, with equality if
\operatorname{dwt}(A)>0
, and for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
,

A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\ \asymp\ {\mathfrak{m}}\,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\,A,\qquad{\mathfrak{v}}(A)/{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\ \asymp\ {\mathfrak{m}}.

Proof.

Replacing
A
by
a_{m}^{-1}A
where
m=\operatorname{dwt}(A)
we arrange
a_{m}=1
, so
a_{r}={\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)
and
A\asymp 1
.
Let
i
,
j
range over
\{0,\dots,r\}
. Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
, and
take
b_{i}\in K
such that
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}=\sum_{i}b_{i}\der^{i}
.
By [ADH, (5.1.3)] we have

b_{i}\ =\ \frac{1}{i!}A^{(i)}({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\ =\ {\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\frac{1}{i!}\operatorname{Ri}(A^{(i)})(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\ =\ {\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\sum_{j\geqslant i}{j\choose i}a_{j}R_{j-i}(q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}).

Take
{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}\in K^{\times}
as in Lemma
III.1.11
.
Then
{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}
(so
[{\mathfrak{m}}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]
, with equality if
m>0
), and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{r}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}
.
Lemma
III.1.15
applied to
A^{(i)}/i!
instead of
A
gives that for all but finitely
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
we have

b_{i}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}_{i}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
for all
i
. Assume that
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
has this property.
From
v({\mathfrak{m}})=v({\mathfrak{m}}_{0})\leqslant\cdots\leqslant v({\mathfrak{m}}_{r})=v({\mathfrak{v}})
we obtain

v({\mathfrak{m}})+qv({\mathfrak{n}})\ =\ v(b_{0})\ \leqslant\ \cdots\ \leqslant\ v(b_{r})\ =\ v({\mathfrak{v}})+q\,v({\mathfrak{n}}).

With
n=\operatorname{dwt}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})
this gives
v(b_{0})=\cdots=v(b_{n})=v(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})
. Thus

{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\ =\ b_{r}/b_{n}\ \asymp\ b_{r}/b_{0}\ \asymp\ ({\mathfrak{n}}^{q}{\mathfrak{v}})/({\mathfrak{n}}^{q}{\mathfrak{m}})\ =\ {\mathfrak{v}}/{\mathfrak{m}}

as claimed.
∎

The next lemma (not used later) is a more precise version of Lemma
III.1.17
, but
with an additional hypothesis on
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
:

Lemma III.1.18
.

Assume
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succ{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-1}
. Then

A({\mathfrak{n}})\sim A{\mathfrak{n}}\sim a_{r}{\mathfrak{n}}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{r}\sim a_{r}{\mathfrak{n}}^{(r)},\qquad{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}})\sim({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{-r}.

Proof.

Let
i
,
j
range over
\{0,\dots,r\}
and take
b_{i}\in K

such that
A{\mathfrak{n}}=\sum_{i}b_{i}\der^{i}
, so
b_{i}={\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{j\geqslant i}{j\choose i}a_{j}R_{j-i}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
.
By Lemma
I.1.16
we have
R_{j-i}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})\sim({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{j-i}
for
{i\leqslant j}
.
From
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succ{\mathfrak{v}}^{-1}\succcurlyeq 1
we get for
i\leqslant j<r
:

a_{r}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{r-i}\ \succcurlyeq\ a_{r}{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{j-i}\ \succ\ a_{r}{\mathfrak{v}}^{-1}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{j-i}\ \succcurlyeq\ a_{j}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{j-i}.

Therefore
b_{i}\sim{\mathfrak{n}}{r\choose i}a_{r}({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{r-i}
, from which the first displayed equivalences follow.
Now
\operatorname{dwt}(A{\mathfrak{n}})=0
and so
{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}})=b_{r}/b_{0}=({\mathfrak{n}}a_{r})/A({\mathfrak{n}})\sim({\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})^{-r}
as claimed.
∎

Let
{\mathfrak{v}}\in K^{\times}
with
{\mathfrak{v}}\not\asymp 1
; so we have the proper convex subgroup of
\Gamma
given by

\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})\ =\ \big\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\,\gamma=o(v{\mathfrak{v}})\big\}\ =\ \big\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\,[\gamma]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]\big\}.

If
K
is
H
-asymptotic, then we also have the convex subgroup

\Delta\ =\ \big\{\gamma\in\Gamma:\,\gamma^{\dagger}>v({\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger})\big\}

of
\Gamma
with
\Delta\subseteq\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})
. If
K
is
H
-asymptotic of Hardy type (Section
I.2
), then we have
{\Delta=\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}
,
and hence the relations
\preccurlyeq_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}
,
\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}
,
\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}
agree with

\preccurlyeq_{\mathfrak{v}}
,
\prec_{{\mathfrak{v}}}
,
\asymp_{{\mathfrak{v}}}
, respectively, from [ADH, p. 407].

Corollary III.1.19
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
and

[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]
where
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)

(
so
0\neq{\mathfrak{v}}\prec 1)
.
Let
B\in K[\der]
and
w\geqslant r
be such that

B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w}A
.
Then for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
we have
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
and

B{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w}A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
.

Proof.

The case
B=0
is trivial, so
assume
B\neq 0
. Take
{\mathfrak{m}}
as in Lemma
III.1.17
, and take
{\mathfrak{m}}_{B}
likewise with
B
in place of
A
. By this lemma,
[{\mathfrak{m}}],[{\mathfrak{m}}_{B}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]
,
hence
{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{B}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}1
.
Moreover, for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
we have
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}A
,

B{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}_{B}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}B
, and
{\mathfrak{v}}/{\mathfrak{w}}\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}
where
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})
; assume that
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
has these properties. Then

B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w}A
yields

B{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\ \asymp\ {\mathfrak{m}}_{B}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}B\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w}A\ \asymp\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{w}A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}.

Now
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}1
gives
{\mathfrak{v}}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{w}}
, hence

B{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w}A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
.
∎

The behavior of the span under compositional conjugation

If
K
is
H
-asymptotic with asymptotic integration, then
\Psi\cap\Gamma^{>}\neq\emptyset
, but it is convenient not to require “asymptotic integration” in some lemmas below. Instead:
In this subsection
K
is
H
-asymptotic and ungrounded with
\Psi\cap\Gamma^{>}\neq\emptyset
.

We let
\phi
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
range over
K^{\times}
. We say that

\phi
is
active
if
\phi
is active in
K
.
Recall from [ADH, pp. 290–292] that
\derdelta
denotes the derivation
\phi^{-1}\der
of
K^{\phi}
, and that

(III.1.1)

A^{\phi}\ =\ a_{r}\phi^{r}\derdelta^{r}+\text{lower order terms in $\derdelta$.}

Lemma III.1.20
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
and
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
is active. Then

A\ \asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ A^{\phi},\qquad{\mathfrak{v}}\ \asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\ \prec^{\flat}\ 1,\qquad{\mathfrak{v}},{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\ \prec_{\phi}^{\flat}\ 1.

Proof.

From
\phi^{\dagger}\prec 1\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}
we get
[\phi]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]
, so
\phi\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}1
. Hence
A^{\phi}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}A

by [ADH, 11.1.4]. For the rest we can arrange
A\asymp 1
, so
A^{\phi}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}1
and
{\mathfrak{v}}\asymp a_{r}
. In view of
(
III.1.1
)
this yields
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}a_{r}\phi^{r}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
.
So
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})^{\dagger}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
, which gives
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\prec^{\flat}1
, and also
{\mathfrak{v}},{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\prec_{\phi}^{\flat}1
.
∎

Lemma III.1.21
.

If
\operatorname{nwt}(A)=r
, then
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})=1
eventually, and if
\operatorname{nwt}(A)<r
, then
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\prec_{\phi}^{\flat}1
eventually.

Proof.

Clearly, if
\operatorname{nwt}(A)=r
, then
\operatorname{dwt}(A^{\phi})=r
and so
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})=1
eventually.
Suppose
\operatorname{nwt}(A)<r
. To show that
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\prec_{\phi}^{\flat}1
eventually, we may replace
A
by
A^{\phi_{0}}
for
suitable active
\phi_{0}
and assume that
n:=\operatorname{nwt}(A)=\operatorname{dwt}(A^{\phi})=\operatorname{dwm}(A^{\phi})
for all active
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
.
Thus
v(A^{\phi})=v(A)+nv\phi
for all active
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
by [ADH, 11.1.11(i)]. Using (
III.1.1
)
we therefore obtain for active
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
:

{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\ \asymp\ a_{r}\phi^{r}/a_{n}\phi^{n}\ =\ {\mathfrak{v}}(A)\phi^{r-n}\ \preccurlyeq\ \phi^{r-n}\ \preccurlyeq\ \phi.

Take
x\in K^{\times}
with
x\not\asymp 1
and
x^{\prime}\asymp 1
; then
x\succ 1
, so
x^{-1}\asymp x^{\dagger}\prec 1
is active. Hence for active
\phi\preccurlyeq x^{-1}
we have
\phi\prec^{\flat}_{\phi}1
and thus
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})\prec^{\flat}_{\phi}1
.
∎

Corollary III.1.22
.

The following conditions on
K
are equivalent:

(i)

K
is
\uplambda
-free;

(ii)

\operatorname{nwt}(B)\leqslant 1
for all
B\in K[\der]

(
so
{\mathfrak{v}}(B^{\phi})\prec_{\phi}^{\flat}1
eventually
)
;

(iii)

\operatorname{nwt}(B)\leqslant 1
for all
B\in K[\der]
of order
2
.

Proof.

The implication (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) follows from [ADH, 13.7.10] and Lemma
III.1.21
, and
(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is clear. Suppose
K
is not
\uplambda
-free. Take
\uplambda\in K
such that
\phi^{\dagger}+\uplambda\prec\phi
for all active
\phi
([ADH, 11.6.1]);
set
B:=(\der+\uplambda)\der=\der^{2}+\uplambda\der
. Then for active
\phi
we have

B^{\phi}=\phi^{2}\big(\derdelta^{2}+(\phi^{\dagger}+\uplambda)\phi^{-1}\derdelta\big)
, so

\operatorname{dwt}(B^{\phi})=2
. Thus (iii)
\Rightarrow
(i).
∎

Lemma
III.1.20
leads to an “eventual” version of Corollary
III.1.14
:

Lemma III.1.23
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free and
B\in K[\der]
is such that
\operatorname{order}(B)\leqslant r
and
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{r+1}A
, where
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
. Then

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)
.

Proof.

By [ADH, 10.1.3, 11.7.18] and Corollary
I.7.10
we can pass to an extension to arrange that
K
is

\upomega
-free. Next, by [ADH, 11.7.23] and (
0.9
) we extend further to arrange that
K
is algebraically closed and newtonian, and thus
\operatorname{d}
-valued by Lemma
I.2.9
. Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)=v(\ker^{\neq}A)
by Proposition
I.4.2
, and
A
splits over
K
by (
0.10
). It remains to show that
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A+B)
: the reverse inclusion then follows by
interchanging
A
and
A+B
, using
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\sim{\mathfrak{v}}(A+B)
.
Let
{\gamma\in{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)}
. Take
{\mathfrak{n}}\in\ker^{\neq}A
with
v{\mathfrak{n}}=\gamma
. Then
A\in K[\der](\der-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})

by [ADH, 5.1.21] and so
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{-1}
,
by [ADH, 5.1.22] and Corollary
III.1.6
. Now

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}(A)
, so
{\gamma=v{\mathfrak{n}}\in{\mathscr{E}}(A+B)}
by Corollary
III.1.14
. Let
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
be active; it remains to show that then
\gamma\in{\mathscr{E}}\big((A+B)^{\phi}\big)
. By Lemma
III.1.20
,
A^{\phi}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}A
;
also
B^{\phi}\preccurlyeq B
by [ADH, 11.1.4].
Lemma
III.1.20
gives
{\mathfrak{v}}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})
, hence

B^{\phi}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})^{r+1}A^{\phi}
. Thus with
K^{\phi}
,
A^{\phi}
,
B^{\phi}
in the role of
K
,
A
,
B
, the above argument leading to
\gamma\in{\mathscr{E}}(A+B)
gives
\gamma\in{\mathscr{E}}(A^{\phi}+B^{\phi})={\mathscr{E}}\big((A+B)^{\phi}\big)
.
∎

For
r=1
we can weaken the hypothesis of
\uplambda
-freeness:

Corollary III.1.24
.

Suppose
K
has asymptotic integration,
r=1
, and
B\in K[\der]
of order
\leqslant 1
satisfies
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{2}A
, where
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
. Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
I.2.10
we replace
K
by an immediate extension to arrange
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)=v(\ker^{\neq}A)
by Lemma
I.4.9
. Now argue as in the proof of Lemma
III.1.23
.
∎

In the next proposition and its corollary
K
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued with algebraically closed constant field
C
and divisible group
K^{\dagger}
of logarithmic derivatives
.
We choose a complement
\Lambda
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear subspace
K^{\dagger}
of
K
. Then we have the set
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
of ultimate exceptional values of
A
with respect to
\Lambda
. The following stability result will be crucial in Section
IV.4
:

Proposition III.1.25
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, and

B\in K[\der]
of order
\leqslant r
satisfies
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{r+1}A
, where
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.

Proof.

Let
\Omega
be the differential fraction field of the universal exponential extension
{\operatorname{U}}=K\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big]

of
K
from Section
II.2
.
Equip
\Omega
with a spectral extension of the valuation of
K
;
see Section
II.5
. Apply Lemma
III.1.23

to
\Omega
in place of
K
to get
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
.
Hence
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
by (
II.5.3
).
∎

In a similar manner we obtain an analogue of Corollary
III.1.24
:

Corollary III.1.26
.

Suppose
K
has asymptotic integration,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
,
r=1
, and
B\in K[\der]
satisfies
\operatorname{order}(B)\leqslant 1
and
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{2}A
, where
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.

Proof.

Let
\Omega
be as in the proof of Proposition
III.1.25
.
Then
\Omega
is ungrounded by Lemma
II.5.3
, hence
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)\rvert\leqslant 1
and
v(\ker^{\neq}_{\Omega}A)\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
by [ADH, p. 481]. But
\dim_{C}\ker_{\Omega}A=1
, so

v(\ker_{\Omega}^{\neq}A)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
. The proof of Lemma
III.1.23
with
\Omega
in place of
K
now gives
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
, so
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
by (
II.5.3
).
∎

In the “real” case we have the following variant of Proposition
III.1.25
:

Proposition III.1.27
.

Suppose
K=H[\imag]
,
\imag^{2}=-1
, where
H
is a real closed
H
-field with asymptotic integration such that

H^{\dagger}=H
and
\operatorname{I}(H)\imag\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Let
B\in K[\der]
of order
\leqslant r
be such that
{B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{r+1}A}
with
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
. Let
\Lambda
be a complement of
the subspace
K^{\dagger}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
K
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
, where the ultimate exceptional values are with respect to
\Lambda
.

Proof.

Take an
H
-closed extension
F
of
H
with
C_{F}=C_{H}
as in Corollary
II.5.23
. Then the algebraically closed
\operatorname{d}
-valued
H
-asymptotic extension
L:=F[\imag]
of
K
is
\upomega
-free,
C_{L}=C
,
\operatorname{I}(L)\subseteq L^{\dagger}
, and

L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
.
Take a complement
\Lambda_{L}\supseteq\Lambda
of the subspace
L^{\dagger}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
L
. Let
{\operatorname{U}}_{L}=L\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda_{L})\big]
be
the universal exponential extension
of
L
from Section
II.2
; it has the universal exponential extension
{\operatorname{U}}:=K\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big]
of
K
as a differential subring.
Let
\Omega
,
\Omega_{L}
be the
differential fraction fields of
{\operatorname{U}}
,
{\operatorname{U}}_{L}
, respectively, and
equip
\Omega_{L}
with a spectral extension of the valuation of
L
; then the restriction of this valuation to

\Omega
is a spectral extension of the valuation of
K
(see remarks preceding Lemma
II.5.16
).
Lemma
III.1.23
applied
to
\Omega_{L}
in place of
K
yields
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega_{L}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega_{L}}(A)
,
hence
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{\Omega}(A)
by
Lemma
II.5.16
and thus
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
.
∎

The span of the linear part of a differential polynomial

In this subsection
P\in K\{Y\}^{\neq}
has order
r
. Recall that the
linear part
of
P
is the
differential operator

L_{P}\ :=\ \sum_{n}\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(0)\,\der^{n}\in K[\der]

of order
\leqslant r
.
We have
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}=L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}}
[ADH, p. 242]; hence items
III.1.9
,
III.1.10
and
III.1.12
above yield information about the span of
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
(provided
L_{P}\neq 0
). We now want to similarly investigate the span of the linear part

L_{P_{+a}}\ =\ \sum_{n}\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(a)\,\der^{n}

of the additive conjugate
P_{+a}
of
P
by some
a\prec 1
.
In the next two lemmas we assume
\operatorname{order}(L_{P})=r
(in particular,
L_{P}\neq 0
),

{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P})\prec 1
, and
a\prec 1
, we set

L:=L_{P},\quad L^{+}:=L_{P_{+a}},\quad{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L),

and set
L_{n}:=\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(0)
and
L_{n}^{+}:=\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(a)
, so
L=\sum_{n}L_{n}\der^{n}
,
L^{+}=\sum_{n}L_{n}^{+}\der^{n}
.
Recall from [ADH, 4.2] the decomposition of
P
into homogeneous parts:
P=\sum_{d}P_{d}
where
P_{d}=\sum_{\lvert{\boldsymbol{i}}\rvert=d}P_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}Y^{{\boldsymbol{i}}}
;
we set
P_{>1}:=\sum_{d>1}P_{d}
.

Lemma III.1.28
.

Suppose
P_{>1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}P_{1}
and
n\leqslant r
. Then

\mathrm{(i)}

L_{r}^{+}\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L_{r}
, and thus
\operatorname{order}(L^{+})=\operatorname{order}(L)=r
;

\mathrm{(ii)}

if
L_{n}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L
, then
L_{n}^{+}\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L_{n}
, and so
v(L_{n}^{+})=v(L_{n})
;

\mathrm{(iii)}

if
L_{n}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L
, then
L_{n}^{+}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L
, and so
v(L_{n}^{+})>v(L)
.

In particular,
L^{+}\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L
,
\operatorname{dwt}L^{+}=\operatorname{dwt}L
,
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(L^{+})\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
.

Proof.

Take
Q,R\in K\{Y\}
with
\deg_{Y^{(n)}}Q\leqslant 0
and
R\in Y^{(n)}K\{Y\}
, such that

P\ =\ Q+(L_{n}+R)Y^{(n)},\qquad\text{so}\qquad\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}\ =\ \frac{\partial R}{\partial Y^{(n)}}Y^{(n)}+L_{n}+R.

Now
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}P_{1}
,
so
\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}-L_{n}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}P_{1}
. In
K[\der]
we thus have

L_{n}^{+}-L_{n}\ =\ \frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(a)-L_{n}\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}L\ \asymp\ L_{r}.

So
L_{n}^{+}-L_{n}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L
and (taking
r=n
)

L_{r}^{+}-L_{r}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L_{r}
. This yields (i)–(iii).
∎

Lemma III.1.29
.

Suppose
P_{>1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}P_{1}
, and let

A,B\in K[\der]
be such that
L=A+B
,
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}L
.
Then

L^{+}\ =\ A+B^{+}\ \text{ where $B^{+}\in K[\der]$, $B^{+}\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}L^{+}$.}

In particular,
L-L^{+}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}L
.

Proof.

Let
A_{n},B_{n}\in K
be such that
A=\sum_{n}A_{n}\der^{n}
and
B=\sum_{n}B_{n}\der^{n}
, so
L_{n}=A_{n}+B_{n}
. Let any
n
(possibly
>r
) be given and
take
Q,R\in K\{Y\}
as in the proof of Lemma
III.1.28
. Then
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}P_{1}
.
Since
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}L
, this yields

\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}-A_{n}\ =\ \frac{\partial R}{\partial Y^{(n)}}Y^{(n)}+B_{n}+R\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}P_{1}.

We have
L_{n}^{+}=\frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(a)
, so

L_{n}^{+}-A_{n}\ =\ \frac{\partial P}{\partial Y^{(n)}}(a)-A_{n}\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}L.

By Lemma
III.1.28
we have

L^{+}\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}L
, hence
B^{+}=L^{+}-A\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{m+1}L^{+}
.
∎

III.2.
Holes and Slots

Throughout this section
K
is an
H
-asymptotic field with small derivation and with rational asymptotic integration. We set
\Gamma:=v(K^{\times})
.
So
K
is pre-
\operatorname{d}
-valued,
\Gamma\neq\{0\}
has no least positive element, and
\Psi\cap\Gamma^{>}\neq\emptyset
. We let
a
,
b
,
f
,
g
range over
K
, and
\phi
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
,
{\mathfrak{w}}
(possibly decorated) over
K^{\times}
. As at the end of the previous section we shorten “active in
K
” to “active”.

Holes

A
hole
in
K
is a triple
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
where

P\in K\{Y\}\setminus K
and

\widehat{a}
is an element of
\widehat{K}\setminus K
, for some
immediate asymptotic extension
\widehat{K}
of
K
,
such that
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
and
P(\widehat{a})=0
. (The extension
\widehat{K}
may vary with
\widehat{a}
.)
The
order
,
degree
, and
complexity
of a hole

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
are defined as the order, (total) degree, and complexity, respectively, of the differential
polynomial
P
.
A hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
is called
minimal
if no hole in
K
has smaller complexity; then
P
is a minimal annihilator of
\widehat{a}
over
K
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole in
K
, then
\widehat{a}
is a
K
-external zero of
P
, in the sense of Section
I.7
.
Conversely, every
K
-external zero
\widehat{a}
of a differential polynomial
P\in K\{Y\}^{\neq}

gives for every
{\mathfrak{m}}\succ\widehat{a}
a hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
.
By Proposition
I.7.35
and Corollary
I.7.41
:

Lemma III.2.1
.

Let
r\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geqslant 1}
, and suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free. Then

\text{$K$ is $\upomega$-free and $r$-newtonian}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$K$ has no hole of order~$\leqslant r$.}

Thus for
\upomega
-free
K
, being newtonian is equivalent to having no holes.
Recall that
K
being henselian is equivalent to
K
having no proper immediate algebraic valued field extension, and hence
to
K
having no hole of order
0
.

Minimal holes are like the “minimal counterexamples” in certain combinatorial settings, and we need to understand such holes in a rather detailed way for later use in inductive arguments. Below we also consider the more general notion of
Z
-minimal hole
,
which has an important role to play as well. We recall that
Z(K,\widehat{a})
is the set of all
Q\in K\{Y\}^{\neq}
that vanish at
(K,\widehat{a})
as defined in [ADH, 11.4].

Lemma III.2.2
.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a hole in
K
. Then
P\in Z(K,\widehat{a})
.
If

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is minimal, then
P
is an element of minimal complexity of
Z(K,\widehat{a})
.

Proof.

Let
a
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
with
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
. Since
\widehat{a}\notin K
lies in an immediate extension of
K
we can take
{\mathfrak{n}}
with
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\widehat{a}-a
.
By [ADH, 11.2.1] we then have
\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}P_{+a}\geqslant\operatorname{ndeg}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\geqslant 1
.
Hence
P\in Z(K,\widehat{a})
. Suppose
P
is not of minimal complexity
in
Z(K,\widehat{a})
. Take
Q\in Z(K,\widehat{a})
of minimal
complexity. Then [ADH, 11.4.8] yields a
K
-external zero
\widehat{b}
of
Q
,
and any
{\mathfrak{n}}\succ\widehat{b}
gives a hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
K
of smaller complexity than
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

In connection with the next result, note that
K
being
0
-newtonian just means that
K
is henselian as a valued field.

Corollary III.2.3
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free and has a minimal hole of order
r\geqslant 1
. Then
K
is
(r-1)
-newtonian,
and
\upomega
-free if
r\geqslant 2
.

Proof.

This is clear for
r=1
(and doesn’t need
\uplambda
-freeness), and for
r\geqslant 2

follows from Lemma
III.2.1
.
∎

Corollary III.2.4
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free and has a minimal hole of order
r\geqslant 2
. Assume also that
C
is algebraically closed and
\Gamma
is divisible. Then

K
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued,
r
-linearly closed, and
r
-linearly newtonian.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
I.2.9
, Corollary
I.7.42
, and Corollary
III.2.3
.
∎

Here is a linear version of Lemma
III.2.1
:

Lemma III.2.5
.

If
K
is
\uplambda
-free, then

\text{$K$ is $1$-linearly newtonian}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$K$ has no hole of degree~$1$ and order~$1$.}

If
r\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geqslant 1}
and
K
is
\upomega
-free, then

\text{$K$ is $r$-linearly newtonian}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$K$ has no hole of degree~$1$ and order~$\leqslant r$.}

Proof.

The first statement follows from Lemma
I.7.33
, and the second statement from Lemma
I.7.34
.
∎

Corollary III.2.6
.

If
K
is
\upomega
-free and has a minimal hole in
K
of order
r
and degree
>1
, then
K
is
r
-linearly newtonian.

Lemma III.2.7
.

Suppose
K
has a hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
of degree
1
, and
L_{P}\in K[\der]^{\neq}
splits over
K
. Then
K
has a hole of complexity
(1,1,1)
.

Proof.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
as in the hypothesis have minimal order. Then
{\operatorname{order}P\geqslant 1}
, so
\operatorname{order}P=\operatorname{order}L_{P}
.
Take
A,B\in K[\der]
such that
\operatorname{order}A=1
and
L_{P}=AB
.
If
\operatorname{order}B=0
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has complexity
(1,1,1)
.
Assume
\operatorname{order}B\geqslant 1
. Then
B(\widehat{a})\notin K
: otherwise, taking
Q\in K\{Y\}
of degree
1
with

L_{Q}=B
and
Q(0)=-B(\widehat{a})
yields a hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
where
\deg Q=1
and
L_{Q}
splits over
K
,
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has smaller order than
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
Set
\widehat{b}:=B(\widehat{a})

and take
R\in K\{Y\}
of degree
1
with
L_{R}=A
and
R(0)=P(0)
. Then

R(\widehat{b})\ =\ R(0)+L_{R}(\widehat{b})\ =\ P(0)+L_{P}(\widehat{a})\ =\ P(\widehat{a})\ =\ 0,

hence for any
{\mathfrak{n}}\succ\widehat{b}
,
(R,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
is a hole in
K
of complexity
(1,1,1)
.
∎

Corollary III.2.8
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free,
C
is algebraically closed, and
\Gamma
is divisible.
Then every minimal hole in
K
of degree
1
has order
1
.
If in addition
K
is
1
-linearly newtonian, then every minimal hole in
K
has degree
>1
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from Corollary
III.2.4
and the preceding lemma.
For the second statement, use the first and Lemma
III.2.5
.
∎

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a hole in
K
. We say
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal
if

P
has minimal complexity in
Z(K,\widehat{a})
. Thus if

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is minimal, then it is
Z
-minimal by Lemma
III.2.2
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, then by [ADH, remarks following 11.4.3], the differential polynomial
P
is a minimal annihilator of
\widehat{a}
over
K
.
Note also that
\operatorname{ndeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\geqslant 1
by [ADH, 11.2.1]. In more detail:

Lemma III.2.9
.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a hole in
K
. Then for all
{\mathfrak{n}}
with
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
,

1\ \leqslant\ \operatorname{dmul}P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ \leqslant\ \operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ \leqslant\ \operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}.

In particular,
\operatorname{ddeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{m}}}P\geqslant 1
.

Proof.

Assume
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Then
\widehat{a}={\mathfrak{n}}\widehat{b}
with
\widehat{b}\prec 1
;
put
Q:=P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\in K\{Y\}^{\neq}
. Then
Q(\widehat{b})=0
, hence
D_{Q}(0)=0
and so
\operatorname{dmul}Q=\operatorname{dmul}P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\geqslant 1
. The rest follows from
[ADH, 6.6.5(ii), 6.6.7, 6.6.9] and
\Gamma^{>}
having no least element.
∎

In the next lemma,
(\uplambda_{\rho})
,
(\upomega_{\rho})
are pc-sequences in
K
as in [ADH, 11.5, 11.7].

Lemma III.2.10
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free and

\upomega\in K
is such that
\upomega_{\rho}\leadsto\upomega

(
so
K
is not
\upomega
-free
)
. Then
we have a hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
in
K
where
P=2Y^{\prime}+Y^{2}+\upomega
and
\uplambda_{\rho}\leadsto\uplambda
, and
each such hole in
K
is a
Z
-minimal hole in
K
.

Proof.

From [ADH, 11.7.13] we obtain
\uplambda
in an immediate asymptotic extension
of
K
such that
\uplambda_{\rho}\leadsto\uplambda
and
P(\uplambda)=0
.
Taking any
{\mathfrak{m}}
with
\uplambda\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
then yields a hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
in
K
with
\uplambda_{\rho}\leadsto\uplambda
, and each such hole in
K

is a
Z
-minimal hole in
K
by [ADH, 11.4.13, 11.7.12].
∎

Corollary III.2.11
.

If
K
is
\uplambda
-free but not
\upomega
-free, then each minimal hole in
K
of positive order has complexity
(1,1,1)
or complexity
(1,1,2)
. If
K
is a Liouville closed
H
-field and not
\upomega
-free, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
is a minimal hole of complexity
(1,1,2)
,
where
\upomega
,
P
,
\uplambda
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
are as in Lemma
III.2.10
.

Here the second part uses Corollary
I.7.29
and Lemma
III.2.5
.

Slots

In some arguments the notion of a hole in
K
turns out to be too stringent. Therefore we introduce a more
flexible version of it:

Definition III.2.12
.

A
slot
in
K
is a triple
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
where
P\in K\{Y\}\setminus K
and
\widehat{a}
is an element of
\widehat{K}\setminus K
, for some immediate asymptotic extension
\widehat{K}
of
K
, such that
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
and
P\in Z(K,\widehat{a})
. The
order
,
degree
, and
complexity
of such a slot in
K
are defined to be the order, degree, and complexity of the differential polynomial
P
, respectively. A slot in
K
of degree
1
is also called a
linear
slot in
K
.
A slot
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
is
Z
-minimal
if
P
is of minimal complexity among elements of
Z(K,\widehat{a})
.

Thus by Lemma
III.2.2
, holes in
K
are slots in
K
, and a hole in
K
is
Z
-minimal iff
it is
Z
-minimal as a slot in
K
.
From [ADH, 11.4.13] we obtain:

Corollary III.2.13
.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a
Z
-minimal slot in
K
and
(a_{\rho})
be a divergent pc-sequence in
K
such that
a_{\rho}\leadsto\widehat{a}
. Then
P
is a minimal differential polynomial of
(a_{\rho})
over
K
.

We say that slots
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
K
are
equivalent
if

P=Q
,
{\mathfrak{m}}={\mathfrak{n}}
, and
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-a)
for all
a
; note that then
Z(K,\widehat{a})=Z(K,\widehat{b})
, so

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal iff
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is
Z
-minimal. Clearly this is an equivalence relation on the class of slots in
K
.
The following lemma often allows us to pass from a
Z
-minimal slot to
a
Z
-minimal hole:

Lemma III.2.14
.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a
Z
-minimal slot in
K
. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is equivalent to a
Z
-minimal hole in
K
.

Proof.

By [ADH, 11.4.8] we obtain
\widehat{b}
in an immediate asymptotic extension of
K
with
P(\widehat{b})=0

and
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-a)
for all
a
.
In particular
\widehat{b}\notin K
,
\widehat{b}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
, so
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a hole in
K
equivalent to
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

By [ADH, 11.4.8] the extension below containing
\widehat{b}
is not required to be immediate:

Corollary III.2.15
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a
Z
-minimal hole in
K
and
\widehat{b}
in an asymptotic extension of
K
satisfies

P(\widehat{b})=0
and
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-a)
for all
a
, then there is an isomorphism

K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle\to K\langle\widehat{b}\rangle
of valued differential fields over
K
sending
\widehat{a}
to
\widehat{b}
.

In particular, equivalent
Z
-minimal holes
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
K

yield an isomorphism
K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle\to K\langle\widehat{b}\rangle
of valued differential fields over
K
sending
\widehat{a}
to
\widehat{b}
.

From Lemmas
III.2.1
and
III.2.14
we obtain:

Corollary III.2.16
.

Let
r\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\geqslant 1}
, and suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free. Then

\text{$K$ is $r$-newtonian}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$K$ has no slot of order~$\leqslant r$.}

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a slot in
K
. Then
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
is a slot in
K
of the same complexity as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, and
if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, then so is
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
;
likewise with “hole in
K
” in place of “slot in
K
”.
For active
\phi
we have the
compositional conjugate

(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
\phi
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
: it is a
slot in
K^{\phi}
of the same complexity as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, it is
Z
-minimal if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is, and it is a hole (minimal hole) in
K^{\phi}
if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole (minimal hole, respectively) in
K
.
If the slots
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
K
are equivalent, then so are

(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(bQ,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
for
b\neq 0
, as well as the slots
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
K^{\phi}
for active
\phi
.

The following conventions are in force in the rest of this section:

We let
r
range over natural numbers
\geqslant 1
and let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
denote a slot in
K
of order
r
, so
P\notin K[Y]
has order
r
. We set
w:=\operatorname{wt}(P)
, so
w\geqslant r\geqslant 1
.

Thus
\operatorname{wt}(P_{+a})=\operatorname{wt}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})=\operatorname{wt}(P^{\phi})=w
.

Refinements and multiplicative conjugates

For
a
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
such that
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}

we obtain a slot
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
in
K

of the same complexity as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
[ADH, 4.3, 11.4].
Slots of this form are said to
refine

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and are called
refinements
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
A refinement of a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is itself a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, then so is any refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole in
K
, then so is each of its refinements, and likewise with “minimal hole” in place of “hole”.
For active
\phi
,
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
iff
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
are equivalent slots in
K
and

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, then

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-a})
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, and the slots

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
,
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-a})
in
K
are equivalent. Conversely, if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
are slots in
K
with equivalent refinements, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
are equivalent.

Lemma III.2.17
.

Let
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
be a slot in
K
. Then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, or

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
refines
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
.

Proof.

If
{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
, then
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
, so
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
whereas if
{\mathfrak{m}}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}
, then
(\widehat{a}-a)-(-a)=\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{n}}
, so

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})=\big((P_{+a})_{+(-a)},{\mathfrak{m}},({\widehat{a}-a})-(-a)\big)

refines
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
.
∎

Lemma III.2.18
.

Let
Q\in K\{Y\}^{\neq}
be such that
Q\notin Z(K,\widehat{a})
. Then there is a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
such that
\operatorname{ndeg}Q_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}=0
and
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\prec\widehat{a}
.

Proof.

Take
b
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
such that
\widehat{a}-b\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
and
\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}Q_{+b}=0
.
We shall find an
a
such that
\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}Q_{+a}=0
,
\widehat{a}-a\preccurlyeq\widehat{a}
, and
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
:
if
\widehat{a}-b\preccurlyeq\widehat{a}
, we take
a:=b
; if
\widehat{a}-b\succ\widehat{a}
,
then
-b\sim\widehat{a}-b
and so
\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}Q=\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}Q_{+b}=0
by [ADH, 11.2.7], hence
a:=0
works. We next arrange
\widehat{a}-a\prec\widehat{a}
: if
\widehat{a}-a\asymp\widehat{a}
, take
a_{1}
with
\widehat{a}-a_{1}\prec\widehat{a}
, so
a-a_{1}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
, hence

\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}Q_{+a_{1}}=\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}Q_{+a}=0
, and thus
a
can be replaced by
a_{1}
.
Since
\Gamma^{>}
has no least element, we can choose
{\mathfrak{n}}
with
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\prec\widehat{a},{\mathfrak{v}}
, and then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
as desired.
∎

If
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
then

D_{P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}=D_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}},+(a/{\mathfrak{m}})}}=D_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
by [ADH, 6.6.5(iii)], and thus

\operatorname{ddeg}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\ =\ \operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},\qquad\operatorname{dmul}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\ =\ \operatorname{dmul}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}.

In combination with Lemma
III.2.9
this has some useful consequences:

Corollary III.2.19
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole in
K
such that
\operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=1
.
Then
\operatorname{ddeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{m}}}P=1
, and for all
{\mathfrak{n}}
with
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
,
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}})
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
with
\operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}=\operatorname{dmul}P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}=1
.

Corollary III.2.20
.

Suppose
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
refines
the hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
. Then

\operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\ =\ 1\ \Longrightarrow\ \operatorname{ddeg}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ =\ \operatorname{dmul}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ =\ 1.

Proof.

Use

1\leqslant\operatorname{dmul}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\leqslant\operatorname{ddeg}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\leqslant\operatorname{ddeg}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=\operatorname{ddeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
III.2.9
applied to

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
.
∎

If
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, then in analogy with
\operatorname{ddeg}
and
\operatorname{dmul}
,

\operatorname{ndeg}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\ =\ \operatorname{ndeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},\qquad\operatorname{nmul}P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\ =\ \operatorname{nmul}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}.

(Use compositional conjugation by active
\phi
.)
Lemma
III.2.9
goes through for slots, provided we use

\operatorname{ndeg}
and
\operatorname{nmul}
instead of

\operatorname{ddeg}
and
\operatorname{dmul}
:

Lemma III.2.21
.

Suppose
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
