\operatorname{order}(L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}})\ =\ r,\qquad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P}).

Also
v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-b\big)>\gamma
, hence

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}\big(L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}}\big)\cap v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-K\big)\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-K)\ \leqslant\ \gamma\ <\ v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-b\big).

Thus
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is isolated.
∎

Remark III.4.7
.

Proposition
III.4.6
goes through if instead of assuming that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, we assume that

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is linear. (Same argument,
using Lemma
III.4.5
in place of Lemma
III.4.4
and

L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}}=L_{P}
in place of Lemma
III.3.24
.)

Corollary III.4.8
.

Suppose
r=1
, and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal or linear.
If
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})=\emptyset
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is isolated.
If
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})\neq\emptyset
, so
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})=\{v\mathfrak{g}\}
where
\mathfrak{g}\in K^{\times}
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is isolated iff
{\mathfrak{m}}\preccurlyeq\mathfrak{g}
or
\widehat{a}-K\succ\mathfrak{g}
.

This follows immediately from Lemmas
III.4.4
and
III.4.5
.
The results in the rest of this subsection are the
raison d’être
of isolated holes:

Proposition III.4.9
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is an isolated hole in
K

which is normal or linear.
Let
\widehat{b}
in an immediate
asymptotic extension of
K
satisfy
{P(\widehat{b})=0}
and
\widehat{b}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-a)
for all
a
, so
\widehat{b}\notin K
.

Proof.

Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
\widehat{b}
by
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}})
,
\widehat{b}/{\mathfrak{m}}
, we arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Let
a
be given; we show
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-a)
. This is clear if
a\succcurlyeq 1
, so assume
{a\prec 1}
.
Corollary
III.3.21
(if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal)
and Lemma
III.2.21
(if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is linear) give
{\operatorname{ndeg}P=1}
. Thus
P
is in newton position at
a
by Corollary
III.2.23
. Moreover
v(\widehat{a}-a)\notin{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})
,
hence
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v^{\operatorname{e}}(P,a)
by Lemma
I.7.15
.
Likewise, if
v(\widehat{b}-a)\notin{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})
, then

v(\widehat{b}-a)=v^{\operatorname{e}}(P,a)
by Lemma
I.7.15
, so
v({\widehat{a}-a})=v(\widehat{b}-a)
.

Thus to finish the proof it is enough to show that
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})\cap v(\widehat{b}-K)\leqslant 0
.
Now
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})\rvert\leqslant r
by Corollary
I.4.5
, so
we have
b\prec 1
such that

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})\cap v(\widehat{b}-K)\ <\ v(\widehat{b}-b),

in particular,
v(\widehat{b}-b)\notin{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, then
Lemma
III.3.24
gives

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+b}}),

so by the above with
b
instead of
a
we have
v(\widehat{a}-b)=v(\widehat{b}-b)
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is linear, then
L_{P_{+a}}=L_{P}=L_{P_{+b}}
, and we obtain likewise
v(\widehat{a}-b)=v(\widehat{b}-b)
.
Hence

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})\cap v(\widehat{b}-K)\ \subseteq\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P_{+a}})\cap\Gamma^{<v(\widehat{a}-b)}\ \subseteq\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-K)\ \leqslant\ 0.

using Lemmas
III.4.4
and
III.4.5
for the last step.
∎

Combining Proposition
III.4.9
with Corollary
III.2.15
yields:

Corollary III.4.10
.

Let
K
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
\widehat{b}
be as in Proposition
III.4.9
, and assume also that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal. Then there is an isomorphism
K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle\to K\langle\widehat{b}\rangle
of valued differential fields
over
K
sending
\widehat{a}
to
\widehat{b}
.

Using the Normalization Theorem, we now obtain:

Corollary III.4.11
.

Suppose
K
is
\upomega
-free and
\Gamma
is divisible. Then every minimal hole in
K
of order
r
has an
isolated refinement

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
such that for any
\widehat{b}
in an immediate
asymptotic extension of
K
with
{P(\widehat{b})=0}
and
\widehat{b}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
there is an isomorphism
K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle\to K\langle\widehat{b}\rangle
of valued differential fields
over
K
sending
\widehat{a}
to
\widehat{b}
.

Proof.

Given a minimal linear hole in
K
of order
r
, use Remark
III.4.7
to refine it to an isolated minimal linear hole

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
of order
r
, and use Corollary
III.4.10
.
Suppose we are given a minimal nonlinear hole in
K
of order
r
. Then
K
is
r
-linearly newtonian by Corollary
III.2.6
. Then Theorem
III.3.33
yields a refinement
(Q,{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
of it and an active
\theta

in
K
such that the minimal hole
(Q^{\theta},{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
in
K^{\theta}
is normal. Proposition
III.4.6
gives an
isolated refinement
(Q^{\theta}_{+d},{\mathfrak{v}},\widehat{d}-d)
of
(Q^{\theta},{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
.
Suitably refining
(Q^{\theta}_{+d},{\mathfrak{v}},\widehat{d}-d)
further followed by compositionally conjugating with a suitable active element of
K^{\theta}
yields by Theorem
III.3.33
and Lemma
III.4.2
a
refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
(and thus of the originally given hole) and an active
\phi
in
K
such that

(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is both normal and isolated. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is isolated, and we can apply Corollary
III.4.10
to
K^{\phi}
and
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in the role of
K
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

For
r=1
we can replace “
\upomega
-free” in Proposition
III.4.9
and Corollary
III.4.10
by the weaker “
\uplambda
-free” (same proofs, using Lemma
I.7.20
instead of Lemma
I.7.15
):

Proposition III.4.12
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is an isolated hole in
K
of order
r=1
, and suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal or linear. Let
\widehat{b}
in an immediate
asymptotic extension of
K
satisfy
{P(\widehat{b})=0}
and
\widehat{b}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-a)
for all
a
.
(
Hence if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, then there is an isomorphism
K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle\to K\langle\widehat{b}\rangle
of valued differential fields
over
K
sending
\widehat{a}
to
\widehat{b}
.
)

This leads to an analogue of Corollary
III.4.11
:

Corollary III.4.13
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free and
\Gamma
is divisible.
Then every quasilinear minimal hole in
K
of order
r=1
has an
isolated refinement

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
such that for any
\widehat{b}
in an immediate
asymptotic extension of
K
with
{P(\widehat{b})=0}
and
\widehat{b}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
there is an isomorphism
K\langle\widehat{a}\rangle\to K\langle\widehat{b}\rangle
of valued differential fields
over
K
sending
\widehat{a}
to
\widehat{b}
.

Proof.

Suppose we are given a quasilinear minimal hole in
K
of order
r=1
.
Then Corollary
III.3.38
yields a refinement
(Q,{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
of it and an active
\theta
in
K

such that the quasilinear minimal hole
(Q^{\theta},{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
in
K^{\theta}
of order
1
is normal.
Proposition
III.4.6
gives an isolated refinement
(Q^{\theta}_{+d},{\mathfrak{v}},\widehat{d}-d)
of
(Q^{\theta},{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
,
and then Corollary
III.3.38
yields a refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{w}},\widehat{d})
and an active
\phi
in
K
such
that
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and isolated. Now apply Proposition
III.4.12

with
K^{\phi}
and
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in the role of
K
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

Next a variant of Lemma
III.2.1
for
r=1
without assuming
\upomega
-freeness:

Corollary III.4.14
.

Suppose
K
is
1
-newtonian and
\Gamma
is divisible. Then
K
has no quasilinear
Z
-minimal slot of order
1
.

Proof.

By Proposition
I.7.28
,
K
is
\uplambda
-free.
Towards a contradiction, let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a quasilinear
Z
-minimal slot in
K
of order
1
.
By Lemma
III.2.14
we arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole in
H
. Using Corollary
III.3.35
,
Lemma
III.4.2
and the remark before it,
and Proposition
III.4.6
, we can refine further so that

(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and isolated for some active
\phi
in
K
.
Then there is no
y\in K
with
P(y)=0
and
y\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
, by
Proposition
III.4.12
, contradicting Lemma
III.2.27
for
L=K
.
∎

Finally, for isolated linear holes, without additional hypotheses:

Lemma III.4.15
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is an isolated linear hole in
K
, and
{\widehat{a}-a}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then

P(a)\neq 0
, and
\gamma=v(\widehat{a}-a)
is the unique element of
\Gamma\setminus{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})
such that
v^{\operatorname{e}}_{L_{P}}(\gamma)=v\big(P(a)\big)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
III.4.5
,
\gamma:=v(\widehat{a}-a)\in\Gamma\setminus{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})
.
Since
\deg P=1
,

L_{P}(\widehat{a}-a)\ =\ L_{P}(\widehat{a})-L_{P}(a)\ =\ -P(0)-L_{P}(a)\ =\ -P(a),

so
P(a)\neq 0
. By Lemma
I.4.6
,

v^{\operatorname{e}}_{L_{P}}(\gamma)=v\big(L_{P}(\widehat{a}-a)\big)=v\big(P(a)\big)
.
∎

In
[
8
]
we shall prove a version of Proposition
III.4.9
without the hypothesis that
\widehat{b}
lies in an
immediate extension of
K
.
In Section
IV.4
below we consider, in a more restricted setting, a variant of isolated slots, with
ultimate exceptional values taking over the role played by exceptional values in Definition
III.4.1
.

III.5.
Holes of Order and Degree One

In this section
K
is a
\operatorname{d}
-valued field of
H
-type with small derivation and rational asymptotic integration. (Later on we will impose
additional restrictions on
K
.) We also let
\widehat{K}
be an immediate asymptotic extension of
K
.
We focus here on slots of complexity
(1,1,1)
in
K
.
As a byproduct we obtain in Corollary
III.5.18
a partial generalization
of Corollary
III.3.49
to minimal holes in
K
of arbitrary degree.
First we establish in the next subsection a useful formal identity.
We let
j
,
k
range over
{\mathbb{N}}
(in addition to
m
,
n
, as usual).

An integration identity

Let
R
be a differential ring, and let
f
,
g
,
h
, range over
R
. We use

\int f=g+\int h
as a suggestive way to express that
f=g^{\prime}+h
, and likewise,
\int f=g-\int h
means that
f=g^{\prime}-h
. For example,

\int f^{\prime}g\ =\ fg-\int fg^{\prime}\qquad\text{(integration by parts)}.

Let
\operatorname{e},\xi\in R^{\times}
satisfy

\operatorname{e}^{\dagger}=\xi
. We wish to expand
\int\operatorname{e}
by iterated integration by parts. Now for
g=\operatorname{e}
we have
g^{\prime}\in R^{\times}
with
\frac{g}{g^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{\xi}
, so in view of
\operatorname{e}=g^{\prime}\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}
:

\int\operatorname{e}\ =\ \int g^{\prime}\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}\ =\ \frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi}-\int g\bigg(\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\prime},

and

\left(\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime}=\left(\frac{1}{\xi}\right)^{\prime}=\frac{-\xi^{\prime}}{\xi^{2}}=\frac{-\xi^{\dagger}}{\xi},

and thus

\int\operatorname{e}\ =\ \frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi}+\int\frac{\xi^{\dagger}}{\xi}\operatorname{e}.

More generally, using the above identities for
g=\operatorname{e}
,

\displaystyle\int f\operatorname{e}\

\displaystyle=\ \int g^{\prime}f\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}\ =\ \frac{f}{\xi}\operatorname{e}-\int g\bigg(f\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\ \frac{f}{\xi}\operatorname{e}-\int g\left(f^{\prime}\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}+f\bigg(\frac{\operatorname{e}}{g^{\prime}}\bigg)^{\prime}\,\right)\ =\ \frac{f}{\xi}\operatorname{e}-\int\left(\frac{f^{\prime}}{\xi}\operatorname{e}+fg\Big(\frac{-\xi^{\dagger}}{\xi}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle=\ \frac{f}{\xi}\operatorname{e}-\int\left(\frac{f^{\prime}}{\xi}\operatorname{e}+\frac{-f\xi^{\dagger}}{\xi}\operatorname{e}\right)\ =\ \frac{f}{\xi}\operatorname{e}+\int\left(\frac{\xi^{\dagger}f-f^{\prime}}{\xi}\right)\operatorname{e}.

Replacing
f
by
f/\xi^{k}
gives the following variant of this identity:

\int\frac{f}{\xi^{k}}\operatorname{e}\ =\ \frac{f}{\xi^{k+1}}\operatorname{e}+\int\frac{(k+1)\xi^{\dagger}f-f^{\prime}}{\xi^{k+1}}\operatorname{e}.

Induction on
m
using the last identity yields:

Lemma III.5.1
.

Set
\zeta:=\xi^{\dagger}
. Then

\int f\operatorname{e}\ =\ \sum_{j=0}^{m}P_{j}(\zeta,f)\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{j+1}}+\int P_{m+1}(\zeta,f)\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}},

where the
P_{j}\in{\mathbb{Q}}\{Z,V\}={\mathbb{Q}}\{Z\}\{V\}
are independent of
R
,
\operatorname{e}
,
\xi
:

P_{0}\ :=\ V,\qquad P_{j+1}\ :=\ (j+1)ZP_{j}-P_{j}^{\prime}.

Thus
P_{j}=P_{j0}V+P_{j1}V^{\prime}+\cdots+P_{jj}V^{(j)}

with all
P_{jk}\in{\mathbb{Q}}\{Z\}

and
P_{jj}=(-1)^{j}
.

For example,

P_{0}=V,\quad P_{1}=ZV-V^{\prime},\quad P_{2}=(2Z^{2}-Z^{\prime})V-3ZV^{\prime}+V^{\prime\prime}.

An asymptotic expansion

In this subsection
\xi\in K
and
\xi\succ^{\flat}1
; equivalently,
\xi\in K
satisfies
\xi\succ 1
and
\zeta:=\xi^{\dagger}\succcurlyeq 1
.

We also assume that
\xi\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
.

Since
\widehat{K}
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued of
H
-type with asymptotic integration, it has by [ADH, 10.2.7] an immediate
asymptotic extension

\widehat{K}(\phi)
with
\phi^{\prime}=\xi
. Then the algebraic closure of
\widehat{K}(\phi)
is still
\operatorname{d}
-valued of
H
-type, by [ADH, 9.5], and so [ADH, 10.4.1] yields a

\operatorname{d}
-valued
H
-asymptotic extension
L
of this algebraic closure with an element
\operatorname{e}\neq 0
such that
\operatorname{e}^{\dagger}=\xi
.
All we need about
L
below is that it is a
\operatorname{d}
-valued
H
-asymptotic extension of
\widehat{K}
with elements
\phi
and
\operatorname{e}

such that
\phi^{\prime}=\xi
and
\operatorname{e}\neq 0,\ \operatorname{e}^{\dagger}=\xi
. Note that then
L
has small derivation, and
\xi\succ^{\flat}1
in
L
. (The element
\phi
will only play an auxiliary role later in this subsection.)

Lemma III.5.2
.

v(\operatorname{e})\notin\Gamma
.

Proof.

Suppose otherwise. Take
a\in K^{\times}
with
a\operatorname{e}\asymp 1
. Then

a^{\dagger}+\xi=(a\operatorname{e})^{\dagger}\in\operatorname{I}(L)\cap K=\operatorname{I}(K)
and thus

\xi\in\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
, a contradiction.
∎

By Lemma
III.5.2
there is for each
g\in L
at most one
\widehat{f}\in\widehat{K}
with
\big(\widehat{f}\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi}\big)^{\prime}=g
.
Let
f\in K^{\times}
be given with
f\preccurlyeq 1
, and suppose
\widehat{f}\in\widehat{K}
satisfies

\big(\widehat{f}\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi}\big)^{\prime}=f\operatorname{e}
.
Our aim is to show that with
P_{j}
as in Lemma
III.5.1
,
the series
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}P_{j}(\zeta,f)\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}
is a kind of asymptotic expansion of
\widehat{f}
. The partial sums

f_{m}:=\sum_{j=0}^{m}P_{j}(\zeta,f)\frac{1}{\xi^{j}}

of this series lie in
K
, with
f_{0}=f

and
f_{n}-f_{m}\prec\xi^{-m}
for
m<n
,
by Lemma
I.1.13
.
More precisely, we show:

Proposition III.5.3
.

We have
\widehat{f}-f_{m}\prec\xi^{-m}
for all
m
.

(
Thus:
f\asymp 1\,\Rightarrow\,\widehat{f}\sim f
.
)

Towards the proof, note that by
Lemma
III.5.1
with
R=L
,

\displaystyle\widehat{f}\,\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi}\

\displaystyle=\ \sum_{j=0}^{m}P_{j}(\zeta,f)\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{j+1}}+I_{m},\quad I_{m}\in L,\text{ and thus}

(III.5.1)

\displaystyle\widehat{f}\

\displaystyle=\ f_{m}+\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m}

where
I_{m}\in\widehat{K}\operatorname{e}
satisfies
I_{m}^{\prime}=P_{m+1}(\zeta,f)\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}
, a condition that determines
I_{m}
uniquely up to an additive constant from
C_{L}
.
The proof of Proposition
III.5.3
now rests on the following lemmas:

Lemma III.5.4
.

In
L
we have

(\operatorname{e}\xi^{l})^{(k)}\ \sim\ \operatorname{e}\xi^{l+k}
, for all
k
and all
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

This is Corollary
I.1.14
with our
L
in the role of
K
there, and taking
\operatorname{e}^{\phi}
there as our
\operatorname{e}\in L
; note that here our
\phi\in L
with
\phi^{\prime}=\xi
is needed.

Lemma III.5.5
.

Suppose
\operatorname{e}\succ\xi^{m+1}
. Then
\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m}\prec\xi^{-m}
.

Proof.

This amounts to
I_{m}\prec\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}
. Suppose
I_{m}\succcurlyeq\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\succ 1
. Then we have
I_{m}^{\prime}\succcurlyeq\big(\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\big)^{\prime}\sim\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m}}

by Lemma
III.5.4
, so
P_{m+1}(\zeta,f)\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\succcurlyeq\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m}}
, and thus
P_{m+1}(\zeta,f)\succcurlyeq\xi
, contradicting Lemma
I.1.13
.
∎

Lemma III.5.6
.

Suppose
\operatorname{e}\preccurlyeq\xi^{m}
. Then
I_{m}\prec 1
and
\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m}\prec\xi^{-m}
.

Proof.

Lemma
I.1.13
gives

P_{m+1}(\zeta,f)\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\preccurlyeq\zeta^{N}\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\preccurlyeq\frac{\zeta^{N}}{\xi}\quad\text{ for some $N\in{\mathbb{N}}$,}

so
v(I_{m}^{\prime})>\Psi_{L}
, and thus

I_{m}\preccurlyeq 1
. If
I_{m}\asymp 1
, then
v(\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m})=v(\xi)-v(\operatorname{e})\notin\Gamma
, contradicting
\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m}=\widehat{f}-f_{m}\in\widehat{K}
, by (
III.5.1
). Thus
I_{m}\prec 1
.
Now assume towards a contradiction that
\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m}\succcurlyeq\xi^{-m}
. Then
\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\preccurlyeq I_{m}\prec 1
, so

I_{m}^{\prime}\succcurlyeq\big(\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m+1}}\big)^{\prime}\sim\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi^{m}}

by Lemma
III.5.4
, and this yields a contradiction as in the proof of Lemma
III.5.5
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
III.5.3
.

Let
m
be given. If
\operatorname{e}\succ\xi^{m+1}
, then

\widehat{f}-f_{m}=\frac{\xi}{\operatorname{e}}I_{m}\prec\xi^{-m}
by Lemma
III.5.5
. Suppose

\operatorname{e}\preccurlyeq\xi^{m+1}
. Then Lemma
III.5.6
(with
m+1
instead of
m
) gives
\widehat{f}-f_{m+1}\prec\xi^{-(m+1)}
, hence
\widehat{f}-f_{m}=(\widehat{f}-f_{m+1})+(f_{m+1}-f_{m})\prec\xi^{-m}
.
∎

Application to linear differential equations of order
1

Proposition
III.5.3
yields information about the asymptotics of solutions (in
\widehat{K}
) of certain linear differential equations of order
1
over
K
:

Corollary III.5.7
.

Let
f,\xi\in K
,
f\preccurlyeq 1
,
\xi\succ^{\flat}1
,
\xi\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
, and suppose
{y\in\widehat{K}}
satisfies
y^{\prime}+\xi y=f
. Then there is for every
m
an element
y_{m}\in K
with
{y-y_{m}\prec\xi^{-m}}
. Also,

f\asymp 1\ \Rightarrow\ y\sim f\xi^{-1}
.

Proof.

Take
L
and
\operatorname{e}\in L
as at the beginning of the previous subsection, and
set
\widehat{f}:=y\xi\in\widehat{K}
. Then for
A:=\der+\xi
we have
A(\widehat{f}/\xi)=f
, so

\left(\widehat{f}\,\frac{\operatorname{e}}{\xi}\right)^{\prime}\ =\ (\widehat{f}/\xi)^{\prime}\operatorname{e}+(\widehat{f}/\xi)\xi\operatorname{e}\ =\ A(\widehat{f}/\xi)\operatorname{e}\ =\ f\operatorname{e},

hence
\widehat{f}
is as in the previous subsection. Now apply Proposition
III.5.3
.
∎

Corollary III.5.8
.

Let
g\in K
,
u\in K^{\times}
be such that
g\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
and
\xi:=g+u^{\dagger}\succ^{\flat}1
. Suppose
z\in\widehat{K}
satisfies
z^{\prime}+gz=u
.
Then
z\sim u/\xi
, and for every
m
there is a
z_{m}\in K
such that
z-z_{m}\prec u\xi^{-m}
.

Proof.

Set
A:=\der+g
.
Then
A_{\ltimes u}=\der+\xi
, so
A(z)=u
yields for
y:=z/u
that
y^{\prime}+\xi y=1
. Now observe that
\xi\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
and use the previous corollary.
∎

Slots of order and degree
1

In the rest of this section we use the material above to analyze slots of order and degree
1
in
K
.

Below
K
is henselian and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is a slot in
K
with
\operatorname{order}P=\deg P=1
and
\widehat{f}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
. We let
f
range over
K
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K^{\times}
, and
\phi
over active elements of
K
.

Thus

\displaystyle P\

\displaystyle=\ a(Y^{\prime}+gY-u)\quad\text{where $a\in K^{\times},\ g,u\in K$,}

\displaystyle P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\

\displaystyle=\ a{\mathfrak{n}}\big(Y^{\prime}+(g+{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})Y-{\mathfrak{n}}^{-1}u\big).

Since
K
is henselian,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is
Z
-minimal and thus equivalent to a hole in
K
, by Lemma
III.2.14
.
Also,
\operatorname{nmul}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=\operatorname{ndeg}P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=1
by Lemma
III.2.21
.
We have
L_{P}=a(\der+g)
, so

g\in K^{\dagger}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \ker L_{P}\neq\{0\},\quad\quad\qquad g\in\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})\neq\emptyset,

using for the second equivalence the remark on
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A)
preceding Lemma
I.4.9
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is isolated, then
P(f)\neq 0
for
\widehat{f}-f\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
by Lemmas
III.2.14
and
III.4.15
, so, taking
f=0
, we have
u\neq 0
.

Lemma III.5.9
.

Suppose
\der K=K
and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})=\emptyset
, so

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is isolated by Lemma
III.4.5
.

Proof.

Passing to an equivalent hole in
K
, arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is a hole in
K
. Since
\der K=K
and
\widehat{f}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
, the remark following Lemma
I.7.21

yields
g\notin K^{\dagger}=\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
, therefore
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})=\emptyset
.
∎

Set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}})
; thus
{\mathfrak{v}}=1
if
g+{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq 1
and
{\mathfrak{v}}=1/(g+{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger})
otherwise. Hence from Example
III.3.3
and the remarks before Lemma
III.3.17
we obtain:

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is normal

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})$ is steep}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1,

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is deep

\displaystyle\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1\text{ and }u\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{v}}.

We have
P(0)=-au
, and if
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec 1
, then
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}\sim(a{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{v}})Y
. Thus

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})$ is strictly normal}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1\text{ and }u\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}{\mathfrak{v}}.

We say that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is
balanced
if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is steep and
P(0)\preccurlyeq S_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}(0)
, equivalently,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is steep and
u\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Thus

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})$ is strictly normal}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})$ is balanced}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})$ is deep},

and with
b\in K^{\times}
,

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})\text{ is balanced }\Longleftrightarrow(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}/{\mathfrak{n}})\text{ is balanced }\Longleftrightarrow(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})\text{ is balanced}.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced, then so is any slot in
K
equivalent to
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
.
Moreover, if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is a hole in
K
, then
P(0)=-L_{P}(\widehat{f})
, so

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced iff it is steep and
L_{P}(\widehat{f})\preccurlyeq S_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}(0)
.
By Corollary
III.3.14
, if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is steep, then
\widehat{f}-f\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
for some
f
.
For balanced
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
we have a variant of this fact:

Lemma III.5.10
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced and
g\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
. Then there is for all
n
an
f
such that
\widehat{f}-f\prec{\mathfrak{v}}^{n}{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Proof.

Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
by an equivalent hole in
K
, we arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is a hole in
K
, and replacing

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
by
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{f}/{\mathfrak{m}})
, that
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Then
{\widehat{f}}^{\prime}+g\widehat{f}=u
with
g=1/{\mathfrak{v}}\succ^{\flat}1
,
g\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
, and
u\preccurlyeq 1
. Hence the lemma follows from Corollary
III.5.7
.
∎

In the next corollary we assume that the subgroup
K^{\dagger}
of
K
is divisible. (Since
K
is henselian and
\operatorname{d}
-valued, this holds if the groups
C^{\times}
and
\Gamma
are divisible.)

Corollary III.5.11
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced and
g\notin\operatorname{I}(K)+K^{\dagger}
. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
has a strictly normal refinement
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f}-f)
.

Proof.

First arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is a hole in
K
.
The previous lemma yields an
f
such that
\widehat{f}-f\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{3}{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Then
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f}-f)
is a strictly normal refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
, by Lemma
III.3.46
(where the latter uses divisibility of
K^{\dagger}
).
∎

Lemma III.5.12
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced with
v\widehat{f}\notin{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})
and
\widehat{f}-f\preccurlyeq\widehat{f}
. Then the refinement
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f}-f)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})

is balanced.

Proof.

By Lemma
III.2.14
we arrange
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is a hole.
Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
and
f
by
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{f}/{\mathfrak{m}})
and
f/{\mathfrak{m}}
we arrange next that
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
.
By the remark preceding Lemma
III.3.2
,
(P_{+f},1,\widehat{f}-f)
is steep.
Take
\phi
such that
v\widehat{f}\notin{\mathscr{E}}\big((L_{P})^{\phi}\big)
, and set
\widehat{g}:=\widehat{f}-f
, so
0\neq\widehat{g}\preccurlyeq\widehat{f}
.
Recall from [ADH, 5.7.5] that
L_{P^{\phi}}=(L_{P})^{\phi}
and hence

L_{P^{\phi}}(\widehat{f})=L_{P}(\widehat{f})
and
L_{P}(\widehat{g})=L_{P^{\phi}}(\widehat{g})
.
Thus

L_{P_{+f}}(\widehat{g})\ =\ L_{P}(\widehat{g})\ \preccurlyeq\ L_{P^{\phi}}\widehat{g}\ \preccurlyeq\ L_{P^{\phi}}\widehat{f}\ \asymp\ L_{P^{\phi}}(\widehat{f})\ =L_{P}(\widehat{f})\ \preccurlyeq\ S_{P}(0)\ =\ S_{P_{+f}}(0),

using [ADH, 4.5.1(iii)] to get the second
\preccurlyeq
and
v\widehat{f}\notin{\mathscr{E}}(L_{P^{\phi}})
to get
\asymp
; the last
\preccurlyeq
uses
(P,1,\widehat{f})
being a hole.
Therefore
(P_{+f},1,\widehat{g})
is balanced.
∎

Combining Lemmas
III.4.2
and
III.5.12
yields:

Corollary III.5.13
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced and isolated, and
\widehat{f}-f\preccurlyeq\widehat{f}
, then the refinement
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f}-f)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is also balanced and isolated.

We call
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})

proper
if the differential polynomial
P
is proper as defined in Section
I.7
(that is,
u\neq 0
and
g+u^{\dagger}\succ^{\flat}1
). If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is proper, then so are
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
for
b\neq 0
and
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}/{\mathfrak{n}})
, as well as each refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
and each slot in
K
equivalent to
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
. By Lemma
I.7.23
,
if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is proper, then so is
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
for
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
.

Lemma III.5.14
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is proper and
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp u
; then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is balanced.

Proof.

Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
by
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{f}/{\mathfrak{m}})
, we arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
.
Then
u\asymp 1
and thus
(P,1,\widehat{f})
is balanced.
∎

Proposition III.5.15
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is proper and
v\widehat{f}\notin{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{P})
. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
has a balanced refinement.

Proof.

We arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
as usual.
By Lemmas
I.7.26
and
III.2.14
we have

\widehat{f}\ \sim\ u/(g+u^{\dagger})\prec^{\flat}u.

Hence if
u\preccurlyeq 1
, then

(P,u,\widehat{f})
refines
(P,1,\widehat{f})
, and so
(P,u,\widehat{f})
is balanced by Lemma
III.5.14
.
Assume now that
u\succ 1
. Then
1\prec u\prec g
by Lemma
I.7.25
and
\operatorname{nmul}P=1
, and hence
u^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq g^{\dagger}\prec g
. So
g\sim g+u^{\dagger}\succ^{\flat}1
, hence
(P,1,\widehat{f})
is steep, and
\widehat{f}\sim u/g
. Set
f:=u/g\prec 1
; then
(P_{+f},1,\widehat{f}-f)
is a steep refinement of
(P,1,\widehat{f})
. Moreover

P_{+f}(0)=P(f)=af^{\prime}\prec a=S_{P_{+f}}(0),

hence
(P_{+f},1,\widehat{f}-f)
is balanced.
∎

Corollary III.5.16
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free. Then there exists
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
and a refinement
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{f}-f})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
such that
(P^{\phi}_{+f},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}-f)
is balanced.

Proof.

Using Remark
III.4.7
we can replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
by a refinement to arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
is isolated. Then
u\neq 0

by the remark before Lemma
III.5.9
, so by Lemma
I.7.24
,
P^{\phi}
is proper, eventually.
Now apply Proposition
III.5.15
to a proper (and isolated)
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
with
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
.
∎

Corollary III.5.17
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free,
\der K=K
,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, and
K^{\dagger}
is divisible. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
has a
refinement
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}-f)
such that
(P_{+f}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}-f)
is strictly normal for some
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
.

Proof.

Corollary
III.5.16
yields a refinement
(P_{+f_{1}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{1},\widehat{f}-f_{1})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
and a
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
such that

(P^{\phi}_{+f_{1}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{1},\widehat{f}-f_{1})
is balanced. By Lemma
III.5.9
with
K^{\phi}
in the role of
K
and

(P^{\phi}_{+f_{1}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{1},\widehat{f}-f_{1})
in the role of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
we can apply Corollary
III.5.11
to
(P^{\phi}_{+f_{1}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{1},\widehat{f}-f_{1})
to give a strictly normal refinement
(P^{\phi}_{f_{1}+f_{2}},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}-f_{1}-f_{2})
of it.
Thus for
f:=f_{1}+f_{2}
the refinement
(P_{+f},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}-f)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{f})
has the property that
(P_{+f}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{f}-f)
is strictly normal.
∎

Combining this corollary with Corollaries
III.2.8
,
III.3.49
, and Lemma
III.3.40
yields:

Corollary III.5.18
.

If
K
is
\upomega
-free and algebraically closed with
\der K=K
and
{\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}}
, then every minimal hole in
K
of order
\geqslant 1
has
a refinement
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{g})
such that
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{g})
is deep and strictly normal, eventually.

Remark
.

Suppose
K
is
\uplambda
-free, with
\der K=K
,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, and
K^{\dagger}
is divisible.
By Corollary
III.3.37
every linear slot in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
has a refinement

(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{g})
such that
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{g})
is deep and normal, eventually.
We don’t know whether every linear minimal hole in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
has a refinement

(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{g})
such that
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{g})
is deep and strictly normal, eventually.
(For
r=1
this holds by Corollary
III.5.17
.)

Part IV
Holes in
H
-Fields

Here we focus on holes in the algebraic closure
K
of a Liouville closed
H
-field
H
with small derivation.
After the preliminary Sections
IV.1
and
IV.2
we come in Sections
IV.3
–
IV.5
to
the technical heart of Part
IV
.
Section
IV.3
shows that every minimal hole in
K
gives rise to a
Z
-minimal slot

(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
H
such that the slot
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
H^{\phi}
is eventually
split-normal
, meaning
normal with its linear part “asymptotically” splitting over
K^{\phi}
; see
Definition
IV.3.3
for the precise definition, and Theorem
IV.3.9
for the detailed statement of the main result of this section.
When
H
is a Hardy field as in
[
6
]
, this asymptotic splitting will allow us to define
a contractive operator on a space of real-valued functions; this operator then has a fixed point whose germ
y
satisfies
Q(y)=0
,
y\prec{\mathfrak{n}}
.
A main difficulty then lies in guaranteeing that such germs
y
have similar asymptotic properties as
\widehat{b}
.
Sections
IV.4
and
IV.5
prepare the ground for dealing with this:
In Section
IV.4
we strengthen the concept of isolated slot to
ultimate
slot (in
H
, or in
K
).
This relies on the ultimate exceptional values of linear differential operators over
K
introduced in Part
II
.
In Section
IV.5
we single out among
split-normal slots those that are
repulsive-normal
,
culminating in the proof of Theorem
IV.5.28
: an analogue of Theorem
IV.3.9
producing from a minimal hole in
K
and for small enough active
\phi>0
in
H
a deep
repulsive-normal ultimate slot in
H^{\phi}
. This is further improved in Theorem
IV.5.43
.

IV.1.
Some Valuation-Theoretic Lemmas

The present section contains preliminaries for the next section on approximating splittings of linear differential operators; these facts
in turn are used in Section
IV.3
on split-normality.
We shall often deal with real closed fields with extra structure, denoted usually by
H
, since the results in this section about such
H
will be applied to
H
-fields (and to Hardy fields in
[
6
]
).
We begin by summarizing some purely valuation-theoretic facts.

Completion and specialization of real closed valued fields

Let
H
be a real closed valued field whose valuation ring
\mathcal{O}
is convex in
H
(with respect to the unique ordering on
H
making
H
an ordered field). Using [ADH, 3.5.15] we equip the algebraic closure
K=H[\imag]
(
\imag^{2}=-1
) of
H
with its unique valuation ring lying over
\mathcal{O}
, which is
\mathcal{O}+\mathcal{O}\imag
. We set
\Gamma:=v(H^{\times})
, so
\Gamma_{K}=\Gamma
.

Lemma IV.1.1
.

The completion
H^{\operatorname{c}}
of the valued field
H
is real closed, its valuation ring is convex in
H^{\operatorname{c}}
, and there is a unique valued field embedding

H^{\operatorname{c}}\to K^{\operatorname{c}}
over
H
. Identifying
H^{\operatorname{c}}
with its image under this embedding we have
H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]=K^{\operatorname{c}}
.

Proof.

For the first two claims, see [ADH, 3.5.20]. By [ADH, 3.2.20] we have a unique valued field embedding

H^{\operatorname{c}}\to K^{\operatorname{c}}
over
H
, and viewing
H^{\operatorname{c}}
as a valued subfield of
K^{\operatorname{c}}
via this embedding we have
K^{\operatorname{c}}=H^{\operatorname{c}}K=H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]
by [ADH, 3.2.29].
∎

We identify
H^{\operatorname{c}}
with its image in
K^{\operatorname{c}}
as in the previous lemma.
Fix a convex subgroup
\Delta
of
\Gamma
. Let
\dot{\mathcal{O}}
be the valuation ring of the coarsening
of
H
by
\Delta
, with maximal ideal
\dot{\smallo}
. Then by [ADH, 3.5.11 and subsequent remarks]
\dot{\mathcal{O}}
and
\dot{\smallo}
are convex in
H
, the specialization
\dot{H}=\dot{\mathcal{O}}/\dot{\smallo}
of
H
by
\Delta
is naturally an ordered and valued field, and the valuation ring of
\dot{H}
is convex in
\dot{H}
. Moreover,

\dot{H}
is even real closed by [ADH, 3.5.16].
Likewise, the coarsening of
K
by
\Delta
has valuation ring

\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
with maximal ideal
\dot{\smallo}_{K}

and valued residue field
\dot{K}
. Thus
\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
lies over
\dot{\mathcal{O}}
by [ADH, 3.4, subsection
Coarsening and valued field extensions
], so

(K,\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K})
is a valued field extension of

(H,\dot{\mathcal{O}})
. In addition:

Lemma IV.1.2
.

\dot{K}
is a valued field extension of
\dot{H}
and an algebraic closure of
\dot{H}
.

Proof.

The second part follows by general valuation theory from
K
being an algebraic closure of
H
. In fact, with the image of
\imag\in\mathcal{O}_{K}\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}

in
\dot{K}
denoted by the same symbol, we have

\dot{K}=\dot{H}[\imag]
.
∎

Next, let
\widehat{H}
be an immediate valued field extension of
H
. We equip
\widehat{H}
with the unique field ordering making it an ordered field extension of
H
in which
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{H}}
is convex; see [ADH, 3.5.12].
Choose
\imag
in a field extension of
\widehat{H}
with
\imag^{2}=-1
. Equip
\widehat{H}[\imag]
with the unique valuation ring of
\widehat{H}[\imag]
that lies over
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{H}}
, namely
{\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{H}}+\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{H}}\imag}
[ADH, 3.5.15]. Let
\widehat{a}=\widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\,\imag\in\widehat{H}[\imag]\setminus H[\imag]
with
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\widehat{H}
, and let
b
,
c
range over
H
.
Then

v\big(\widehat{a}-(b+c\imag)\big)\ =\ \min\!\big\{v(\widehat{b}-b),v(\widehat{c}-c)\big\}

and thus
v\big(\widehat{a}-H[\imag]\big)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
and
v\big(\widehat{a}-H[\imag]\big)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
.

Lemma IV.1.3
.

We have
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
or
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
. Moreover, the following are equivalent:

\mathrm{(i)}

v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
;

\mathrm{(ii)}

for all
b
there is a
c
with
v\big(\widehat{a}-(b+c\imag)\big)=v(\widehat{b}-b)
;

\mathrm{(iii)}

v\big(\widehat{a}-H[\imag]\big)=v(\widehat{b}-H)
.

Proof.

For the first assertion, use that
v(\widehat{b}-H),v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq\Gamma_{\infty}
are downward closed. Suppose
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
, and let
b
be given.
If
\widehat{c}\in H
, then for
c:=\widehat{c}
we have
v\big(\widehat{a}-(b+c\imag)\big)=v(\widehat{b}-b)
. Suppose
\widehat{c}\notin H
. Then
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq\Gamma
does not have a largest element and

v(\widehat{b}-b)\in v(\widehat{c}-H)
, so we have
c
with
v(\widehat{b}-b)<v(\widehat{c}-c)
; thus

v\big(\widehat{a}-(b+c\imag)\big)=\min\!\big\{v(\widehat{b}-b),v(\widehat{c}-c)\big\}=v(\widehat{b}-b).

This shows (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii). Moreover,
(ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) follows from
v\big(\widehat{a}-H[\imag]\big)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
, and (iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) from
v\big(\widehat{a}-H[\imag]\big)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
.
∎

So if
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
, then:
\widehat{a}
is special over
H[\imag]\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \widehat{b}
is special over
H
.

To apply Lemma
IV.1.3
to
H
-fields we assume in the next lemma more generally that
H
is equipped with a derivation making it a
\operatorname{d}
-valued field and that
\widehat{H}
is equipped with a derivation
\der
making it an asymptotic field extension of
H
; then
\widehat{H}
is also
\operatorname{d}
-valued
with the same constant field as
H
[ADH, 9.1.2].

Lemma IV.1.4
.

Suppose
H
is closed under integration. Then we have:

v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)\ \Longrightarrow\ v(\der\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\der\widehat{c}-H).

Proof.

Assume
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
.
Let
b\in H
, and take
g\in H
with
g^{\prime}=b
; adding a suitable constant to
g
we arrange
\widehat{b}-g\not\asymp 1
.
Next, take
h\in H
with
\widehat{b}-g\asymp\widehat{c}-h
. Then

\der\widehat{b}-b\ =\ \der(\widehat{b}-g)\ \asymp\ \der(\widehat{c}-h)\ =\ \der\widehat{c}-h^{\prime},

so
v(\der\widehat{b}-b)\in v(\der\widehat{c}-H)
.
∎

Embedding into the completion

In this subsection
K
is an asymptotic field,
\Gamma:=v(K^{\times})\neq\{0\}
, and

L
is an asymptotic field extension of
K
such that
\Gamma
is cofinal in
\Gamma_{L}
.

Lemma IV.1.5
.

Let
a\in L
and let
(a_{\rho})
be a c-sequence in
K
with
a_{\rho}\to a
in
L
. Then for each
n
,
(a_{\rho}^{(n)})
is a c-sequence in
K
with
a_{\rho}^{(n)}\to a^{(n)}
in
L
.

Proof.

By induction on
n
it suffices to treat the case
n=1
. Let

\gamma\in\Gamma_{L}
; we need to show the existence of an index
\sigma
such that
v(a^{\prime}-a_{\rho}^{\prime})>\gamma
for all
\rho>\sigma
.
By [ADH, 9.2.6] we have
f\in L^{\times}
with
f\prec 1
and
v(f^{\prime})\geqslant\gamma
.
Take
\sigma
such that
v(a-a_{\rho})>vf
for all
\rho>\sigma
.
Then
v(a^{\prime}-a_{\rho}^{\prime})>v(f^{\prime})\geqslant\gamma
for
\rho>\sigma
. ∎

Let
K^{\operatorname{c}}
be the completion of the valued differential field
K
; then

K^{\operatorname{c}}
is asymptotic by [ADH, 9.1.6]. Lemma
IV.1.5
and [ADH, 3.2.13 and 3.2.15] give:

Corollary IV.1.6
.

Let
(a_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family of elements of
L
such that
a_{i}
is the limit in
L

of a c-sequence in
K
, for each
i\in I
.
Then there is a unique embedding
K\big\langle(a_{i})_{i\in I}\big\rangle\to K^{\operatorname{c}}
of valued differential fields over
K
.

Next suppose that
H
is a real closed asymptotic field whose
valuation ring
\mathcal{O}
is convex in
H
with
\mathcal{O}\neq H
, the asymptotic extension
\widehat{H}
of
H

is immediate, and
\imag
is an element of an asymptotic
extension of
\widehat{H}
with
\imag^{2}=-1
. Then
\imag\notin\widehat{H}
, and we identify
H^{\operatorname{c}}
with a valued subfield of
H[\imag]^{\operatorname{c}}

as in Lemma
IV.1.1
, so that

H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]=H[\imag]^{\operatorname{c}}
as in that lemma. Using also Lemma
IV.1.5
we see that

H^{\operatorname{c}}
is actually a valued
differential
subfield of the asymptotic field
H[\imag]^{\operatorname{c}}
, and so
H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]=H[\imag]^{\operatorname{c}}
also as
asymptotic
fields. Thus by Corollary
IV.1.6
applied to
K:=H
and

L:=\widehat{H}
:

Corollary IV.1.7
.

Let
a\in\widehat{H}[\imag]
be the limit in
\widehat{H}[\imag]
of a c-sequence in
H[\imag]
. Then
\operatorname{Re}a
,
\operatorname{Im}a
are limits in
\widehat{H}
of c-sequences in
H
, hence
there is a unique embedding

H[\imag]\big\langle\!\operatorname{Re}a,\operatorname{Im}a\big\rangle\to H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]
of valued differential fields over
H[\imag]
.

IV.2.
Approximating Linear Differential Operators

In this section
K
is a valued differential field with small derivation,

\Gamma:=v(K^{\times})
.
For later use we prove here Corollaries
IV.2.6
and
IV.2.9
and consider
strong splitting
.
Much of this section rests on the following basic estimate for linear differential operators which split over
K
:

Lemma IV.2.1
.

Let
b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\in K
and
n
be given.
Then there exists
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma^{\geqslant}
such that for all
b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
with
\gamma>\gamma_{0}
and
v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant(n+r)\gamma
for
i=1,\dots,r
, we have
v(B-B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant vB+n\gamma
, where

B\ :=\ (\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r})\in K[\der],\quad B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ (\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\in K[\der].

Proof.

By induction on
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
. The case
r=0
is clear (any
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma^{\geqslant}
works). Suppose the lemma holds for a certain
r
.
Let
b_{1},\dots,b_{r+1}\in K
and
n
be given. Set
\beta_{i}:=vb_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,r+1
). Take
\gamma_{0}
as in the lemma applied to

b_{1},\dots,b_{r}
and
n+1
in place of
n
, and
let
\gamma_{1}:=\gamma_{0}
if
b_{r+1}=0
,
\gamma_{1}:=\max\big\{\gamma_{0},\lvert\beta_{r+1}\rvert\big\}
otherwise.
Let
b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r+1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
with

\gamma>\gamma_{1}
and
v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant(n+r+1)\gamma
for
i=1,\dots,r+1
.
Set

B\ :=\ (\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r}),\qquad B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ (\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\qquad E\ :=\ B-B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}.

Then

B(\der-b_{r+1})\ =\ B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}_{r+1})+B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}_{r+1}-b_{r+1})+E(\der-b_{r+1}).

Inductively we have
vE\geqslant vB+(n+1)\gamma
. Suppose
E\neq 0
and
0\neq b_{r+1}\not\asymp 1
. Then by
[ADH, 6.1.5],

\displaystyle v_{E}(\beta_{r+1})-v_{B}(\beta_{r+1})\

\displaystyle=\ vE-vB+o(\beta_{r+1})

\displaystyle\geqslant\ (n+1)\gamma+o(\beta_{r+1})

\displaystyle\geqslant\ n\gamma+|\beta_{r+1}|+o(\beta_{r+1})\ >\ n\gamma.

Hence, using
E(\der-b_{r+1})=E\der-Eb_{r+1}
and
v(E\der)=v(E)\neq v_{E}(\beta_{r+1})
,

\displaystyle v\big(E(\der-b_{r+1})\big)\ =\ \min\!\big\{vE,v_{E}(\beta_{r+1})\big\}\

\displaystyle>\ \min\!\big\{vB,v_{B}(\beta_{r+1})\big\}+n\gamma

\displaystyle=\ v\big(B(\der-b_{r+1})\big)+n\gamma,

where for the last equality we use
vB\neq v_{B}(\beta_{r+1})
. Also,

v\big(B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}_{r+1}-b_{r+1})\big)=v_{B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}\big(v(b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}_{r+1}-b_{r+1})\big)\geqslant v_{B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}\big((n+r+1)\gamma\big)=v_{B}\big((n+r+1)\gamma\big)

where we use [ADH, 6.1.7] for the last equality. Moreover, by [ADH, 6.1.4],

v_{B}\big((n+r+1)\gamma\big)-n\gamma\ \geqslant\ vB+(r+1)\gamma+o(\gamma)\ >\ vB\ \geqslant\ v\big(B(\der-b_{r+1})\big).

This yields the desired result for
E\neq 0
,
0\neq b_{r+1}\not\asymp 1
. The cases
E\neq 0
,
b_{r+1}=0
and
E=0
,
0\neq b_{r+1}\not\asymp 1
are simpler versions of the above, and so is the case
E\neq 0
,
b_{r+1}\asymp 1
using
[ADH, 5.6.1(i)]. The remaining cases,
E=0
,
b_{r+1}=0
and
E=0
,
b_{r+1}\asymp 1
, are even simpler to handle.
∎

Corollary IV.2.2
.

Let
a,b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\in K
,
a\neq 0
.
Then there exists
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma^{\geqslant}
such that for all
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
with
\gamma>\gamma_{0}
,
v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant va+\gamma
, and
v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant(r+1)\gamma
for
i=1,\dots,r
, we have

v(A-A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant vA+\gamma
, where

A\ :=\ a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r})\in K[\der],\quad A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\in K[\der].

Proof.

Take
\gamma_{0}
as in the previous lemma applied to
b_{1},\dots,b_{r}
and
n=1
, and let
B=(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r})
,
A=aB
. Let
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
be such that
\gamma>\gamma_{0}
,
v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant va+\gamma
, and
v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant(r+1)\gamma
for
i=1,\dots,r
. Set
B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})
,
A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
.
Then

E\ :=\ A-A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ a(B-B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})+(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}.

Lemma
IV.2.1
gives
vB^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}=vB
, and so

v\big(a(B-B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\big)\ \geqslant\ va+vB+\gamma\ =\ vA+\gamma,\quad v\big((a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\big)\ =\ v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})+vB\ \geqslant\ vA+\gamma,

so
vE\geqslant vA+\gamma
.
∎

In the rest of this subsection we assume
P\in K\{Y\}\setminus K
,
set
r:=\operatorname{order}P
, and let
{\boldsymbol{i}}
,
{\boldsymbol{j}}
range over
{\mathbb{N}}^{1+r}
.

Lemma IV.2.3
.

For
\delta:=v\big(P-P(0)\big)
and all
h\in\smallo
we have
v\big(P_{+h}-P\big)\geqslant\delta+\textstyle\frac{1}{2}vh
.

Proof.

Note that
\delta\in\Gamma
and
v(P_{{\boldsymbol{j}}})\geqslant\delta
for all
{\boldsymbol{j}}
with
\lvert{\boldsymbol{j}}\rvert\geqslant 1
.
Let
h\in\smallo^{\neq}
and
{\boldsymbol{i}}
be given; we claim that
v\big((P_{+h})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}-P_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\big)\geqslant\delta+\textstyle\frac{1}{2}vh
.
By [ADH, (4.3.1)] we have

(P_{+h})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\ =\ P_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}+Q(h)\quad\text{where $Q(Y):=\sum_{\lvert{\boldsymbol{j}}\rvert\geqslant 1}{{\boldsymbol{i}}+{\boldsymbol{j}}\choose{\boldsymbol{i}}}P_{{\boldsymbol{i}}+{\boldsymbol{j}}}\,Y^{{\boldsymbol{j}}}\in K\{Y\}$.}

From
Q(0)=0
and [ADH, 6.1.4] we obtain

v(Q_{\times h})\ \geqslant\ v(Q)+vh+o(vh)\ \geqslant\ \delta+\textstyle\frac{1}{2}vh.

Together with
v\big(Q(h)\big)\geqslant v(Q_{\times h})
this yields the lemma.
∎

Corollary IV.2.4
.

Let
f\in K
. Then there exists
\delta\in\Gamma
such that for all
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
with
f-f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\prec 1
we have

v\big(P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}-P_{+f}\big)\geqslant\delta+\textstyle\frac{1}{2}v(f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-f)
.

Proof.

Take
\delta
as
in the preceding lemma with
P_{+f}
in place of
P
and
h=f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-f
.
∎

Corollary IV.2.5
.

Let
a,b_{1},\dots,b_{r},f\in K
be such that

A\ :=\ L_{P_{+f}}\ =\ a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r}),\qquad a\ \neq\ 0.

Then there exists
\gamma_{1}\in\Gamma^{\geqslant}
such that for all
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma
, if

\gamma>\gamma_{1},\ v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant va+\gamma,\ v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant(r+1)\gamma\ (i=1,\dots,r),\text{ and }v(f-f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant 4\gamma,

then

\mathrm{(i)}

v\big(P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}-P_{+f}\big)\geqslant vA+\gamma
; and

\mathrm{(ii)}

L_{P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}}=a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})+E
where
vE\geqslant vA+\gamma
.

Proof.

Take
\gamma_{0}
as in Corollary
IV.2.2
applied to
a,b_{1},\dots,b_{r}
, and
take
\delta
as
in Corollary
IV.2.4
.
Then
\gamma_{1}:=\max\{\gamma_{0},vA-\delta\}
has the required property.
∎

In the next result
L
is a valued differential field extension of
K
with small derivation such that
\Gamma
is cofinal in
\Gamma_{L}
.
Then the natural inclusion
K\to L
extends uniquely to an embedding
K^{\operatorname{c}}\to L^{\operatorname{c}}

of valued fields by [ADH, 3.2.20]. It is easy to check that this is even an embedding of valued
differential
fields; we identify
K^{\operatorname{c}}
with a valued differential subfield of
L^{\operatorname{c}}
via this embedding.

Corollary IV.2.6
.

Let
a,b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\in L^{\operatorname{c}}
and
f\in K^{\operatorname{c}}
be such that in
L^{\operatorname{c}}[\der]
,

A\ :=\ L_{P_{+f}}\ =\ a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r}),\qquad a,f\neq 0,\quad{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1,

and let
w\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then there are
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in L
and
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
such that

a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ \sim\ a,\qquad f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ \sim\ f,\qquad A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=L_{P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}}\ \sim\ A,\qquad\operatorname{order}A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ r,\qquad{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}},

and such that for
\Delta:=\big\{\alpha\in\Gamma_{L}:\,\alpha=o\big(v({\mathfrak{v}})\big)\big\}
we have in
L[\der]
,

A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})+E,\qquad E\prec_{\Delta}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A.

Proof.

Let
\gamma_{1}\in\Gamma_{L}^{\geqslant}
be as in Corollary
IV.2.5
applied to
L^{\operatorname{c}}
in place of
K
, and take
\gamma_{2}\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma_{2}\geqslant\max\{\gamma_{1},\frac{1}{4}vf\}+vA
and

\gamma_{2}\geqslant v\big((P_{+f})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\big)
for all
{\boldsymbol{i}}
with
(P_{+f})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\neq 0
.
Let
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
\gamma>\gamma_{2}
. Then

\gamma-vA>\gamma_{1}
. By the
density of
K
,
L
in
K^{\operatorname{c}}
,
L^{\operatorname{c}}
, respectively, we can take

a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in L
and
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
such that

v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \geqslant\ va+(\gamma-vA),\qquad v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant(r+1)(\gamma-vA)\ \text{ for $i=1,\dots,r$,}

and

v(f-f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant 4(\gamma-vA)>vf
. Then
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\sim a
,
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\sim f
, and by Corollary
IV.2.5
,

v\big(P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}-P_{+f}\big)\ \geqslant\ \gamma,\quad A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=L_{P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}}\ =\ a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})+E,\quad vE\geqslant\gamma.

Hence

(P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\sim(P_{+f})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}
if
(P_{+f})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\neq 0
, and

v\big((P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}\big)>\gamma_{2}\geqslant vA
if
(P_{+f})_{{\boldsymbol{i}}}=0
, so
{A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\sim A}
,
\operatorname{order}A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}=r
, and
{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\sim{\mathfrak{v}}
.
Choosing
\gamma
so that also
\gamma>v({\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A)+\Delta
we achieve in addition that
E\prec_{\Delta}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A
.
∎

Keeping it real

In this subsection
H
is a real closed
H
-asymptotic field with small derivation whose valuation ring is convex, with

\Gamma:=v(H^{\times})\neq\{0\}
, and
K
is the asymptotic extension
H[\imag]
of
H
with
\imag^{2}=-1
.
Then
H^{\operatorname{c}}
is real closed and
{H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]=K^{\operatorname{c}}}
as valued field extension of
H
according to Lemma
IV.1.1
, and as asymptotic field extension of
H
by the discussion after Corollary
IV.1.6
.
Using the real splittings from Definition
I.1.5
we show here that we can “preserve the reality of
A
” in Corollary
IV.2.6
.

Lemma IV.2.7
.

Let
A\in H^{\operatorname{c}}[\der]
be of order
r\geqslant 1
and let
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})\in H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]^{r}

be a real splitting of
A
over
H^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]
. Then for every

\gamma\in\Gamma
there are
g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,g_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}

in
H[\imag]

such that
v(g_{i}-g_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})>\gamma
for
i=1,\dots,r
,

A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ (\der-g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-g_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\in H[\der],

and
(g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,g_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})
is a real splitting of
A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
over
H[\imag]
.

Proof.

We can reduce to the case where
r=1
or
r=2
.
If
r=1
, then the lemma holds trivially, so suppose
r=2
. Then again the lemma holds trivially if
g_{1},g_{2}\in H^{\operatorname{c}}
, so we can assume instead that

g_{1}\ =\ a-b\imag+b^{\dagger},\quad g_{2}\ =\ a+b\imag,\qquad a\in H^{\operatorname{c}},\ b\in(H^{\operatorname{c}})^{\times}.

Let
\gamma\in\Gamma
be given. The density of
H
in
H^{\operatorname{c}}
gives
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H
with
v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant\gamma
.
Next, choose
