\gamma^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\geqslant\max\{\gamma,vb\}
and
\alpha^{\prime}>\gamma
for all nonzero
\alpha>\gamma^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-vb
in
\Gamma
,
and take
b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H
with
v(b-b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})>\gamma^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
. Then
v(b-b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})>\gamma
and
b\sim b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
.
In fact,
b=b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(1+\varepsilon)
where
v\varepsilon+vb=v(b-b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})>\gamma^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
and so

v\big((b/b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})^{\dagger}\big)=v(\varepsilon^{\prime})>\gamma
. Set
g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\imag+{b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}^{\dagger}
and
g_{2}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}+b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\imag
. Then

\displaystyle v(g_{1}-g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\

\displaystyle=\ v\big(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}+(b/b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})^{\dagger}+(b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-b)\imag\big)\ >\ \gamma,\quad v(g_{2}-g_{2}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ >\ \gamma,

\displaystyle(\der-g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdot(\der-g_{2}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\

\displaystyle=\ \der^{2}-\big(2a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}+b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}{}^{\dagger}\big)\der+\big((-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})^{\prime}+a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}{}^{2}+a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}{}^{\dagger}+b^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}{}^{2}\big)\ \in\ H[\der].

Hence
(g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},g_{2}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})
is a real splitting of

A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=(\der-g_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})(\der-g_{2}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\in H[\der]
.
∎

In the next two corollaries
a\in(H^{\operatorname{c}})^{\times}
and

b_{1},\dots,b_{r}\in K^{\operatorname{c}}
are such that

A\ :=\ a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{r})\in H^{\operatorname{c}}[\der],

(b_{1},\dots,b_{r})
is a real splitting of
A
over

K^{\operatorname{c}}
, and
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1
. We set
\Delta:=\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})
.

Corollary IV.2.8
.

Suppose
A=L_{P_{+f}}
with
P\in H\{Y\}

of order
r\geqslant 1
and
f
in
(H^{\operatorname{c}})^{\times}
. Let
\gamma\in\Gamma
and
w\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then there is
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H^{\times}
such that
v(f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-f)\geqslant\gamma
,

(IV.2.1)

\ f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ \sim\ f,\quad A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ L_{P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}}\ \sim\ A,\quad\operatorname{order}A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ r,\quad{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}},

and we have
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H^{\times}
,
b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
, and
B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H[\der]
with

A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}=B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}+E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
,
E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\prec_{\Delta}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A
, such that

B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\qquad v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\ v(b_{1}-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\ \dots\ ,v(b_{r}-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \geqslant\ \gamma,

and
(b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})
is a real splitting of
B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
over
K
.

Proof.

We apply Corollary
IV.2.6
with
H
,
K
in the role of
K
,
L
, and take
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
as in the proof of that corollary. We can assume
\gamma>\gamma_{2}
, so that
\gamma-vA>0
.
The density of
H
in
H^{\operatorname{c}}
gives
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H
such that
v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant\max\!\big\{va+(\gamma-vA),\gamma\big\}
(so
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\sim a
),
and Lemma
IV.2.7
gives
b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
such that

v(b_{i}-b_{i}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant\max\!\big\{(r+1)(\gamma-vA),\gamma\big\}
for
i=1,\dots,r
, and

(b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})
is a real splitting of

B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\in H[\der]

over
K
.
Take
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H
with
v(f-f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\geqslant\max\!\big\{4(\gamma-vA),\gamma\big\}
.
Then (
IV.2.1
) follows from the proof of Corollary
IV.2.6
. We can increase
\gamma
so that
\gamma>v({\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A)+\Delta
, and then we have

A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\prec_{\Delta}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A
.
∎

This result persists after multiplicative conjugation:

Corollary IV.2.9
.

Suppose
A=L_{P_{+f,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
with
P\in H\{Y\}
of order
r\geqslant 1
, and
f
in
(H^{\operatorname{c}})^{\times}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
. Let
\gamma\in\Gamma
,
w\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then there is
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H^{\times}
such that

v(f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-f)\geqslant\gamma,\quad f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ \sim\ f,\quad A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ L_{P_{+f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}\ \sim\ A,\quad\operatorname{order}A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ r,\quad{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}},

and we have
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H^{\times}
,
b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K
, and
B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H[\der]
with the properties stated in the previous corollary.

Proof.

Put
Q:=P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}\in H\{Y\}
,
g:=f/{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\operatorname{c}}
; then
Q_{+g}=P_{+f,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
. Applying the previous corollary to
Q
,
g
in place of
P
,
f
yields

g^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H^{\times}
,
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H^{\times}
, and
{b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in K}
such that
v(g^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}-g)\geqslant\ \gamma-v{\mathfrak{m}}
,

g^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ \sim\ g,\qquad A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ :=\ L_{Q_{+g^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}}}\ \sim\ A,\qquad\operatorname{order}A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ r,\qquad{\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}}

and

A^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}=B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}+E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
, with
B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\in H[\der]
,
E^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\prec_{\Delta}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}A
,
and

B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}\ =\ a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}(\der-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\cdots(\der-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\qquad v(a-a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\ v(b_{1}-b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}),\ \dots\ ,v(b_{r}-b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})\ \geqslant\ \gamma,

and
(b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}})
is a real splitting of
B^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
over
K
.
Therefore
f^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}:=g^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
and
a^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},b_{1}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}},\dots,b_{r}^{\mathord{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.6}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}}
have the required properties.
∎

Strong splitting

In this subsection
H
is a real closed
H
-field with small derivation and asymptotic integration
. Thus
K:=H[\imag]
is a
\operatorname{d}
-valued extension of
H
.
Let
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
have order
r\geqslant 1
and set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)
, and let
f
,
g
,
h
(possibly subscripted) range over
K
.
Recall from Section
I.1
that a splitting of
A
over
K
is an
r
-tuple
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
such that

A\ =\ f(\der-g_{1})\cdots(\der-g_{r})\quad\text{where $f\neq 0$.}

We call such a splitting
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
of
A
over
K

strong
if

\operatorname{Re}g_{j}\succcurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}
for
j=1,\dots,r
, and
we say that
A

splits strongly over
K
if there is a strong splitting of
A
over
K
.
This notion is mainly of interest for
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec 1
, since otherwise
{\mathfrak{v}}=1
, and then any splitting of

A
over
K
is a strong splitting of
A
over
K
.

Lemma IV.2.10
.

Let
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
be a strong splitting of
A
over
K
. If
{h\neq 0}
, then
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is a strong splitting of
hA
over
K
. If
h\asymp 1
, then
{(g_{1}-h^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-h^{\dagger})}
is a strong splitting of
Ah
over
K
.

Proof.

The first statement is clear, so suppose
h\asymp 1
. Now use Lemma
I.1.1

and the fact that
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec 1
implies
{\operatorname{Re}h^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq h^{\dagger}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}}
. If
{\mathfrak{v}}=1
, then use that
{\mathfrak{v}}(Ah)=1
by Corollary
III.1.3
.
∎

Lemma IV.2.11
.

Suppose
g\asymp\operatorname{Re}g
. Then
A=\der-g
splits strongly over
K
.

Proof.

Assuming
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec 1
gives
{\mathfrak{v}}^{\prime}\prec 1
, so
{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}\prec 1/{\mathfrak{v}}\asymp g\asymp\operatorname{Re}g
.
∎

In particular, every
A\in H[\der]^{\neq}
of order
1
splits strongly over
K
.

Lemma IV.2.12
.

Suppose
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is a strong splitting of
A
over
K
and
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
. Let
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
be active in
H
and set
h_{j}:=\phi^{-1}\big(g_{j}-(r-j)\phi^{\dagger}\big)
for
j=1,\dots,r
. Then
(h_{1},\dots,h_{r})
is a strong splitting of
A^{\phi}
over
K^{\phi}=H^{\phi}[\imag]
.

Proof.

By Lemma
I.1.2
,
(h_{1},\dots,h_{r})
is a splitting of
A^{\phi}
over
K^{\phi}
.
We have
\phi^{\dagger}\prec 1\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}
,
so
\operatorname{Re}h_{j}\sim\phi^{-1}\operatorname{Re}g_{j}\succcurlyeq\phi^{-1}{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}
for
j=1,\dots,r
.
Set
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A^{\phi})
and
\derdelta:=\phi^{-1}\der
.
Lemma
III.1.20
gives

{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}\asymp{\mathfrak{w}}^{\dagger}
, so

\phi^{-1}{\mathfrak{v}}^{\dagger}\asymp\derdelta({\mathfrak{w}})/{\mathfrak{w}}
.
∎

In the next two results we assume that for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
and

{\mathfrak{n}}\in H^{\times}
there is given an element

{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\in H^{\times}
such that
({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})^{\dagger}=q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}

(
and thus

v({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})=q\,v({\mathfrak{n}})
).

Lemma IV.2.13
.

Suppose
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is a splitting of
A
over
K
,
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec 1
,
{\mathfrak{n}}\in H^{\times}
, and
[{\mathfrak{v}}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{n}}]
. Then for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
with at most
r
exceptions,
(g_{1}-q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is a strong splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
over
K
.

Proof.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
. Then

(g_{1}-q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is a splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
over
K
, by Lemma
I.1.1
. Moreover,
\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})\big]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{n}}]
, by Lemma
III.1.9
, so

{\mathfrak{v}}(A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})^{\dagger}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
.
Thus if
\operatorname{Re}g_{j}\not\sim q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
for
j=1,\dots,r
, then

(g_{1}-q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is a strong splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
over
K
.
∎

Corollary IV.2.14
.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a steep slot in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
whose linear part
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
splits over
K
and such that
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta}{\mathfrak{m}}
for
\Delta:=\Delta\big({\mathfrak{v}}(L)\big)
. Then for all sufficiently small
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
, any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp|{\mathfrak{v}}(L)|^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
in
K^{\times}
gives a steep refinement
\big(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}\big)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
whose linear part
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}
splits strongly over
K
.

Proof.

Note that
|f|\asymp f
for all
f
. Lemma
III.3.1
gives
q_{0}\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
such that
for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
with
q\leqslant q_{0}
and any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp|{\mathfrak{v}}(L)|^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
,

(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is a steep refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
. Now apply Lemma
IV.2.13
with
L
,
{\mathfrak{v}}(L)
,
|{\mathfrak{v}}(L)|
in the respective roles
of
A
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
, and use Lemma
IV.2.10
and the fact that for
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp|{\mathfrak{v}}(L)|^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
we have
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}=L\cdot{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}=L|{\mathfrak{v}}(L)|^{q}h
with
h\asymp 1
.
∎

We finish this section with a useful fact on slots in
K
.
Given such a slot
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, the element
\widehat{a}
lies in an immediate asymptotic extension of
K
that might not be of the form

\widehat{H}[\imag]
with
\widehat{H}
an immediate
H
-field extension of
H
.
By the next lemma we can nevertheless often reduce to this situation, and more:

Lemma IV.2.15
.

Suppose
H
is
\upomega
-free. Then every
Z
-minimal slot in
K
of positive order is equivalent to a hole

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
K
with
\widehat{b}\in\widehat{K}=\widehat{H}[\imag]
for some immediate
\upomega
-free newtonian
H
-field extension
\widehat{H}
of
H
.

Proof.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a
Z
-minimal slot in
K
of order
\geqslant 1
.
Take an immediate
\upomega
-free newtonian
H
-field extension
\widehat{H}
of
H
; such
\widehat{H}
exists by (
0.9
).
Then
\widehat{K}=\widehat{H}[\imag]
is also newtonian by (
0.12
).
Now apply Corollary
III.2.29
with
L:=\widehat{K}
to obtain
\widehat{b}\in\widehat{K}
such that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a hole in
K
equivalent to
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

IV.3.
Split-Normal Slots

In this section
H
is a real closed
H
-field with small derivation and asymptotic integration. We let
\mathcal{O}:=\mathcal{O}_{H}
be its valuation ring and
C:=C_{H}
its constant field.
We fix an immediate asymptotic extension
\widehat{H}
of
H
with valuation ring
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}
and an element
\imag
of an asymptotic extension of
\widehat{H}
with
\imag^{2}=-1
.
Then
\widehat{H}
is also an
H
-field by [ADH, 10.5.8],
\imag\notin\widehat{H}
and
K:=H[\imag]
is an algebraic closure of
H
. With
\widehat{K}:=\widehat{H}[\imag]
we have the inclusion diagram

\textstyle{{\widehat{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{K}={\widehat{H}}[\imag]}
\textstyle{H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K=H[\imag]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

By [ADH, 3.5.15, 10.5.7],

K
and
\widehat{K}
are
\operatorname{d}
-valued with valuation rings
\mathcal{O}+\mathcal{O}\imag
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}+\widehat{\mathcal{O}}\imag
and
with the same constant field
C[\imag]
, and
\widehat{K}
is an immediate extension of
K
. Thus
H
,
K
,
\widehat{H}
,
\widehat{K}
have the same

H
-asymptotic couple
(\Gamma,\psi)
.

Lemma IV.3.1
.

Let
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}\setminus H
. Then
Z(H,\widehat{a})=Z\big(K,\widehat{a}\big)\cap H\{Y\}
.

Proof.

The inclusion “
\supseteq
” is obvious since the Newton degree of a differential polynomial
Q\in H\{Y\}^{\neq}

does not change when
H
is replaced by its algebraic closure; see [ADH, 11.1].
Conversely, let
P\in Z(H,\widehat{a})
. Then for all
{\mathfrak{v}}\in H^{\times}
and
a\in H
such that
a-\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
we have
\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}H_{+a}\geqslant 1
.
Let
{\mathfrak{v}}\in H^{\times}
and
z\in K
be such that
z-\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
.
Take
a,b\in H
such that
z=a+b\imag
. Then
a-\widehat{a},b\imag\prec{\mathfrak{v}}
and hence
\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}P_{+z}=\operatorname{ndeg}_{\prec{\mathfrak{v}}}P_{+a}\geqslant 1
, using [ADH, 11.2.7].
Thus
P\in Z\big(K,\widehat{a}\big)
.
∎

Corollary IV.3.2
.

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a slot in
H
with
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}
. Then

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is also a slot in
K
, and if

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal as a slot in
K
, then

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal as a slot in
H
.
Moreover,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole in
H
iff
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a hole in
K
, and
if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a minimal hole in
K
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a minimal hole in
H
.

Proof.

The first three claims are obvious from
\widehat{K}
being an immediate extension of
K
and the previous lemma.
Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is minimal as a hole in
K
.
Let
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widetilde{b})
be a hole in
H
;
thus
\widetilde{b}\in\widetilde{H}
where
\widetilde{H}
is an immediate asymptotic extension of
H
.
By the first part of the corollary applied to
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widetilde{b})
and
\widetilde{H}
in place of

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
\widehat{H}
, respectively,
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widetilde{b})
is also a hole in
K
. Hence
\operatorname{c}(P)\leqslant\operatorname{c}(Q)
, proving the last claim.
∎

In the next subsection we define the notion of a
split-normal
slot in
H
. Later in this section we employ the results of Sections
III.3
–
IV.2
to show, under suitable hypotheses on
H
, that minimal holes in
K
of order
\geqslant 1
give rise to a split-normal
Z
-minimal slots in
H
. (Theorem
IV.3.9
.)
We then investigate which kinds of refinements preserve split-normality, and also consider a strengthening of split-normality.

Defining split-normality

In this subsection
b
ranges over
H
and
{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
. Also,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
with
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}\setminus H
and linear part
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
. Set
w:=\operatorname{wt}(P)
, so
w\geqslant r
; if
\operatorname{order}L=r
, we set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.

Definition IV.3.3
.

We say that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
split-normal
if
\operatorname{order}L=r
, and

(SN1)

{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
;

(SN2)

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\geqslant 1}=Q+R
where
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q}
splits over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

Note that in (SN2) we do not require that
Q=(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

Lemma IV.3.4
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, and with
Q
,
R
as in
(SN2)
we have
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}-Q\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
, so
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}\sim Q
.

Proof.

We have
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}=Q+R_{1}
and
R_{1}\preccurlyeq R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
, and thus

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}-Q\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
. Now
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal because
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{>1}=R_{>1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.
∎

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}=Q+R
where
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q}
splits over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal.
Thus if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and
L
splits over
K
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal; in particular, if

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal of order
r=1
, then it is split-normal.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal, then so are
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
and
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}})
.
Note also that if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal,
then with
Q
as in (SN2) we have
{\mathfrak{v}}(L)\sim{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q})
, by Lemma
III.1.1
. If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal and
H
is
\uplambda
-free, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(L_{Q})
with
Q
as in (SN2), by
Lemmas
IV.3.4
and
III.1.23
.

Lemma IV.3.5
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal and
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
is active in
H
and
\phi>0

(
so
H^{\phi}
is still an
H
-field
)
. Then the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
is split-normal.

Proof.

We first arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Note that
L_{P^{\phi}}=L^{\phi}
has order
r
. Put
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P^{\phi}})
, and take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2). Then

{\mathfrak{v}}\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{w}}\prec^{\flat}_{\phi}1
by Lemma
III.1.20
. Moreover,
L_{Q^{\phi}}=L_{Q}^{\phi}
splits over
K^{\phi}
; see [ADH, p. 291] or Lemma
I.1.2
. By
[ADH, 11.1.4],

R^{\phi}\ \asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}R\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}P_{1}\ \asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}P_{1}^{\phi},

so
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal.
∎

Since we need to preserve
H
being an
H
-field when compositionally conjugating, we say:
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is eventually
split-normal
if there exists an active
\phi_{0}
in
H
such that
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal for all active
\phi\preccurlyeq\phi_{0}
in
H
with
\phi>0
.
We use this terminology in a similar way with “split-normal” replaced by other properties of slots of order
r\geqslant 1
in real closed
H
-fields with small derivation and asymptotic integration, such as “deep” and “deep and split-normal”.

Achieving split-normality

Assume
H
is
\upomega
-free and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a minimal hole in
K=H[\imag]
of order
r\geqslant 1
, with
{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
and
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
. Note that then
K
is
\upomega
-free by [ADH, 11.7.23],
K
is

(r-1)
-newtonian by Corollary
III.2.3
, and
K
is
r
-linearly closed by Corollary
III.2.4
.
In particular, the linear part of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
0
or splits over
K
.
If
\deg P=1
, then
r=1
by
Corollary
III.2.8
.
If
\deg P>1
, then

K
and
H
are
r
-linearly newtonian by Corollary
III.2.6
and Lemma
I.7.30
.
In particular, if
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian, then
H
is
r
-linearly newtonian.

In this subsection we let
a
range over
K
,
b
,
c
over
H
, and
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
.

Lemma IV.3.6
.

Let
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
be a hole in
H
with
\operatorname{c}(Q)\leqslant\operatorname{c}(P)
and

\widehat{b}\in\widehat{H}
. Then
\operatorname{c}(Q)=\operatorname{c}(P)
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
is minimal and remains a minimal hole in
K
. The linear part of
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})

is
0
or splits over
K
, and
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
has a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)

(
in
H
)
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Proof.

By Corollary
IV.3.2
,

(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
is a hole in
K
, and this hole in
K
is minimal with
\operatorname{c}(Q)=\operatorname{c}(P)
, since
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is minimal. By Corollary
IV.3.2
again,
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
as a hole in
H
is also minimal. Since
K
is
r
-linearly closed, the linear part of
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
is
0
or splits over
K
.
Corollary
III.3.34
gives a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)
of the minimal hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b})
in
H

such that
(Q_{+b}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)
is deep and normal, eventually. Thus the linear part of
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)
is not
0
, and
as
\operatorname{c}(Q_{+b})=\operatorname{c}(P)
, this linear part splits over
K
. Hence for active
\phi
in
H
the linear part of

(Q_{+b}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)
splits over
K^{\phi}=H^{\phi}[\imag]
. Thus
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{p}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually split-normal.
∎

Now
\widehat{a}=\widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\,\imag
with
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\widehat{H}
, and
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
. Moreover,
\widehat{b}\notin H
or
\widehat{c}\notin H
.
Since
\widehat{a}
is differentially algebraic over
H
, so is its conjugate
\widehat{b}-\widehat{c}\,\imag
, and therefore its
real and imaginary parts
\widehat{b}
and
\widehat{c}
are differentially algebraic over
H
; thus
Z(H,\widehat{b})\neq\emptyset
for
\widehat{b}\notin H
, and

Z(H,\widehat{c})\neq\emptyset
for
\widehat{c}\notin H
. More precisely:

Lemma IV.3.7
.

We have
\operatorname{trdeg}\!\big(H\langle\widehat{b}\rangle|H\big)\leqslant 2r
. If
\widehat{b}\notin H
, then
Z(H,\widehat{b})\cap H[Y]=\emptyset
, so

1\leqslant\operatorname{order}Q\leqslant 2r
for all
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
of minimal complexity.
These statements also hold for
\widehat{c}
instead of
\widehat{b}
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from
\widehat{b}\in H\langle\widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\,\imag,\widehat{b}-\widehat{c}\,\imag\rangle
. Suppose
\widehat{b}\notin H
. If
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
has minimal complexity, then [ADH, 11.4.8] yields an element
f
in a proper immediate asymptotic extension of
H
with
Q(f)=0
, so
Q\notin H[Y]
.
∎

Lemma IV.3.8
.

Suppose
\deg P=1
and
\widehat{b}\notin H
.
Let
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
be of minimal complexity; then either
\operatorname{order}{Q}=1
, or
\operatorname{order}{Q}=2
,
\deg{Q}=1
.
Let
\widehat{Q}\in H\{Y\}
be a minimal annihilator of

\widehat{b}
over
H
;
then either
\operatorname{order}{\widehat{Q}}=1
, or
\operatorname{order}{\widehat{Q}}=2
,
\deg{\widehat{Q}}=1
, and
L_{\widehat{Q}}\in H[\der]
splits over
K
.

Proof.

Recall that
r=1
by Corollary
III.2.8
. Example
I.1.7
and
Lemma
I.1.8

give a
\widetilde{Q}\in H\{Y\}
of degree
1
and order
1
or
2
such that
\widetilde{Q}(\widehat{b})=0
and
L_{\widetilde{Q}}

splits over
K
. Then
\operatorname{c}(\widetilde{Q})=(1,1,1)
or
\operatorname{c}(\widetilde{Q})=(2,1,1)
, which proves the claim about
Q
, using also Lemma
IV.3.7
.
Also,
\widetilde{Q},\widehat{Q}\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
, hence
\operatorname{c}(Q)\leqslant\operatorname{c}(\widehat{Q})\leqslant\operatorname{c}(\widetilde{Q})
. If
\operatorname{c}(\widehat{Q})=\operatorname{c}(\widetilde{Q})
, then
\widehat{Q}=a\widetilde{Q}
for some
a\in H^{\times}
. The claim about
\widehat{Q}
now follows easily.
∎

By Corollary
III.3.34
and Lemma
III.3.23
, our minimal hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
has a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
such that eventually
(P_{+a}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is deep and normal.
Moreover, as
K
is
r
-linearly closed, the
linear part of
(P_{+a}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
, for active
\phi
in
H
, splits over
K^{\phi}=H^{\phi}[\imag]
. Our main goal in this subsection is to prove analogues
of these facts for suitable
Z
-minimal slots
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
or
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
:

Theorem IV.3.9
.

If
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian, then one of the following holds:

\mathrm{(i)}

\widehat{b}\notin H
and there exists a
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
with a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and split-normal;

\mathrm{(ii)}

\widehat{c}\notin H
and there exists a
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
with a refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Lemmas
IV.3.10
,
IV.3.11
and Corollaries
IV.3.13
–
IV.3.16

below are more precise (only Corollary
IV.3.15
has
H
being
1
-linearly newtonian as a hypothesis) and together give Theorem
IV.3.9
.
We first deal with the case where
\widehat{b}
or
\widehat{c}
is in
H
:

Lemma IV.3.10
.

Suppose
\widehat{c}\in H
. Then some hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has the same complexity
as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
. Any such hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
is minimal and has a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Proof.

Let
A,B\in H\{Y\}
be such that
P_{+\widehat{c}\,\imag}(Y)=A(Y)+B(Y)\,\imag
.
Then
A(\widehat{b})=B(\widehat{b})=0
. If
A\neq 0
, then
\operatorname{c}(A)\leqslant\operatorname{c}(P)
gives that

Q:=A
has the desired property by Lemma
IV.3.6
. If
B\neq 0
, then likewise
Q:=B
has the desired property.
The rest also follows from that lemma. ∎

Thus if
\widehat{c}\in H
, we obtain a strong version of (i) in Theorem
IV.3.9
. Likewise, the next lemma gives a strong version of (ii) in Theorem
IV.3.9
if
\widehat{b}\in H
.

Lemma IV.3.11
.

Suppose
\widehat{b}\in H
. Then there is a hole
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
with the same complexity
as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
. Every such hole in
H
is minimal and has a refinement
(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and split-normal.

This follows by applying Lemma
IV.3.10
with
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
replaced by the minimal hole
\big(P_{\times\imag},{\mathfrak{m}},-\imag\widehat{a}\big)
in
K
, which has the same complexity as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.

We assume in the rest of this subsection that
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\notin H
and that

Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
has minimal complexity
.
Hence
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
, and so is every refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
.
If
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
b=\operatorname{Re}a
, then

(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
.
Conversely, if
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
and
v\big({\widehat{b}-H}\big)\subseteq v({\widehat{c}-H})
, then
Lemma
IV.1.3
yields a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
with
\operatorname{Re}a=b
. Recall from that lemma
that
v({\widehat{b}-H})\subseteq v({\widehat{c}-H})
is equivalent to
v({\widehat{a}-K})=v({\widehat{b}-H})
; in this case,

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is special iff
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is special.
Recall also that if
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is deep, then so is each of its refinements

(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b}-b)
, by Corollary
III.3.8
.

Here is a key technical fact underlying Theorem
IV.3.9
:

Proposition IV.3.12
.

Suppose the hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
is special, the slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
is normal,
and
v\big({\widehat{b}-H}\big)\subseteq v({\widehat{c}-H})
. Then some refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{b}-b})
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has the property that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is eventually split-normal.

Proof.

Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}})
,
(Q_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{b}/{\mathfrak{m}})
, respectively, we reduce to the case
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
; then
\widehat{a},\widehat{b}\prec 1
.
Since
\widehat{a}
is special over
K=H[\imag]
,

\Delta\ :=\ \big\{\delta\in\Gamma:\ \lvert\delta\rvert\in v(\widehat{a}-K)\big\}

is a convex subgroup of
\Gamma
which is cofinal in
v(\widehat{a}-K)
and hence
in
v(\widehat{b}-H)
, so
\widehat{b}
is special over
H
. Compositionally conjugate
H
,
\widehat{H}
,
K
,
\widehat{K}

by a suitable active
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
in
H^{>}
, and replace
P
,
Q
by
P^{\phi}
,
Q^{\phi}
,
to arrange
\Gamma^{\flat}\subseteq\Delta
; in particular,
\Psi\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
and
\psi(\Delta^{\neq})\subseteq\Delta
.
Multiplying
P
,
Q
by suitable elements of
H^{\times}
we also arrange that
P,Q\asymp 1
.
By Lemma
IV.3.5
it suffices to show that then
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
has a split-normal refinement

(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
, and this is what we shall do.

Note that
H
,
\widehat{H}
,
K
,
\widehat{K}
have small derivation, so the specializations
\dot{H}
,
\dot{\widehat{H}}
,
\dot{K}
,
\dot{\widehat{K}}
of
H
,
\widehat{H}
,
K
,
\widehat{K}
, respectively, by
\Delta
, are valued differential fields with small derivation. These specializations are asymptotic with
asymptotic couple
(\Delta,\psi|\Delta^{\neq})
, and of
H
-type with asymptotic integration, by [ADH, 9.4.12]; in addition they are
\operatorname{d}
-valued, by [ADH, 10.1.8].
The natural inclusions
\dot{\mathcal{O}}\to\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
,

\dot{\mathcal{O}}\to\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{H}}
,
\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{H}}\to\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{K}}
, and

\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}\to\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{K}}

induce valued differential field embeddings

\dot{H}\to\dot{K}
,
\dot{H}\to\dot{\widehat{H}}
,
\dot{\widehat{H}}\to\dot{\widehat{K}}
and
\dot{K}\to\dot{\widehat{K}}
, which we make into inclusions by
the usual identifications; see [ADH, pp. 405–406].
By Lemma
IV.1.2
and the remarks preceding it,
\dot{H}
is real closed with convex valuation ring and
\dot{K}
is an algebraic closure of
\dot{H}
. Moreover,
\dot{\widehat{H}}
is an immediate
extension of
\dot{H}
and
\dot{\widehat{K}}
is an immediate
extension of
\dot{K}
.
Denoting the image of
\imag
under the residue morphism
\dot{{\mathcal{O}}}_{\widehat{K}}\to\dot{\widehat{K}}
by the same symbol, we then have
\dot{K}=\dot{H}[\imag]
,
\dot{\widehat{K}}=\dot{\widehat{H}}[\imag]
, and
\imag\notin\dot{\widehat{H}}
. This gives the following inclusion diagram:

\textstyle{\dot{\widehat{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{\widehat{K}}=\dot{\widehat{H}}[\imag]}
\textstyle{\dot{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{K}=\dot{H}[\imag]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Now
\widehat{a}\in\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{K}}\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{K}}
and
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{H}}\subseteq\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{H}}
, and

\dot{\widehat{a}}=\dot{\widehat{b}}+\dot{\widehat{c}}\,\imag
,
\operatorname{Re}\dot{\widehat{a}}=\dot{\widehat{b}}
,
\operatorname{Im}\dot{\widehat{a}}=\dot{\widehat{c}}
.
For all
a\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
we have
v(\dot{\widehat{a}}-\dot{a})=v(\widehat{a}-a)\in\Delta
, hence

\dot{\widehat{a}}\notin\dot{K}
; likewise
{v(\widehat{b}-b)}\in\Delta

for all
b\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}
, so
\dot{\widehat{b}}\notin\dot{H}
. Moreover, for all
\delta\in\Delta
there is an
a\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
with
{v(\dot{\widehat{a}}-\dot{a})}=\delta
;
hence
\dot{\widehat{a}}
is the limit of a c-sequence in
\dot{K}
. This leads us to consider the completions
\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
and
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
of
\dot{H}
and
\dot{K}
.
By [ADH, 4.4.11] and Lemma
IV.1.1
, these yield an inclusion diagram of valued differential field extensions:

\textstyle{\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}=\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]}
\textstyle{\dot{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{K}=\dot{H}[\imag]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where
\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
is real closed with algebraic closure
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}=\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]
. These completions are
\operatorname{d}
-valued by [ADH, 9.1.6]. By Corollary
I.7.5
,

\dot{K}
and
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
are
\upomega
-free and
{(r-1)}
-newtonian; thus
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
is
r
-linearly closed by Corollary
I.7.42
. We identify the valued differential subfield
\dot{K}\big\langle\!\operatorname{Re}\dot{\widehat{a}},\operatorname{Im}\dot{\widehat{a}}\big\rangle
of
\dot{\widehat{K}}
with its image under
the embedding into
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
over
\dot{K}
from
Corollary
IV.1.7
; then
\dot{\widehat{a}}\in\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
and

\dot{\widehat{b}}=\operatorname{Re}\dot{\widehat{a}}\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
. This leads to the next inclusion diagram:

\textstyle{\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}}
\textstyle{\dot{H}\langle\dot{\widehat{b}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{K}\langle\dot{\widehat{a}}\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\dot{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

By Corollary
I.5.21
,

\dot{P}\in\dot{K}\{Y\}
is a minimal annihilator of
\dot{\widehat{a}}
over
\dot{K}
and has the same complexity as
P
. Likewise,

\dot{Q}\in\dot{H}\{Y\}
is a minimal annihilator of
\dot{\widehat{b}}
over

\dot{H}
and has the same complexity as
Q
.
Let
s:=\operatorname{order}Q=\operatorname{order}\dot{Q}
, so
1\leqslant s\leqslant 2r
by Lemma
IV.3.7
, and
the linear part
A\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}[\der]
of
\dot{Q}_{+\dot{\widehat{b}}}
has order
s
as well. By [ADH, 5.1.37] applied to
\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
,
\dot{H}
,
\dot{P}
,
\dot{Q}
,
\dot{\widehat{a}}
in the role of
K
,
F
,
P
,
S
,
f
, respectively,
A
splits over
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}=\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]
, so Lemma
I.1.4
gives a real splitting
(g_{1},\dots,g_{s})
of
A
over
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
:

A\ =\ f(\der-g_{1})\cdots(\der-g_{s}),\qquad f,g_{1},\dots,g_{s}\in\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}},\ f\neq 0.

The slot
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
in
H
is normal, so
{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+\widehat{b}}})\sim{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q})\prec^{\flat}1
by Lemma
III.1.28
, hence

{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
in
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
by Lemma
III.1.7
.
Then
Corollary
IV.2.8
gives
a,b\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}
and
b_{1},\dots,b_{s}\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
with
\dot{a},\dot{b}\neq 0
in

\dot{H}
such that for the linear part
\widetilde{A}\in\dot{H}[\der]
of
\dot{Q}_{+\dot{b}}
,

\dot{b}\ \sim\ \dot{\widehat{b}},\qquad\widetilde{A}\ \sim\ A,\qquad\operatorname{order}\widetilde{A}\ =\ s,\qquad{\mathfrak{w}}\ :=\ {\mathfrak{v}}(\widetilde{A})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}}(A),

and such that for
w:=\operatorname{wt}(Q)
and with
\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})\subseteq\Delta
:

\widetilde{A}=\widetilde{B}+\widetilde{E},\ \widetilde{B}=\dot{a}(\der-\dot{b}_{1})\cdots(\der-\dot{b}_{s})\in\dot{H}[\der],\quad\widetilde{E}\in\dot{H}[\der],\quad\widetilde{E}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}\widetilde{A},

and
(\dot{b}_{1},\dots,\dot{b}_{s})
is a real splitting of
\widetilde{B}
over
\dot{K}
. Lemma
I.1.6
shows that we can change
b_{1},\dots,b_{s}
if necessary, without changing
\dot{b}_{1},\dots,\dot{b}_{s}
, to arrange that
B:=a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{s})
lies in
\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]\subseteq H[\der]
and

(b_{1},\dots,b_{s})
is a real splitting of
B
over
K
.
Now
\widehat{b}-b\prec\widehat{b}\prec 1
, so

(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
is a refinement of the normal slot
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
. Hence

(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
is normal by Proposition
III.3.25
, so

{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b}})\prec^{\flat}1
.
By Lemma
III.1.7
we have
\dot{\mathfrak{v}}={\mathfrak{w}}
, so
\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})=\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})\subseteq\Delta
. Hence in
H[\der]
:

L_{Q_{+b}}\ =\ B+E,\quad E\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der],\ E\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}L_{Q_{+b}}.

Thus
(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
is split-normal.
∎

Recall from the beginning of this subsection that if
{\deg P>1}
,
then
K=H[\imag]
is
r
-linearly newtonian; this allows
us to remove the assumptions that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is special and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is normal in Proposition
IV.3.12
, by reducing to that case:

Corollary IV.3.13
.

Suppose
\deg P>1
and
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
.
Then
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has a special refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Proof.

By Lemmas
III.2.26
and
III.3.23
, the hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
has a quasilinear refinement
{(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)}
. (The use of Lemma
III.3.23
is because we require
{{\mathfrak{n}}\in H^{\times}}
.)
Let
b=\operatorname{Re}a
. Then, using Lemma
IV.1.3
for the second equality,

v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-K\big)\ =\ v(\widehat{a}-K)\ =\ v(\widehat{b}-H)\ =\ {v\big((\widehat{b}-b)-H\big)},

and
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
is a
Z
-minimal refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
.
We replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
and
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
, respectively, to arrange that the hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
is quasilinear.
Then
by Proposition
I.5.12
and
K
being
r
-linearly newtonian,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is special.
Hence
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is also special, so Proposition
III.3.36
gives a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
and an active
{\phi_{0}\in H^{>}}
such that
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is deep and normal.
Refinements of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
remain quasilinear, by Corollary
III.2.23
. Since
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
we have
a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
with
\operatorname{Re}a=b
.
Then by Lemma
III.2.35
the minimal hole
(P^{\phi_{0}}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
in
H^{\phi_{0}}[\imag]
is special. Now apply Proposition
IV.3.12
with
H^{\phi_{0}}
,
(P^{\phi_{0}}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
,
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})

in place of
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, respectively: it gives
{b_{0}\in H}
and a
refinement

\big((Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)\ =\ \big(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)

of

(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
, and thus a refinement
\big(Q_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
of
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
, such that
\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
is eventually split-normal.
By the remark before Proposition
IV.3.12
,
\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
is also eventually deep.
∎

Recall that
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
or
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
. The following corollary concerns the second case:

Corollary IV.3.14
.

If
\deg P>1
,
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
, and
R\in Z(H,\widehat{c})
has minimal complexity, then the
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a special refinement
(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{c}-c})
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Proof.

Apply Corollary
IV.3.13
to the minimal hole
(P_{\times\imag},{\mathfrak{m}},-\imag\widehat{a})
in
H[\imag]
.
∎

In the next two corollaries we handle the case
\deg P=1
.
Recall from Lemma
IV.3.8
that then

\operatorname{order}{Q}=1
or
\operatorname{order}{Q}=2
,
\deg{Q}=1
.
Theorem
III.3.33
gives:

Corollary IV.3.15
.

Suppose
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian and
\operatorname{order}{Q}=1
.
Then the slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Corollary IV.3.16
.

Suppose
\deg P=1
and
\operatorname{order}Q=2
,
\deg Q=1
.
Let
\widehat{Q}\in H\{Y\}
be a minimal annihilator of
\widehat{b}
over
H
.
Then
\big(\widehat{Q},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b}\big)
is a
Z
-minimal hole in
H
and has a refinement
\big({\widehat{Q}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b}\big)
such that
\big(\widehat{Q}^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b}\big)
is eventually deep and split-normal.

Proof.

By the proof of Lemma
IV.3.8
we have
\operatorname{c}(Q)=\operatorname{c}(\widehat{Q})

(hence
\big(\widehat{Q},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b}\big)
is a
Z
-minimal hole in
H
)
and
L_{\widehat{Q}}
splits over
H[\imag]
. Corollary
III.3.12
gives a refinement
\big({\widehat{Q}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b}\big)
of
\big(\widehat{Q},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b}\big)
whose linear part has Newton weight
0
and such that the slot
\big({\widehat{Q}^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b}\big)
in
H^{\phi}
is deep, eventually. Moreover, by Lemmas
III.3.17
and
III.2.31
,
\big(\widehat{Q}^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b}\big)
is normal and its linear part splits over
H^{\phi}[\imag]
, eventually. Thus
\big(\widehat{Q}^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b}\big)
is eventually deep and split-normal.
∎

This concludes the proof of Theorem
IV.3.9
.

Split-normality and refinements

We now study the behavior of split-normality under refinements. In this subsection
a
ranges over
H
and
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
range over
H^{\times}
.
Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
with
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}\setminus H
, and
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
,
w:=\operatorname{wt}(P)
.
Here is the split-normal analogue of Lemma
III.3.19
:

Lemma IV.3.17
.

Suppose
\operatorname{order}(L)=r
and
{\mathfrak{v}}
is such that
(SN1)
and
(SN2)
hold, and
{\mathfrak{v}}(L)\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal.

Proof.

Same as that of
III.3.19
, but with
R
as in (SN2) instead of
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{>1}
.
∎

Now split-normal analogues of Propositions
III.3.25
and
III.3.26
:

Lemma IV.3.18
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal. Let a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{a}-a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be given. Then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
is also split-normal.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
III.3.25
we arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
and show for
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P})
, using Lemmas
III.1.28
and
IV.3.4
, that
\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{+a}})=r
and

(P_{+a})_{1}\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}P_{1},\quad{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{+a}})\sim_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}},\quad(P_{+a})_{>1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{+a})_{1}.

Now take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2) for
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Then
P_{1}=Q+R_{1}
, and so by Lemma
III.1.29
for
A=L_{Q}
we
obtain
(P_{+a})_{1}-Q\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{+a})_{1}
, and thus

(P_{+a})_{\geqslant 1}-Q\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{+a})_{1}
.
Hence (SN2) holds with
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
and
P_{+a}
instead of
P
. Thus the slot
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
in
H
is split-normal by Lemma
IV.3.17
.
∎

Lemma IV.3.19
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal,
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}
, and
[{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{v}}]
,
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.
Then the refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})

is split-normal: if
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
P
,
Q
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
are as in
(SN2)
, then
(SN2)
holds with
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
R_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}})
in place of
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
Q
,
R
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
.

Proof.

Set
\widetilde{L}:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}
. Lemma
III.3.1
gives
\operatorname{order}(\widetilde{L})=r
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(\widetilde{L})\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
. Thus
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{>1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{1}
by Proposition
III.3.26
. Now arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
in the usual way, and
take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2) for
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Then

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{1}\ =\ (P_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ =\ Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}+(R_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},\qquad(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{>1}\ =\ (R_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{>1}\ =\ (R_{>1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}

by [ADH, 4.3], where

Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
, and
L_{Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}=L_{Q}{\mathfrak{n}}
splits over
K
.
Using [ADH, 4.3, 6.1.3] and
[{\mathfrak{n}}]\leqslant[{\mathfrak{v}}]
we obtain

(R_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ \asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{n}}R_{1}\ \preccurlyeq\ {\mathfrak{n}}R\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{n}}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}P_{1}\ \asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}\ =\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{1}.

Hence (SN2) holds for
{\mathfrak{n}},Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},R_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{v}}(\widetilde{L})
in place of
{\mathfrak{m}},Q,R,{\mathfrak{v}}
.
∎

Recall our standing assumption in this section that
H
is a real closed
H
-field. Thus
H
is
\operatorname{d}
-valued, and
for all
{\mathfrak{n}}
and
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
we have
{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\in H^{\times}
such that
({\mathfrak{n}}^{q})^{\dagger}=q{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
.
In the
rest of this section we fix such an
{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
for all
{\mathfrak{n}}
and
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
.

Now we upgrade Corollary
III.3.31
with “split-normal” instead of “normal”:

Lemma IV.3.20
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
,
(P,1,\widehat{a})
is split-normal,
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\prec 1
, and for
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P})
we have
[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]
.
Then
(P,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q},\widehat{a})
is a split-normal refinement
of
(P,1,\widehat{a})
for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0<q<1
.

Proof.

Corollary
III.3.31
gives that
(P,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q},\widehat{a})
is a normal refinement of
(P,1,\widehat{a})
for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0<q<1
.
Take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2) for
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Then
L=L_{Q}+L_{R}
where
L_{Q}
splits over
H[\imag]
and
L_{R}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}L
,
for
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.
Applying Corollary
III.1.19
to
A:=L
,
B:=L_{R}
we obtain:

L_{R}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}L{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
,
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L{\mathfrak{n}}^{q})
, for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
.

Let
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
be such that
0<q<1
,
(P,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q},\widehat{a})
is a normal refinement of
(P,1,\widehat{a})
, and
L_{R}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}L{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
, with
{\mathfrak{w}}
as above.
Then
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}})_{1}=Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}}+(R_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}}
where
Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}}
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}}}=L_{Q}{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
splits over
H[\imag]
,
and
(R_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}})_{1}
for
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}}})
. Since
(P,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q},\widehat{a})
is normal, we also have

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}})_{>1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}})_{1}
. Thus
(P,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q},\widehat{a})
is split-normal.
∎

Remark
.

We do not know if in this last lemma we can drop the assumption
[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]
.

Strengthening split-normality

In this subsection
a,b
range over
H
and
{\mathfrak{m}},{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
,
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
and weight
w:=\operatorname{wt}(P)
, so
{w\geqslant 1}
, and
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
. If

\operatorname{order}L=r
, we set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.

With an eye towards later use in connection with fixed point theorems over Hardy fields we strengthen here the concept of
split-normality; in the next subsection we show how to improve Theorem
IV.3.9
accordingly.
See the last subsection of Section
IV.2
for the notion of strong splitting.

Definition IV.3.21
.

Call

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})

almost strongly split-normal
if
\operatorname{order}L=r
,

{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
, and the following strengthening of (SN2) holds:

(SN2as)

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\geqslant 1}=Q+R
where
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q}
splits strongly over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

We say that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
strongly split-normal

if
\operatorname{order}L=r
,
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
, and the following condition is satisfied:

(SN2s)

P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=Q+R
where
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,

L_{Q}
splits strongly over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

To facilitate use of (SN2s) we observe:

Lemma IV.3.22
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly split-normal and
P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=Q+R
as in
(SN2s)
. Then
Q\sim(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
,

{\mathfrak{v}}_{Q}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q})\sim{\mathfrak{v}}
, so
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}_{Q}^{w+1}Q
.

Proof.

We have
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}=Q+R_{1}
, so

Q=(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}-R_{1}
with
R_{1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
. Now apply Lemma
III.1.1
to
A:=L
and
B:=-L_{R_{1}}
.
∎

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, then

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal and hence normal by Lemma
IV.3.4
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and
L
splits strongly over
K
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
