is almost strongly split-normal;
in particular, if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal of order
r=1
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, by Lemma
IV.2.11
.
Moreover:

Lemma IV.3.23
.

The following are equivalent:

(i)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly split-normal;

(ii)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal and strictly normal;

(iii)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal and
P(0)\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

Proof.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly split-normal, and
let
Q
,
R
be as in (SN2s). Then
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\geqslant 1}=Q+R_{\geqslant 1}
,
L_{Q}
splits strongly over
K
, and
R_{\geqslant 1}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.
Hence
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, and thus normal.
Also
P(0)=R(0)\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
, so
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal.
This shows (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii), and (ii)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is clear.
For (iii)
\Rightarrow
(i) suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal and

P(0)\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
. Take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2as). Then
P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=Q+\widetilde{R}
where

\widetilde{R}:=P(0)+R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
.
Thus
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly split-normal.
∎

Corollary IV.3.24
.

If
L
splits strongly over
K
, then

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is strongly split-normal }\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is strictly normal.}

The following diagram summarizes some implications between these variants of normality, for slots
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H

of order
r\geqslant 1
:

strongly split-normal
almost strongly split-normal
split-normal
strictly normal
normal

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, then so are

(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
and
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}})
, and likewise with “strongly”
in place of “almost strongly”.

Here is a version of Lemma
IV.3.18
for (almost) strong split-normality:

Lemma IV.3.25
.

Suppose
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, then so is
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly split-normal,
Z
-minimal, and

\widehat{a}-a\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{r+w+1}{\mathfrak{m}}
, then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
is strongly split-normal.

Proof.

The first part follows from Lemma
IV.3.18
and its proof.
In combination with Lemmas
III.3.42
and
IV.3.23
, this also yields the second part.
∎

Lemma IV.3.26
.

Suppose that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal and
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Then for all sufficiently small
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
, any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
yields an almost strongly split-normal refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.

Proof.

We arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
, so
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}1
. Take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2) with
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
, and take
q_{0}\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
such that
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}^{q_{0}}\prec 1
. By Lemma
IV.2.13
we
can decrease
q_{0}
so that for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0<q\leqslant q_{0}
and any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}^{q}
,
L_{Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}=L_{Q}{\mathfrak{n}}
splits strongly over
K
.
Suppose
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
,
0<q\leqslant q_{0}
, and
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}^{q}
.
Then
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is an almost strongly split-normal refinement of
(P,1,\widehat{a})
, by Lemma
IV.3.19
.
∎

Corollary IV.3.27
.

Suppose that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, deep, and split-normal. Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has a refinement which is deep and
almost strongly split-normal.

Proof.

Lemma
III.3.13
gives
a
such that
\widehat{a}-a\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
. By Corollary
III.3.8
, the refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
of

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is deep with
{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}})\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
, and by Lemma
IV.3.18
it is also
split-normal.
Now apply Lemma
IV.3.26
to
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
in place of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and again use Corollary
III.3.8
.
∎

We now turn to the behavior of these properties under compositional conjugation.

Lemma IV.3.28
.

Let
\phi
be active in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, then so is
the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
. Likewise with “strongly”
in place of “almost strongly”.

Proof.

We arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
,
assume
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal, and take
Q
,
R
as in (SN2as).
The proof of Lemma
IV.3.5
shows that with
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P^{\phi}})

we have
{\mathfrak{w}}\prec^{\flat}_{\phi}1
and
(P^{\phi})_{\geqslant 1}=Q^{\phi}+R^{\phi}
where

Q^{\phi}\in H^{\phi}\{Y\}
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q^{\phi}}
splits over
H^{\phi}[\imag]
, and

R^{\phi}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}(P^{\phi})_{1}
. By Lemma
IV.2.12
,
L_{Q^{\phi}}=L_{Q}^{\phi}
even splits strongly over
H[\imag]
.
Hence
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal.
The rest follows from Lemma
IV.3.23
and
the fact that if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal, then so is
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

If
H
is
\upomega
-free and
r
-linearly newtonian, then by Corollary
III.3.48
, every
Z
-minimal slot in
H
of order
r

has a refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
such that the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
is eventually deep and strictly normal.
Corollary
IV.3.30
of the next lemma
is a variant of this fact for strong split-normality.

Lemma IV.3.29
.

Assume
H
is
\upomega
-free and
r
-linearly newtonian, and every
{A\in H[\der]}
of order
r
splits over
K
.
Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal. Then there is a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
\phi
in
H

with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
such that
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is deep and strictly normal, and its linear part splits strongly over
K^{\phi}

(
so
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is strongly split-normal by Corollary
IV.3.24
)
.

Proof.

For any active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
we may replace
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, respectively. We may
also replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by any of its refinements.
Now Theorem
III.3.33
gives a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
\phi
in
H
such that
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
and
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is deep and normal.
Replacing
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
, respectively,
we thus arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
itself is deep and normal. We show that then the lemma holds with
\phi=1
.
For this we first replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by a suitable refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{a}-a})
to arrange by
Corollary
III.3.47
that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal and
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Now
L
splits over
K
, so by
Corollary
IV.2.14
,
for sufficiently small
{q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}}
, any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp|{\mathfrak{v}}|^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
gives a refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
whose linear part
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}
has order
r
and splits strongly over
K
.
For each such
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is deep by Corollary
III.3.8
, and for some such
{\mathfrak{n}}
,

(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is also strictly normal, by Remark
III.3.45
.
∎

The previous lemma in combination with Lemma
IV.3.28
yields:

Corollary IV.3.30
.

With the same assumptions on
H
,
K
as in Lemma
IV.3.29
, every
Z
-minimal slot in
H
of order
r
has a refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
such that

(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.

For
r=1
the splitting assumption is automatically satisfied (and this is the case most relevant later).
We do not know whether “every

A\in H[\der]^{\neq}
of order
\leqslant r
splits over
K
” is strictly weaker than “
K
is
r
-linearly closed”.

Achieving strong split-normality

We make the same assumptions as in the subsection
Achieving split-normality
:
H
is
\upomega
-free and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a minimal hole in
K=H[\imag]
of order
r\geqslant 1
, with
{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
and
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
.
Recall that
K
is also

\upomega
-free [ADH, 11.7.23].
We have

\widehat{a}\ =\ \widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\,\imag,\qquad\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\widehat{H}.

We let
a
range over
K
,
b
,
c
over
H
, and
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
.
In connection with the next two lemmas we note that given
an active
\phi
in
H
with
{0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1}
,
if

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal (strictly normal, respectively), then
so is
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, by Lemma
III.3.20
(Lemma
III.3.40
, respectively);
moreover, if the linear part of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
splits strongly over
K
, then the linear part of
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
splits strongly over
K^{\phi}=H^{\phi}[\imag]
,
by Lemma
IV.2.12
. Here is a “complex” version of Lemma
IV.3.29
, with a similar proof:

Lemma IV.3.31
.

For some refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
,
the hole
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
in
K^{\phi}
is deep and normal, its linear part splits
strongly over
K^{\phi}
, and it is moreover strictly normal if
\deg P>1
.

Proof.

For any active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1

we may replace

H
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
and the minimal hole
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K^{\phi}
. We may
also replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by any of its refinements
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
.
As noted before Theorem
IV.3.9
, Corollary
III.3.34
and Lemma
III.3.23
give a refinement

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
{\phi}
in
H

with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
such that
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is deep and normal.
Replacing
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
, respectively,
we thus arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
itself is deep and normal. We show that then the lemma holds with
\phi=1
.

Set
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
and
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.
Lemma
III.3.13
gives
a
with
{\widehat{a}-a\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}}
.
If
{\deg P>1}
, then
K
is
r
-linearly newtonian and we use Corollary
III.3.16
to take
a
such that
even
\widehat{a}-a\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+2}{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
, we thus arrange by Lemma
III.3.7
and
Proposition
III.3.25
that
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
, and also by Lemma
III.3.46
that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal if
\deg P>1
.
Now
L
splits over
K
, since
K
is
r
-linearly closed by Corollary
III.2.4
.
Then by Corollary
IV.2.14
, for sufficiently small
{q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}}
, any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp|{\mathfrak{v}}|^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
gives a refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
whose linear part
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}
splits strongly over
K
.
For such
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is deep by Lemma
III.3.7
and normal by Proposition
III.3.26
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal, then for some such
{\mathfrak{n}}
,

(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is also strictly normal, thanks to Lemma
III.3.44
.
∎

The following version of Lemma
IV.3.31
also encompasses linear
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
:

Lemma IV.3.32
.

Suppose
\der K=K
and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Then there is a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
such that
the hole
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
in
K^{\phi}
is deep and strictly normal, and its linear part splits
strongly over
K^{\phi}
.

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
IV.3.31
we need only consider the case
\deg P=1
. Then we have
r=1
by Corollary
III.2.8
. (See now the remark following this proof.)
As in the proof of Lemma
IV.3.31
we may replace

H
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for any active
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
in
H^{>}
by
H^{\phi}
and
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, and
also
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by any of its refinements
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
. Recall here that
{\mathfrak{n}}\in H^{\times}
.
Hence using a remark preceding Lemma
III.3.39
and Corollary
III.5.17

we arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal, and thus balanced and deep. We show that then the lemma holds with
\phi=1
.

Set
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
,
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.
Lemmas
III.5.9
and
III.5.10
yield an
a
with
\widehat{a}-a\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}^{4}{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
arranges that
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
, by Lemmas
III.3.7
and
III.3.41
. As in the proof of Lemma
IV.3.31
, for sufficiently small
{q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}}
, any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp|{\mathfrak{v}}|^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
now gives a strictly normal and deep refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
whose linear part splits strongly over
K
.
∎

Remark
.

Suppose we replace our standing assumption that
H
is
\upomega
-free and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a minimal hole in
K
by the assumption that
H
is
\uplambda
-free and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
K
of order and degree
1
(so
K
is
\uplambda
-free by [ADH, 11.6.8] and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal).
Then Lemma
IV.3.32
goes through with “hole” replaced by “slot”. Its proof also goes through with the references to Lemmas
III.3.7
and
III.3.41
replaced by references to Corollary
III.3.8
and Lemma
III.3.42
. The end of that proof refers to the end of the proof of Lemma
IV.3.31
, and there one should replace Proposition
III.3.26
by Corollary
III.3.27
, and Lemma
III.3.44
by Remark
III.3.45
.

In the remainder of this subsection we prove the following variant of Theorem
IV.3.9
:

Theorem IV.3.33
.

If
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian, then one of the following holds:

(i)

\widehat{b}\notin H
and there exists a
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
with a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and almost strongly split-normal;

(ii)

\widehat{c}\notin H
and there exists a
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
with a refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and almost strongly split-normal.

Moreover, if
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian and either
\deg P>1
, or
\widehat{b}\notin H
and
Z(H,\widehat{b})
contains an element of order
1
, or
\widehat{c}\notin H
and
Z(H,\widehat{c})
contains an element of order
1
,
then
(i)
holds with “almost” omitted, or
(ii)
holds with “almost” omitted.

Towards the proof of this theorem we first show:

Lemma IV.3.34
.

Suppose
\widehat{b}\notin H
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
with a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and split-normal.
Then
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and almost strongly split-normal.

Proof.

Let
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
be a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
and
let
\phi_{0}
be active in
H
such that
0<\phi_{0}\preccurlyeq 1

and
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is deep and split-normal.
Then Corollary
IV.3.27

yields a refinement
\big((Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)
of
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
which is deep and
almost strongly split-normal. Hence

\big((Q_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)\ =\ \big(Q_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})\big)

is a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, and
\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})\big)
is eventually deep and almost strongly split-normal by Lemma
IV.3.28
.
∎

Likewise:

Lemma IV.3.35
.

Suppose
\widehat{c}\notin H
, and
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
with a refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and split-normal.
Then
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
has a refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and almost strongly split-normal.

Theorem
IV.3.9
and the two lemmas above give the first part of Theorem
IV.3.33
.
We break up the proof of the “moreover” part into several cases, along the lines of the proof of Theorem
IV.3.9
. We begin with the case where
\widehat{b}\in H
or
\widehat{c}\in H
.

Lemma IV.3.36
.

Suppose
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian,
\widehat{b}\notin H
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
of order
r
, and some
refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and split-normal.
Then
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
with
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
eventually deep and strongly split-normal.

Proof.

Lemma
IV.3.34
gives a refinement

(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
with
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
eventually deep and almost strongly split-normal.
We upgrade this to “strongly split-normal” as follows: Take active
\phi_{0}
in
H
with
0<\phi_{0}\preccurlyeq 1

such that the slot
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
in
H^{\phi_{0}}
is deep and almost strongly split-normal.
Now
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian, hence
r
-linearly newtonian. Therefore
Corollary
III.3.47
yields a deep and strictly normal refinement

\big((Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)

of
\big(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b\big)
.
By
Lemma
IV.3.25
, this refinement is still almost strongly split-normal, thus strongly split-normal by
Lemma
IV.3.23
.
Then by Lemma
IV.3.28
,

\big(Q_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)

is a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
such that
\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})\big)
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.
∎

Lemmas
IV.3.10
and
IV.3.36
give the following:

Corollary IV.3.37
.

Suppose
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian and
\widehat{c}\in H
. Then there is a hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
of the same complexity
as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
. Every such hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
is minimal and has a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.

Just as Lemma
IV.3.10
gave rise to Lemma
IV.3.11
, Corollary
IV.3.37
leads to:

Corollary IV.3.38
.

Suppose
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian and
\widehat{b}\in H
. Then there is a hole
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
of the same complexity
as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
. Every such hole in
H
is minimal and has a refinement
(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.

In the following two lemmas we assume that
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\notin H
. Let
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
be of minimal complexity, so
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
, as is each of its refinements.
The next lemma strengthens Corollary
IV.3.13
:

Lemma IV.3.39
.

Suppose
\deg P>1
and
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
. Then

(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.

Proof.

Corollary
IV.3.13
and Lemma
IV.3.34
give a refinement

(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
and an active
\phi_{0}
in
H
with
0<\phi_{0}\preccurlyeq 1

such that the slot
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
in
H^{\phi_{0}}
is deep and almost strongly split-normal. From
\deg P>1
we obtain that
H
is
r
-linearly newtonian. Now argue as in the proof of Lemma
IV.3.36
.
∎

Similarly we obtain a strengthening of Corollary
IV.3.14
, using that corollary and Lemma
IV.3.35
in place of Corollary
IV.3.13
and Lemma
IV.3.34
in the proof:

Lemma IV.3.40
.

If
\deg P>1
,
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
, and
R\in Z(H,\widehat{c})
has minimal complexity, then the
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a refinement
(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{c}-c})
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.

We now prove the “moreover” part of Theorem
IV.3.33
.
Thus, suppose
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian.
If
\widehat{b}\in H
, then
\widehat{c}\notin H
and
Corollary
IV.3.38
yields a strong version of (ii)
with “almost” omitted. Likewise, if
\widehat{c}\in H
, then
\widehat{b}\notin H
and Corollary
IV.3.37
yields a strong version of (i),
with “almost” omitted.
In the rest of the proof we assume
{\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\notin H}
.
By Lemma
IV.1.3
we have
v({\widehat{b}-H})\subseteq v({\widehat{c}-H})
or
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v({\widehat{b}-H})
,
and thus Lemmas
IV.3.39
and
IV.3.40
take care of the case
{\deg P>1}
.
If
Z(H,\widehat{b})
contains an element of order
1
, and
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
has minimal complexity, then
\operatorname{order}Q=1
by Lemma
IV.3.7
, so Corollary
IV.3.30
and the remark following it
yield (i) with “almost” omitted. Likewise, if
Z(H,\widehat{c})
contains an element of order
1
, then (ii) holds with “almost” omitted.
∎

Revisiting newtonianity

We now use our results about isolated holes and split-normality to obtain
with Corollary
IV.3.41
a sharper first-order characterization of newtonianity than provided by our definition of this notion in [ADH].

Let
H
be a real closed
H
-field with small derivation and asymptotic integration
.
Let
P\in H\{Y\}^{\neq}
have order
r\geqslant 1
and weight
w
. Just for the next corollary, call
P

strongly split-normal
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

L_{P}
has order
r
and
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P})\prec^{\flat}1
; and

(2)

P=Q+R
where
Q\in H\{Y\}
is homogeneous of degree
1
,
\operatorname{order}Q=r
,

L_{Q}
splits strongly over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}P_{1}
.

Call
P

eventually deep and strongly split-normal
if
\operatorname{ndeg}S_{P}=0
and for all small enough active
\phi>0
in
H
,
the differential polynomial
P^{\phi}\in H^{\phi}\{Y\}
is strongly split-normal with respect to
H^{\phi}
.
Note:
\operatorname{ndeg}P=\operatorname{nmul}P=1
for such
P
.

Corollary IV.3.41
.

Assume
H
is
\upomega
-free. Then the following are equivalent:

(i)

H
is newtonian;

(ii)

H[\imag]
is
1
-linearly newtonian and
every eventually deep and strongly split-normal
P
in
H\{Y\}^{\neq}
of order
\geqslant 1
has
a zero
y\prec 1
in
H
.

Proof.

The direction (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii) is clear from (
0.12
) and [ADH, 14.2.11]. For (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i),
suppose
H
is not newtonian and
H[\imag]
is
1
-linearly newtonian.
By Proposition
III.4.9
it is enough to show that then
H
has an isolated hole
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
such that for all small enough active
\phi>0
in
H
the hole
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H^{\phi}
is deep and
strongly split-normal. We set
K:=H[\imag]
and let
b
,
c
range over
H
.

Lemma
III.2.1
and subsequent remarks
give a minimal hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
, where
{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
. Then
\deg P>1
by Corollary
III.2.8
.
By Lemma
IV.2.15
we arrange that
{\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}:=\widehat{H}[\imag]}
where
\widehat{H}
is an immediate
\upomega
-free newtonian
H
-field extension of
H
, so

\widehat{a}=\widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\imag
with
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\widehat{H}
.
Then
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
or
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
by Lemma
IV.1.3
; we assume

v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
. (The other case is similar.)
The equivalence (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii) of that lemma then gives
\widehat{b}\notin H
.

Take
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
of minimal complexity. Then
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal slot
in
H
, of positive order by Lemma
IV.3.7
.
Given any refinement

(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, Lemma
IV.1.3
gives
c
with
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-b)
for
a:=b+c\imag
, and we may then replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
and
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
, respectively,
whenever convenient.
Likewise, for any active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
, we can also replace
H
,
K
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by
H^{\phi}
,
K^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
.

Suppose now that
\widehat{c}\notin H
.
Use Corollary
IV.3.13
to arrange that
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is normal.
Next, use Proposition
III.4.6
to arrange that
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is isolated, but possibly no longer normal.
Being isolated persists under refinement, so we can
use Lemma
IV.3.39
to arrange that
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal. With Lemma
III.2.14
, changing
\widehat{b}
if necessary, we arrange that

(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is an isolated hole in
H
, not just an isolated slot in
H
, thus achieving our goal.

Finally, suppose that
\widehat{c}\in H
. Then use Corollary
IV.3.37
and Proposition
III.4.6
to choose

Q
such that
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a minimal and isolated hole in
H
with the property that

(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is eventually deep and strongly split-normal.
∎

IV.4.
Ultimate Slots

In this section
H
is a Liouville closed
H
-field
with small derivation,
\widehat{H}
is an immediate asymptotic extension
of
H
, and
\imag
is an element of an asymptotic extension of
\widehat{H}

with
\imag^{2}=-1
.

Then
\widehat{H}
is an
H
-field,
\imag\notin\widehat{H}
,

K:=H[\imag]
is an algebraic closure of
H
, and
\widehat{K}:=\widehat{H}[\imag]
is an immediate
\operatorname{d}
-valued extension of
K
. (See the beginning of Section
IV.3
.) Let
C
be the constant field of
H
, let
\mathcal{O}
denote the valuation ring of
H
and
\Gamma
its value group. Accordingly, the constant field of
K
is
C_{K}=C[\imag]
and the valuation ring of
K
is
\mathcal{O}_{K}=\mathcal{O}+\mathcal{O}\imag
.
Let
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
{\mathfrak{w}}
range over
H^{\times}
and
\phi
over the elements of
H^{>}
which are active in
H
(and hence in
K
).

In Section
I.2
we introduced

W\ :=\ \big\{\!\operatorname{wr}(a,b):\ a,b\in H,\ a^{2}+b^{2}=1\big\}.

Note that
W
is a subspace of the

{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
H
, because
W\imag=S^{\dagger}
where

S\ :=\ \{a+b\imag:\ a,b\in H,\ a^{2}+b^{2}=1\}

is a divisible subgroup of
K^{\times}
. We have
K^{\dagger}=H+W\imag
by Lemma
I.2.4
.
Thus there exists a complement
\Lambda
of the subspace
K^{\dagger}
of
K
such that
\Lambda\subseteq H\imag
, and in this section we fix such
\Lambda
and let
\lambda
range over
\Lambda
.
Let
{\operatorname{U}}=K\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big]
be the universal exponential extension of
K
defined in Section
II.2
.

For
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
we have its set
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\subseteq\Gamma
of ultimate exceptional values,
which a-priori might depend on our choice of
\Lambda
. We now make good on a promise from Section
II.5
by showing under the mild assumption
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and with our restriction
\Lambda\subseteq H\imag
there is no such dependence:

Corollary IV.4.1
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Then for
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
, the status of
A
being terminal does not depend on the choice of
\Lambda
, and the set
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
of ultimate exceptional values of
A
also does not depend on this choice.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda^{*}\subseteq H\imag
also be a complement of
K^{\dagger}
. Let
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{*}
be the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear bijection
\Lambda\to\Lambda^{*}
with
\lambda-\lambda^{*}\in W\imag
for all
\lambda
. Then by Lemmas
I.2.8
and
I.2.13
,

\lambda-\lambda^{*}\in\operatorname{I}(H)\imag\ \subseteq\ \operatorname{I}(K)\ \subseteq\ (\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\times})^{\dagger}

for all
\lambda
. Now use Lemma
II.5.6
and Corollary
II.5.7
.
∎

Corollary IV.4.2
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Let
A=\der-g\in K[\der]
where
g\in K
and let
\mathfrak{g}\in H^{\times}
be such that
\mathfrak{g}^{\dagger}=\operatorname{Re}g
. Then

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\ =\ v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\ =\ \{v\mathfrak{g}\}.

In particular, if
\operatorname{Re}g\in\operatorname{I}(H)
, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)=\{0\}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in K^{\times}
and
\lambda
be such that
g=f^{\dagger}+\lambda
. Then

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\ =\ v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)\ =\ \{vf\}

by Lemma
II.5.12
and its proof.
Recall that
K^{\dagger}=H+\operatorname{I}(H)\imag
by Lemma
I.2.13
and remarks preceding it, so
g\in K^{\dagger}
iff
\operatorname{Im}g\in\operatorname{I}(H)
.
Consider first the case
g\notin K^{\dagger}
. Then by Corollary
IV.4.1
we can change

\Lambda
if necessary to arrange
\lambda:=(\operatorname{Im}g)\imag\in\Lambda
so that we can take
f:=\mathfrak{g}
in the above.
Now suppose
g\in K^{\dagger}
. Then
g=(\mathfrak{g}h)^{\dagger}
where
h\in K^{\times}
,
h^{\dagger}=(\operatorname{Im}g)\imag
. Then
we can take
f:=\mathfrak{g}h
,
\lambda:=0
, and we have
h\asymp 1
since
h^{\dagger}\in\operatorname{I}(H)\imag\subseteq\operatorname{I}(K)
.
∎

Corollary IV.4.3
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, and let
F
be a Liouville closed
H
-field extension of
H
, and
L:=F[\imag]
.
Then the subspace
L^{\dagger}
of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
L
has a
complement
\Lambda_{L}
with
\Lambda\subseteq\Lambda_{L}\subseteq F\imag
. For any such
\Lambda_{L}
and

A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
we have
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}(A_{\lambda})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{e}}_{L}(A_{\lambda})\cap\Gamma
for all
\lambda
, and thus

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\subseteq{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{L}(A)
, where
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}_{L}(A)
is the
set of ultimate exceptional values of
A\in L[\der]^{\neq}
with respect to
\Lambda_{L}
.

Proof.

By the remarks at the beginning of this subsection applied to
F
,
L
in place of
H
,
K
we have

L^{\dagger}=F+W_{F}\imag
where

W_{F}
is a subspace of the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-linear space
F
. Also
K^{\dagger}=H+\operatorname{I}(H)\imag
by Lemma
I.2.13
, and
L^{\dagger}\cap K=K^{\dagger}
by Lemma
II.5.22
. This yields a
complement
\Lambda_{L}
of
L^{\dagger}
in
L
with

\Lambda\subseteq\Lambda_{L}\subseteq F\imag
.
Since
H
is Liouville closed and hence
\uplambda
-free by [ADH, 11.6.2], its algebraic closure
K
is
\uplambda
-free by [ADH, 11.6.8]. Now the rest follows from remarks preceding Lemma
II.5.10
.
∎

Given
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
, let
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A^{\phi})
be the set of ultimate exceptional values
of the linear differential operator
A^{\phi}\in K^{\phi}[\derdelta]
,
\derdelta=\phi^{-1}\der
, with respect to
\Lambda^{\phi}=\phi^{-1}\Lambda
.
We summarize some properties of ultimate exceptional values used later in this section:

Lemma IV.4.4
.

Let
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
have order
r
. Then for all
b\in K^{\times}
and all
\phi
,

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(bA)\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A),\quad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(Ab)\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)-vb,\quad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A^{\phi})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A).

Moreover, if
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, then:

(i)

\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\rvert\leqslant r
;

(ii)

\dim_{C[\imag]}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=r\ \Longrightarrow\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)=v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)
;

(iii)

under the assumption that
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
and
B\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{r+1}A
where
B\in K[\der]
has order
\leqslant r
,
we have
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A+B)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
;

(iv)

for
r=1
we have
\lvert{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\rvert=1
and
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)=v_{\operatorname{g}}(\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}^{\neq}A)
.

Proof.

For the displayed equalities, see Remark
II.5.8
. Now assume
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Then
K^{\dagger}=H+\operatorname{I}(H)\imag
,
so (i) and (ii) follow from Proposition
II.5.24

and (iii) from Proposition
III.1.27
. Corollary
IV.4.2
yields (iv).
∎

Recall from Lemma
I.2.9
that if
K
is
1
-linearly newtonian, then
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Then

K^{\dagger}=H+\operatorname{I}(H)\imag
, so our
\Lambda
has the form
\Lambda_{H}\imag
with
\Lambda_{H}
a complement of
\operatorname{I}(H)
in
H
. Conversely, any complement
\Lambda_{H}
of
\operatorname{I}(H)
in
H
yields a complement
\Lambda=\Lambda_{H}\imag
of
K^{\dagger}
in
K
with
\Lambda\subseteq H\imag
. Now
\operatorname{I}(H)
is a
C
-linear subspace of
H
, so

\operatorname{I}(H)
has a complement
\Lambda_{H}
in
H
that is a
C
-linear subspace of
H
, and then
\Lambda:=\Lambda_{H}\imag
is also a
C
-linear subspace of
K
.

Lemma IV.4.5
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
g\in K
,
g-\lambda\in K^{\dagger}
. Then

\operatorname{Im}g\in\operatorname{I}(H)\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \lambda=0,\qquad\operatorname{Im}g\notin\operatorname{I}(H)\ \Longrightarrow\ \lambda\sim(\operatorname{Im}g)\imag.

Proof.

Recall that
\Lambda=\Lambda_{H}\imag
where
\Lambda_{H}
is a complement of
\operatorname{I}(H)
in
H
, so
\lambda=\lambda_{H}\imag
where
\lambda_{H}\in\Lambda_{H}
. Also,
K^{\dagger}=H\oplus\operatorname{I}(H)\imag
, hence
\operatorname{Im}(g)-\lambda_{H}\in\operatorname{I}(H)
; this proves the displayed equivalence. Suppose
\operatorname{Im}g\notin\operatorname{I}(H)
; since
\operatorname{I}(H)
is an
\mathcal{O}_{H}
-submodule of
H
and
\lambda_{H}\notin\operatorname{I}(H)
, we then have
\operatorname{Im}(g)-\lambda_{H}\prec\lambda_{H}
,
so
\lambda=\lambda_{H}\imag\sim\operatorname{Im}(g)\imag
.
∎

Corollary IV.4.6
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
,
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
has order
r
,
\dim_{C[\imag]}\ker_{{\operatorname{U}}}A=r
, and

\lambda
is an eigenvalue of
A
with respect to
\Lambda
. Then
\lambda\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-1}
.

Proof.

Take
f\neq 0
and
g_{1},\dots,g_{r}
in
K
with
A=f(\der-g_{1})\cdots(\der-g_{r})
.
By Corollary
III.1.6
we have
g_{1},\dots,g_{r}\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{-1}
, and
so Corollary
II.4.6
gives
j\in\{1,\dots,r\}
with
g_{j}-\lambda\in K^{\dagger}
. Now use Lemma
IV.4.5
.
∎

Ultimate slots in
H

In this subsection
a
,
b
range over
H
.

Also,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
, where
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}\setminus H
. Recall that
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}=L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}}
, so
if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, then
L_{P}
has order
r
.

Corollary IV.4.7
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and the slot
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is split-normal with linear part
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
.
Then with
Q
and
R
as in
(SN2)

we have
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L)={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{Q})
.

This follows from Lemmas
IV.3.4
and
IV.4.4
(iii). In a similar vein we have an analogue of Lemma
III.3.24
:

Lemma IV.4.8
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
L_{P}
and
L_{P_{+a}}
have order
r
, and if
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})
.

Proof.

We have
L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}=L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}}
and
L_{P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}},+a/{\mathfrak{m}}}}=L_{P_{+a}}{\mathfrak{m}}
. The slot
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}},1,\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}})
in
H
is normal and
a/{\mathfrak{m}}\prec 1
. Lemma
III.1.29
applied to
\widehat{H}
,
P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
in place of
K
,
P
,
a
, respectively, gives:
L_{P}
and
L_{P_{+a}}
have order
r
, and

L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}}-L_{P_{+a}}{\mathfrak{m}}\ =\ L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}-L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}},+a/{\mathfrak{m}}}}\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}\ {\mathfrak{v}}^{r+1}L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}}

where
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}})\prec^{\flat}1
by (N1). Suppose now that
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. Then

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}})+v({\mathfrak{m}})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}}{\mathfrak{m}})+v({\mathfrak{m}})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})

by Lemma
IV.4.4
(iii).
∎

The notion introduced below is modeled on that of “isolated slot” (Definition
III.4.1
):

Definition IV.4.9
.

Call
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})

ultimate
if for all
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
,

\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{+a}})=r\ \text{ and }\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)\ <\ v(\widehat{a}-a);

equivalently, for all
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
:
\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{+a}})=r
and whenever

{\mathfrak{w}}\preccurlyeq\widehat{a}-a
is such that
v({\mathfrak{w}})\in{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})
, then

{\mathfrak{w}}\prec\widehat{a}-b
for all
b
. (Thus if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate, then it is isolated.)

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate, then so is every equivalent slot in
H
and
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
, as well as the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
(by Lemma
IV.4.4
). The proofs of the next two lemma are like those of their “isolated” versions, Lemmas
III.4.2
and
III.4.3
:

Lemma IV.4.10
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate, then so is any of its refinements.

Lemma IV.4.11
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate, then so is any of its multiplicative conjugates.

The ultimate condition is most useful in combination with other properties:

Lemma IV.4.12
.

If
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, then

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is ultimate}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)\leqslant v{\mathfrak{m}}.

Proof.

Use Lemma
IV.4.8
and the equivalence
\widehat{a}-a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}\Leftrightarrow a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
.
∎

The “ultimate” version of Lemma
III.4.5
has the same proof:

Lemma IV.4.13
.

If
\deg P=1
, then

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is ultimate}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)\leqslant v{\mathfrak{m}}.

The next proposition is the “ultimate” version of Proposition
III.4.6
:

Proposition IV.4.14
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
,
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal.
Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has an ultimate refinement.

Proof.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is not already ultimate.
Then Lemma
IV.4.12
gives
\gamma
with

\gamma\in{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-H),\quad\gamma>v{\mathfrak{m}}.

Lemma
IV.4.4
(i) gives
|{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})|\leqslant r
,
so we can take

\gamma\ :=\ \max{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-H),

and then
\gamma>v{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Take
a
and
{\mathfrak{n}}
with
v(\widehat{a}-a)>\gamma=v({\mathfrak{n}})
;
then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Let
b\prec{\mathfrak{n}}
; then

a+b\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
, so by Lemma
IV.4.8
,

\operatorname{order}(L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}})\ =\ r,\qquad{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}})\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P}).

Also
v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-b\big)>\gamma
, hence

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}\big(L_{(P_{+a})_{+b}}\big)\cap v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-H\big)\ =\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)\ \leqslant\ \gamma\ <\ v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-b\big).

Thus
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is ultimate.
∎

Remark IV.4.15
.

Proposition
IV.4.14
goes through if instead of assuming that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, we assume
that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is linear. (Same argument, using Lemma
IV.4.13
in place of Lemma
IV.4.12
.)

Finally, here is a consequence of Corollaries
II.5.13
,
IV.4.2
, and Lemma
IV.4.12
, where we recall that
\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}})=\operatorname{order}(L_{P}{\mathfrak{m}})=\operatorname{order}(L_{P})
:

Corollary IV.4.16
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal of order
r=1
.
Then
L_{P}=f(\der-g)
with
f\in H^{\times}
,
g\in H
, and for
\mathfrak{g}\in H^{\times}
with
\mathfrak{g}^{\dagger}=g
we have:

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is ultimate}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is isolated}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad.\text{$\mathfrak{g}\succcurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}$ or $\mathfrak{g}\prec\widehat{a}-H$.}

(
In particular, if
g\in\operatorname{I}(H)
and
{\mathfrak{m}}\preccurlyeq 1
,
then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate.
)

Ultimate slots in
K

In this subsection,
a
,
b
range over
K=H[\imag]
.

Also
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
, where
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
.
Lemma
IV.4.8
goes through in this setting, with
H
in the proof replaced by
K
:

Lemma IV.4.17
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, and
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then
L_{P}
and
L_{P_{+a}}
have order
r
, and if
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P})={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})
.

We adapt Definition
IV.4.9
to slots in
K
:
call
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})

ultimate
if for all
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
we have
\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{+a}})=r
and

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})\cap v(\widehat{a}-K)<v(\widehat{a}-a)
. If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate, then it is isolated.
Moreover, if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate, then so is
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
as well as
the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K^{\phi}
.
Lemmas
IV.4.10
and
IV.4.11
go through in the present context, and so do Lemmas
IV.4.12
and
IV.4.13
with
H
replaced by
K
. The analogue of Proposition
IV.4.14

follows likewise:

Proposition IV.4.18
.

If
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has an ultimate refinement.

Remark IV.4.19
.

Proposition
IV.4.18
also holds if instead of assuming that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal, we assume
that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is linear.

Corollary
IV.4.2
and the
K
-versions of Lemmas
IV.4.12
and
IV.4.13
yield:

Corollary IV.4.20
.

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
,
r=1
, and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal or linear. Then

L_{P}=f(\der-g)
with
f\in K^{\times},g\in K
, and for
\mathfrak{g}\in H^{\times}
with

\mathfrak{g}^{\dagger}=\operatorname{Re}g
we have:

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is ultimate}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$\mathfrak{g}\succcurlyeq{\mathfrak{m}}$ or
$\mathfrak{g}\prec\widehat{a}-K$.}

(
In particular, if
\operatorname{Re}g\in\operatorname{I}(H)
and
{\mathfrak{m}}\preccurlyeq 1
,
then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate.
)

Using the norm to characterize being ultimate

We use here the “norm”
\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert
on
{\operatorname{U}}

and the gaussian extension
v_{\operatorname{g}}
of the valuation of
K
from Section
II.1
.

Lemma IV.4.21
.

For
u\in{\operatorname{U}}^{\times}
we have
\lVert u\rVert^{\dagger}=\operatorname{Re}u^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

For
u=f\operatorname{e}(\lambda)
,
f\in K^{\times}
we have
\lVert u\rVert=|f|
and

u^{\dagger}=f^{\dagger}+\lambda
, so

\lVert u\rVert^{\dagger}\ =\ |f|^{\dagger}\ =\ \operatorname{Re}f^{\dagger}\ =\ \operatorname{Re}u^{\dagger},

using Corollary
I.2.5
for the second equality.
∎

Using Corollary
II.1.10
, Lemma
IV.4.21
, and [ADH, 10.5.2(i)] we obtain:

Lemma IV.4.22
.

Let
\mathfrak{W}\subseteq H^{\times}
be
\preccurlyeq
-closed.
Then for all
u\in{\operatorname{U}}^{\times}
,

\lVert u\rVert\in\mathfrak{W}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad v_{\operatorname{g}}u\in v(\mathfrak{W})\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$\operatorname{Re}u^{\dagger}<{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}$ for all ${\mathfrak{n}}\notin\mathfrak{W}$.}

Let
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
be a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
.
Applying Lemma
IV.4.22
to the set
\mathfrak{W}=\{{\mathfrak{w}}:\ {\mathfrak{w}}\prec\widehat{a}-H\}
—
so
v(\mathfrak{W})=\Gamma\setminus v(\widehat{a}-H)
—we obtain a reformulation of the condition “
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate”
in terms of the “norm”
\lVert\,\cdot\,\rVert
on
{\operatorname{U}}
:

Corollary IV.4.23
.

The following are equivalent
(
with
a
ranging over
H
)
:

(i)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate;

(ii)

for all
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
:
\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{+a}})=r
and whenever
u\in{\operatorname{U}}^{\times}
,
v_{\operatorname{g}}u\in{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})
, and

\lVert u\rVert\preccurlyeq\widehat{a}-a
, then
\lVert u\rVert\prec\widehat{a}-H
;

(iii)

for all
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
:
\operatorname{order}(L_{P_{+a}})=r
and whenever
u\in{\operatorname{U}}^{\times}
,
v_{\operatorname{g}}u\in{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})
, and

\lVert u\rVert\preccurlyeq\widehat{a}-a
, then

\operatorname{Re}u^{\dagger}<{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
for all
{\mathfrak{n}}
with

v({\mathfrak{n}})\in v(\widehat{a}-H)
.

A counterexample

Suppose
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
and
H
is not
\upomega
-free.
(Example
I.3.16
provides such
H
.)
Let
(\uplambda_{\rho})
and
(\upomega_{\rho})
be as in Lemma
III.2.10
with

H
in the role of
K
there. That lemma yields a minimal hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
in
H
with
P=2Y^{\prime}+Y^{2}+\upomega
(
\upomega\in H
). This is a good source of counterexamples:

Lemma IV.4.24
.

The minimal hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
in
H
is ultimate, and none of its refinements is quasilinear or normal.

Proof.

Let
a\in H
. Then

P_{+a}=2Y^{\prime}+2aY+Y^{2}+P(a)

and thus
L_{P_{+a}}=2(\der+a)
, so for
b\in H^{\times}
with
b^{\dagger}=-a
we have

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L_{P_{+a}})=\{vb\}
, by Corollary
IV.4.2
.
Thus
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
is ultimate iff
\uplambda-a\prec b
for all
a\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
in
H
and
b\in H^{\times}
with
b^{\dagger}=-a

and
vb\in v(\uplambda-H)
; the latter holds by [ADH, 11.5.6] since
v(\uplambda-H)=\Psi
.
Hence
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
is ultimate. No refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
is quasilinear by Corollary
III.2.25
and
[ADH, 11.7.9], and so by Corollary
III.3.21
, no refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\uplambda)
is normal.
∎

IV.5.
Repulsive-Normal Slots

In this section
H
is a real closed
H
-field with
small derivation and asymptotic integration, with
\Gamma:=v(H^{\times})
.
Also
K:=H[\imag]
with
\imag^{2}=-1
is an algebraic closure of
H
.
We study here the concept of a repulsive-normal slot in
H
, which strengthens that of a split-normal slot in
H
.
Despite their name,
repulsive-normal slots will turn out to have attractive analytic properties in the realm of Hardy fields.

Attraction and repulsion

In this subsection
a
,
b
range over
H
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
,
f
,
g
,
h
(possibly with subscripts) over
K
, and
\gamma
,
\delta
over
\Gamma
.
We say that
f
is
attractive
if
\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1
and
\operatorname{Re}f<0
, and

repulsive
if
\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1
and
\operatorname{Re}f>0
.
If
\operatorname{Re}f\sim\operatorname{Re}g
, then
f
is attractive iff
g
is attractive, and likewise with
“repulsive” in place of “attractive”.
Moreover, if
a>0
,
a\succcurlyeq 1
, and
f
is attractive (repulsive), then
af
is attractive (repulsive, respectively).

Definition IV.5.1
.

Let
\gamma>0
; we say
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive
if
v(\operatorname{Re}f)<\gamma^{\dagger}
or
{\operatorname{Re}f>0}
.
Given
S\subseteq\Gamma
, we say
f
is
S
-repulsive
if
f
is

\gamma
-repulsive for all
\gamma\in S\cap\Gamma^{>}
, equivalently,

\operatorname{Re}f>0
, or
v(\operatorname{Re}f)<\gamma^{\dagger}
for all
\gamma\in S\cap\Gamma^{>}
.

Note the following implications for
\gamma>0
:

f
is
\gamma
-repulsive

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{Re}f\neq 0,

f
is
\gamma
-repulsive,
\operatorname{Re}g\sim\operatorname{Re}f

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\text{$g$ is $\gamma$-repulsive}.

The following is easy to show:

Lemma IV.5.2
.

Suppose
\gamma>0
and
\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1
. Then
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive iff
v(\operatorname{Re}f)<\gamma^{\dagger}
or
f
is repulsive.
Hence, if
f
is repulsive, then
f
is
\Gamma
-repulsive; the converse of this implication holds if
\Psi
is not
bounded from below in
\Gamma
.

Let
\gamma,\delta>0
. If
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive and
a>0
,
a\succcurlyeq 1
, then
af
is
\gamma
-repulsive.
If
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive and
\delta
-repulsive, then
f
is
(\gamma+\delta)
-repulsive. If
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive
and
\gamma>\delta
, then
f
is
(\gamma-\delta)
-repulsive. Moreover:

Lemma IV.5.3
.

Suppose
\gamma\geqslant\delta=v{\mathfrak{n}}>0
. Set
g:=f-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
. Then:

\text{$f$ is $\gamma$-repulsive}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$f$ is $\delta$-repulsive and $g$ is $\gamma$-repulsive.}

Proof.

Note that
\gamma\geqslant\delta>0
gives
\gamma^{\dagger}\leqslant\delta^{\dagger}
.
Suppose
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive; by our remark,
f
is
\delta
-repulsive. Now if
v(\operatorname{Re}f)<\gamma^{\dagger}
, then
\operatorname{Re}g\sim\operatorname{Re}f
, whereas if
{\operatorname{Re}f>0}
, then
\operatorname{Re}g=\operatorname{Re}f-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}>\operatorname{Re}f>0
; in both cases,
g
is
\gamma
-repulsive.
Conversely, suppose
f
is
\delta
-repulsive and
g
is
\gamma
-repulsive.
If
\operatorname{Re}f>0
, then clearly
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive. Otherwise,
v(\operatorname{Re}f)<\delta^{\dagger}
, hence
\operatorname{Re}g\sim\operatorname{Re}f
, so
f
is also
\gamma
-repulsive.
∎

In a similar way we deduce a useful characterization of repulsiveness:

Lemma IV.5.4
.

Suppose
\gamma=v{\mathfrak{m}}>0
. Set
g:=f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
. Then:

\text{$f$ is repulsive}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1$, $f$ is $\gamma$-repulsive, and
$g$ is repulsive.}

Proof.

Suppose
f
is repulsive; then by Lemma
IV.5.2
,
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive. Moreover,

\operatorname{Re}g=\operatorname{Re}f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}>\operatorname{Re}f>0
, hence
\operatorname{Re}g\succcurlyeq 1
and

\operatorname{Re}g>0
, that is,
g
is repulsive.
Conversely, suppose
\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1
,
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive, and
g
is repulsive.
If
v(\operatorname{Re}f)<\gamma^{\dagger}
, then
\operatorname{Re}f\sim\operatorname{Re}g
;
otherwise
\operatorname{Re}f>0
. In both cases,
f
is repulsive.
∎

Corollary IV.5.5
.

Suppose
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive where
\gamma=v{\mathfrak{m}}>0
, and
\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1
. Then
f
is repulsive iff
f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
is repulsive, and
f
is attractive iff
f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
is attractive.

Proof.

The first equivalence is immediate from Lemma
IV.5.4
;
this equivalence yields

f
is attractive

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$f$ is not repulsive}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}$ is not repulsive}

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$\operatorname{Re}f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}\prec 1$ or $f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}$ is attractive.}

Thus if
f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
is attractive, so is
f
. Now assume towards a contradiction that
f
is attractive and
f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
is not.
Then
\operatorname{Re}f<0
and
\operatorname{Re}f-{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}\prec 1
by the above equivalence, so

\operatorname{Re}f\sim{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
thanks to
\operatorname{Re}f\succcurlyeq 1
. But
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive, that is,

\operatorname{Re}f\succ{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
or
\operatorname{Re}f>0
, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma IV.5.6
.

Suppose
\gamma=v{\mathfrak{m}}>0
and
v(\operatorname{Re}g)\geqslant\gamma^{\dagger}
. Then for all sufficiently large
c\in C^{>}
we have

\operatorname{Re}g-c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}>0

(
and hence
g-c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
is
\Gamma
-repulsive
)
.

Proof.

If
v(\operatorname{Re}g)>\gamma^{\dagger}
, then
\operatorname{Re}g-c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}\sim-c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}>0
for all
c\in C^{>}
.
Suppose
v(\operatorname{Re}g)=\gamma^{\dagger}
. Take
c_{0}\in C^{\times}
with
\operatorname{Re}g\sim c_{0}{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
;
then
\operatorname{Re}g-c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}>0
for
c>c_{0}
.
∎

In the rest of this subsection we assume that
S\subseteq\Gamma
. If
f
is
S
-repulsive, then so is
af
for
a>0
,
a\succcurlyeq 1
.
If
S>0
,
\delta>0
, and
f
is
S
-repulsive and
\delta
-repulsive, then
f
is
(S+\delta)
-repulsive.

Lemma IV.5.7
.

Suppose
f
is
S
-repulsive and
0<\delta=v{\mathfrak{n}}\in S
. Then

(i)

f
is
(S-\delta)
-repulsive;

(ii)

g:=f-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
is
S
-repulsive.

Proof.

Let
\gamma\in(S-\delta)
,
\gamma>0
.
Then
\gamma+\delta\in S
, so

f
is
(\gamma+\delta)
-repulsive, hence
\gamma
-repulsive.
This shows (i).
For (ii), suppose
\gamma\in S
,
\gamma>0
; we need to show that
g
is
\gamma
-repulsive.
If
\gamma\geqslant\delta
, then
g
is
\gamma
-repulsive
by Lemma
IV.5.3
. Taking
\gamma=\delta
we see that
g
is
\delta
-repulsive, hence if
\gamma<\delta
, then

g
is also
\gamma
-repulsive.
∎

Let
A\in K[\der]^{\neq}
have order
r\geqslant 1
. An
S
-repulsive splitting
of
A
over
K
is a splitting
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
of
A
over
K
where
g_{1},\dots,g_{r}
are
S
-repulsive.
An
S
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
remains an

S
-repulsive splitting of
hA
over
K
for
h\neq 0
.
We say that
A
splits
S
-repulsively
over
K
if
there is an
S
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
.
From Lemmas
I.1.1
and
IV.5.7
we obtain:

Lemma IV.5.8
.

Suppose
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is an
S
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
and
{0<\delta=v{\mathfrak{n}}\in S}
.
Then
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is an
(S-\delta)
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
, and
(h_{1},\dots,h_{r}):={(g_{1}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})}
is an
S
-repulsive splitting of

A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
.

(
Hence
{(h_{1},\dots,h_{r})}
is also an
(S-\delta)
-repulsive splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
.
)

Note that if
\phi
is active in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
, and
f
is
\gamma
-repulsive (in
K
), then
\phi^{-1}f
is
\gamma
-repulsive in
K^{\phi}=H^{\phi}[\imag]
.

Lemma IV.5.9
.

Suppose
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is an
S
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
and
{S\cap\Gamma^{>}}\not\subseteq\Gamma^{\flat}
.
Let
\phi
be active in
H
with
0<\phi\prec 1
, and set
h_{j}:=g_{j}-(r-j)\phi^{\dagger}
for
j=1,\dots,r
.
Then
(\phi^{-1}h_{1},\dots,\phi^{-1}h_{r})
is an
S
-repulsive splitting of
A^{\phi}
over
K^{\phi}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
I.1.2
,
(\phi^{-1}h_{1},\dots,\phi^{-1}h_{r})
is splitting of
A^{\phi}
over
K^{\phi}
.
Let
j\in\{1,\dots,r\}
. If
\operatorname{Re}g_{j}>0
, then
\phi^{\dagger}<0
yields
\operatorname{Re}h_{j}\geqslant\operatorname{Re}g_{j}>0
.
Otherwise,

v(\operatorname{Re}g_{j})<\gamma^{\dagger}
whenever
0<\gamma\in S
; in particular,
\operatorname{Re}g_{j}\succ 1\succ\phi^{\dagger}
,
so
\operatorname{Re}h_{j}\sim\operatorname{Re}g_{j}
. In both cases
h_{j}
is
S
-repulsive, so

\phi^{-1}h_{j}
is
S
-repulsive in
K^{\phi}
.
∎

Proposition IV.5.10
.

Suppose
S\cap\Gamma^{>}\neq\emptyset
,
nS\subseteq S
for all
n\geqslant 1
, the ordered constant field
C
of
H
is archimedean, and

(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is a splitting of
A
over
K
. Then there exists
\gamma\in S\cap\Gamma^{>}

such that for any
{\mathfrak{m}}
with
\gamma=v{\mathfrak{m}}
:
({g_{1}-n{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}},\dots,{g_{r}-n{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}})
is an
S
-repulsive splitting of

A{\mathfrak{m}}^{n}
over
K
, for all big enough
n
.

Proof.

Let
J
be the set of
j\in\{1,\dots,r\}
such that

g_{j}
is not
S
-repulsive. If
\gamma>0
and
g
is not
\gamma
-repulsive, then
g
is not

\delta
-repulsive, for all
\delta\geqslant\gamma
.
Hence we can take
\gamma\in S\cap\Gamma^{>}
such that
g_{j}
is not
\gamma
-repulsive, for all
j\in J
.
Suppose
\gamma=v{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Lemma
IV.5.6
yields
m\geqslant 1
such that for all
n\geqslant m
, setting
{\mathfrak{n}}:={\mathfrak{m}}^{n}
,

g_{j}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
is
\Gamma
-repulsive for all
j\in J
. For such
{\mathfrak{n}}
we have
v{\mathfrak{n}}\in S
, so
by Lemma
IV.5.7
(ii),
g_{j}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
is also
S
-repulsive for
j\notin J
.
∎

Corollary IV.5.11
.

If
C
is archimedean and
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is a splitting of
A
over
K
,
then there exists
\gamma>0
such that for all
{\mathfrak{m}}
with
\gamma=v{\mathfrak{m}}
:
{(g_{1}-n{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-n{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger})}
is a
\Gamma
-repulsive splitting of

A{\mathfrak{m}}^{n}
over
K
, for all big enough
n
. If
\Gamma\neq\Gamma^{\flat}
then we can choose such
\gamma>\Gamma^{\flat}
.

Proof.

Taking
S=\Gamma
this
follows from Proposition
IV.5.10
and its proof.
∎

In logical jargon, the condition that
C
is archimedean is not
first-order
. But it is satisfied when
H
is a Hardy field, the case
where the results of this section will be applied. For other possible uses we indicate here a first-order variant of Proposition
IV.5.10
with essentially the same proof:

Corollary IV.5.12
.

Suppose
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is a splitting of
A
over
K
. Then there exists
{\mathfrak{m}}\prec 1
such that for all sufficiently large
c\in C^{>}
and all
{\mathfrak{n}}
, if
{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}=c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
, then
({g_{1}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}},\dots,{g_{r}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}})
is a
\Gamma
-repulsive splitting of

A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
.

In connection with this corollary we recall from
[
1
, p. 105]

that
H
is said to be
closed under powers
if for all
c\in C
and
{\mathfrak{m}}
there is an
{\mathfrak{n}}

with
c{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}={\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
.

In the rest of this section
\widehat{H}
is an immediate asymptotic extension of
H
and
\imag
with
\imag^{2}=-1
lies in an asymptotic extension of
\widehat{H}
. Also
K:=H[\imag]
and
\widehat{K}:=\widehat{H}[\imag]
.

Let
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}\setminus H
, so

v({\widehat{a}-H})

is a downward closed subset of
\Gamma
.
We say that
f
is
\widehat{a}
-repulsive
if
f
is
v({\widehat{a}-H})
-repulsive; that is,
\operatorname{Re}f>0
, or
\operatorname{Re}f\succ{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
for all
a
,
{\mathfrak{m}}

with
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp\widehat{a}-a\prec 1
.
(Of course, this notion is only interesting if
v({\widehat{a}-H})\cap\Gamma^{>}\neq\emptyset
, since otherwise every
f
is
\widehat{a}
-repulsive.)
Various earlier results give:

Lemma IV.5.13
.

Suppose
f
is
\widehat{a}
-repulsive. Then

(i)

b>0,\ b\succcurlyeq 1\ \Longrightarrow\ bf
is
\widehat{a}
-repulsive;

(ii)

f
is
(\widehat{a}-a)
-repulsive;

(iii)

{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp 1\ \Longrightarrow\ f
is
\widehat{a}{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsive;

(iv)

{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\widehat{a}-a\prec 1\ \Longrightarrow\ f
is
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}}
repulsive and
f-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
is
\widehat{a}
-repulsive.

For (iv), use Lemma
IV.5.7
.
An
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting
of
A
over
K
is a

v({\widehat{a}-H})
-repulsive splitting
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
of
A
over
K
:

A\ =\ f(\der-g_{1})\cdots(\der-g_{r})\qquad\text{where $f\neq 0$ and $g_{1},\dots,g_{r}$ are $\widehat{a}$-repulsive.}

We say
that
A

splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively
over
K
if it splits
v({\widehat{a}-H})
-repulsively over
K
.
Thus if
A
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K
, then so does
hA
(
h\neq 0
), and

A
splits
(\widehat{a}-a)
-repulsively over
K
, and splits
\widehat{a}{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsively over
K
for
{\mathfrak{m}}\asymp 1
.
Moreover, from Lemma
IV.5.8
we obtain:

Corollary IV.5.14
.

Suppose
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is an
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
and
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\widehat{a}-a\prec 1
.
Then
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is an
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}}
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
and
{(g_{1}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})}
is an
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting of

A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
.

Taking
S:=v(\widehat{a}-H)
in Proposition
IV.5.10
we obtain:

Corollary IV.5.15
.

If
\widehat{a}\preccurlyeq 1
is special over
H
,
C
is archimedean, and
A
splits over
K
, then
A{\mathfrak{n}}
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K
for some
a
and
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\widehat{a}-a\prec 1
.

Recall that in Section
IV.2
we defined a splitting
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
of
A
over
K
to be
strong
if
\operatorname{Re}g_{j}\succcurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)^{\dagger}
for
j=1,\dots,r
.

Lemma IV.5.16
.

Suppose
\widehat{a}-a\prec^{\flat}1
for some
a
. Let
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
be an
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
, let
\phi
be active in
H
with
0<\phi\prec 1
,
and set

h_{j}\ :=\ \phi^{-1}\big(g_{j}-(r-j)\phi^{\dagger}\big)\qquad(j=1,\dots,r).

Then

(h_{1},\dots,h_{r})
is an
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting of
A^{\phi}
over
K^{\phi}=H^{\phi}[\imag]
.
If
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec^{\flat}1
and
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
is strong, then
(h_{1},\dots,h_{r})
is strong.

This follows from Lemmas
IV.2.12
and
IV.5.9
.

Lemma IV.5.17
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1
and
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}1
.
Let
(g_{1},\dots,g_{r})
be an
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting of
A
over
K
. Then for all sufficiently small
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
and any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\lvert{\mathfrak{v}}\rvert^{q}
,
(g_{1}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is a strong
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}}
-repulsive splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
.

Proof.

Take
q_{0}\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
with
\widehat{a}\prec|{\mathfrak{v}}|^{q_{0}}\prec 1
. Then for any
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0<q\leqslant q_{0}
and any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\lvert{\mathfrak{v}}\rvert^{q}
,

(g_{1}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is an
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}}
-repulsive splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
, by Corollary
IV.5.14
.
Using Lemmas
IV.2.13
and
IV.2.10
(in that order) we can decrease
q_{0}
so that for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0<q\leqslant q_{0}
and
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp\lvert{\mathfrak{v}}\rvert^{q}
,

(g_{1}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger},\dots,g_{r}-{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is also a strong splitting of
A{\mathfrak{n}}
over
K
.
∎

In the rest of this subsection we assume that
H
is Liouville closed with
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.

We choose a complement
\Lambda\subseteq H\imag
of
K^{\dagger}
in
K
as in Section
IV.4
and set
{\operatorname{U}}:=K\big[\!\operatorname{e}(\Lambda)\big]
.
We then have the set
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\subseteq\Gamma
of ultimate exceptional values of
A

(which doesn’t depend on
\Lambda
by Corollary
IV.4.1
).
Recall from Corollary
I.2.28
that
H
is of Hardy type iff
C
is archimedean.

We now assume
r=1
and
\widehat{a}\prec 1
is special over
H
, and let

\Delta
be the nontrivial convex subgroup of
\Gamma
that is cofinal in
v(\widehat{a}-H)
.

Lemma IV.5.18
.

Suppose
C
is archimedean and

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)<0
. Then
A
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K
.

Proof.

We may arrange
A=\der-f
.
Take
u\in{\operatorname{U}}^{\times}
with
u^{\dagger}=f
, and
b:=\lVert u\rVert\in H^{>}
.
Then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)=\{vb\}
by Lemma
II.5.12
and its proof, hence

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)<0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$b\succ 1$ or $vb>\Delta$,}

and
\operatorname{Re}f=b^{\dagger}
by Lemma
IV.4.21
.
If
b\succ 1
, then
\operatorname{Re}f>0
, and if
vb>\Delta
, then for all
\delta\in\Delta^{\neq}
we have
\psi(vb)<\psi(\delta)
by Lemma
I.2.24
, so
\operatorname{Re}f\succ{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
for all
a
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
with
\widehat{a}-a\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}\prec 1
.
In both cases
A
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K
.
∎

Lemma IV.5.19
.

Suppose
A\in H[\der]
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1
.
Then
0\notin{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
, and if

A
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K
, then
{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)<0
.

Proof.

We again arrange
A=\der-f
and
take
u
,
b
as in the proof of Lemma
IV.5.18
. Then

f\in H
and
b^{\dagger}=f=-1/{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\succ 1
, so
b\not\asymp 1
, and thus
0\notin\{vb\}={\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)
. Now suppose
A
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K
, that is,

f>0
or
f\succ{\mathfrak{m}}^{\dagger}
for all
a
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
with
\widehat{a}-a\asymp{\mathfrak{m}}\prec 1
.
In the first case
f=b^{\dagger}
and
b\not\asymp 1
yield
b\succ 1
.
In the second case
\psi(vb)=vf<\psi(\delta)
for all
\delta\in\Delta^{\neq}
, hence
vb\notin\Delta
.
∎

Combining Lemma
IV.2.11
with the previous two lemmas yields:

Corollary IV.5.20
.

Suppose
A\in H[\der]
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1
, and
H
is of Hardy type. Then
A
splits strongly over
K
, and we have the equivalence

\text{$A$ splits $\widehat{a}$-repulsively over $K$}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ {\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(A)\cap v(\widehat{a}-H)\ \leqslant\ 0.

Defining repulsive-normality

In this subsection

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
with
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{H}\setminus H
and linear part
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
.

Set
w:=\operatorname{wt}(P)
;
if
\operatorname{order}L=r
, set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
.
We let
a
,
b
range over
H
and
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
.

Definition IV.5.21
.

Call
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})

repulsive-normal
if
\operatorname{order}L=r
, and

(RN1)

{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
;

(RN2)

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\geqslant 1}=Q+R
where
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q}
splits
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsively over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

Compare this with “split-normality” from Definition
IV.3.3
: clearly repulsive-normal implies split-normal, and hence normal.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and
L
splits
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsively over
K
, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal, then so are
(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
and
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}})
.

Lemma IV.5.22
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal and
\phi
is active in
H
such that
{0<\phi\prec 1}
, and
\widehat{a}-a\prec^{\flat}{\mathfrak{m}}

for some
a
. Then the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
is repulsive-normal.

Proof.

First arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
, and let
Q
,
R
be as in (RN2) for
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
.
Now
(P^{\phi},1,\widehat{a})
is split-normal by Lemma
IV.3.5
. In fact,

P^{\phi}_{\geqslant 1}=Q^{\phi}+R^{\phi}
, and
the proof
of this lemma shows that
R^{\phi}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}P_{1}^{\phi}

where
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P^{\phi}})
.
By Lemma
IV.5.16
,
L_{Q^{\phi}}=L_{Q}^{\phi}
splits
\widehat{a}
-repulsively over
K^{\phi}
.
So
(P^{\phi},1,\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal.
∎

If
\operatorname{order}L=r
,
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
, and
\widehat{a}-a\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
, then
\widehat{a}-a\prec^{\flat}{\mathfrak{m}}
. Thus we obtain from
Lemmas
III.3.13
and
IV.5.22
the following result:

Corollary IV.5.23
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, deep, and repulsive-normal. Let
\phi
be active in
H
with
0<\phi\prec 1
. Then the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
is repulsive-normal.

Before we turn to the task of obtaining repulsive-normal slots, we deal with the preservation of repulsive-normality under refinements.

Lemma IV.5.24
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal, and let
Q
,
R
be as in
(RN2)
.
Let
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
be a steep refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
where
{\mathfrak{n}}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
or

{\mathfrak{n}}={\mathfrak{m}}
. Suppose

(P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{\geqslant 1}-Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}(P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}})_{1}\qquad\text{where ${\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}})$.}

Then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is repulsive-normal.

Proof.

By (RN2),
L_{Q}
splits
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsively over
K
, so
L_{Q}
also splits

(\widehat{a}-a)/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsively over
K
. We have
(\widehat{a}-a)/{\mathfrak{m}}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}\prec 1
or

(\widehat{a}-a)/{\mathfrak{m}}\prec 1={\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}
, so
L_{Q}
splits
(\widehat{a}-a)/{\mathfrak{n}}
-repulsively over
K
by the first part of Corollary
IV.5.14
, and hence
L_{Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}}}=L_{Q}\cdot({\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}})
splits
(\widehat{a}-a)/{\mathfrak{n}}
-repulsively over
K
by the second part of that
Corollary
IV.5.14
. Thus
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
is repulsive-normal.
∎

The proofs of Lemmas
IV.3.18
,
IV.3.19
,
IV.3.20
give the following repulsive-normal analogues of these lemmas, using also Lemma
IV.5.24
; for Lemma
IV.5.27
below we adopt the notational conventions about
{\mathfrak{n}}^{q}
(
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
) stated
before Lemma
IV.3.20
.

Lemma IV.5.25
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal and
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
is a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
, then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
is also repulsive-normal.

Lemma IV.5.26
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal,
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\prec{\mathfrak{m}}
, and
{[{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}]\leqslant\big[{\mathfrak{v}}]}
. Then the refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})

is repulsive-normal: if
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
P
,
Q
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
are as in
(RN2)
, then
(RN2)
holds with
{\mathfrak{n}}
,
Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
R_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}/{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}})
in place of
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
Q
,
R
,
{\mathfrak{v}}
.

Lemma IV.5.27
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
,
(P,1,\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal,
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{n}}\prec 1
, and for
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P})

we have
[{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}]<[{\mathfrak{v}}]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]
;
then
(P,{\mathfrak{n}}^{q},\widehat{a})
is a repulsive-normal refinement
of
(P,1,\widehat{a})
for all but finitely many
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
0<q<1
.

Achieving repulsive-normality

In this subsection we adopt the setting of the subsection
Achieving split-normality

of Section
IV.3
:
H
is
\upomega
-free and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a minimal hole in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
,
{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
, and
\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}\setminus K
, with

\widehat{a}=\widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\imag
,
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\widehat{H}
.

We let
a
range over
K
,
b
,
c
over
H
, and
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
. We prove here the following variant of Theorem
IV.3.9
:

Theorem IV.5.28
.

Suppose the constant field
C
of
H
is archimedean and
\deg P>1
. Then one of the following conditions is satisfied:

\mathrm{(i)}

\widehat{b}\notin H
and some
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has a special refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal;

\mathrm{(ii)}

\widehat{c}\notin H
and some
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a special refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal.

To establish this theorem we need to take up the approximation arguments in the proof of Theorem
IV.3.9
once again. While in that proof we treated the cases
{\widehat{b}\in H}
and
{\widehat{c}\in H}
separately to obtain stronger results in those cases (Lemmas
IV.3.10
,
IV.3.11
),
here we proceed differently and first show a repulsive-normal version of
Proposition
IV.3.12
which also applies to those cases.
In the rest of
this subsection we assume that
C
is archimedean.

Proposition IV.5.29
.

Suppose the hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
is special and
v({\widehat{b}-H})\subseteq v({\widehat{c}-H})

(
so
\widehat{b}\notin H
)
. Let
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
be a
Z
-minimal deep normal slot in
H
. Then
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has a repulsive-normal refinement.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
IV.3.12
we first arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
, and set

\Delta\ :=\ \big\{\delta\in\Gamma:\ \lvert\delta\rvert\in v\big(\widehat{a}-K\big)\big\},

a convex subgroup of
\Gamma
which is cofinal in
v(\widehat{a}-K)=v({\widehat{b}-H})
,
so
\widehat{b}
is special over
H
.
Lemma
III.3.13
applied to
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
and
{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q})\prec^{\flat}1
gives that

\Gamma^{\flat}
is strictly contained in
\Delta
. To show that
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
has a repulsive-normal refinement, we follow the proof of Proposition
IV.3.12
, skipping the initial compositional conjugation, and arranging first that
P,Q\asymp 1
. Recall from that proof that
\dot{\widehat{a}}\in\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}=\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}[\imag]
and
\operatorname{Re}\dot{\widehat{a}}=\dot{\widehat{b}}\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}\setminus\dot{H}
, with
\dot{\widehat{b}}\prec 1
,
\dot{Q}\in\dot{H}\{Y\}
, and so
\dot{Q}_{+\dot{\widehat{b}}}\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}\{Y\}
. Let
A\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}[\der]
be the linear part
of
\dot{Q}_{+\dot{\widehat{b}}}
. Recall from that proof that
1\leqslant s:=\operatorname{order}Q=\operatorname{order}A\leqslant 2r
and that

A
splits over
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
. Then Lemma
I.1.4
gives a
real
splitting

(g_{1},\dots,g_{s})
of
A
over
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
:

A\ =\ f(\der-g_{1})\cdots(\der-g_{s}),\qquad 0\neq f\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}},\ g_{1},\dots,g_{s}\in\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}.

It follows easily from [ADH, 10.1.8] that the real closed
\operatorname{d}
-valued field
\dot{H}
is an
H
-field, and so its completion
\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
is also a real closed
H
-field by [ADH, 10.5.9]. Recall also that
\Delta=v(\dot{H}^{\times})
is the value group of
\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
and properly contains
\Gamma^{\flat}
. Thus we can apply Corollary
IV.5.11
with
\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}
in the role of
H
to get
{\mathfrak{n}}\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}
with
0\neq\dot{\mathfrak{n}}\prec^{\flat}1
and
m
such that for all
n>m
,

(h_{1},\dots,h_{s}):=({g_{1}-n\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}},\dots,g_{s}-n\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger})
is a
\Delta
-repulsive splitting of
A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}
over
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
, so
\operatorname{Re}h_{1},\dots,\operatorname{Re}h_{s}\neq 0
. For any
n
,
A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}
is the
linear part of
\dot{Q}_{+\dot{\widehat{b}},\times\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}\in\dot{H}^{\operatorname{c}}\{Y\}
,
and
(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})
is also a real splitting
of
A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}
over
\dot{K}^{\operatorname{c}}
:

A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}\ =\ \dot{{\mathfrak{n}}}^{n}f(\der-h_{1})\cdots(\der-h_{s}).

By increasing
m
we arrange that for all
n>m
we have

g_{j}\not\sim n\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{\dagger}
(
j=1,\dots,s
), and also
{\mathfrak{v}}(A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n})\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)

provided
\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big]<[\dot{\mathfrak{n}}]
;
for the latter part use Lemma
III.1.16
.
Below we assume
n>m
. Then
{\mathfrak{v}}(A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n})\prec 1
: to see this use
Corollary
III.1.4
,
{\mathfrak{v}}(A)\prec 1
, and
g_{j}\preccurlyeq h_{j}

(j=1,\dots,s
). Note that

h_{1},\dots,h_{s}\succcurlyeq 1
.
We now apply
Corollary
IV.2.9
to

\dot{H}
,
\dot{K}
,
\dot{Q}
,
s
,
\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}
,
\dot{\widehat{b}}
,
\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}f
,
h_{1},\dots,h_{s}

in place of
H
,
K
,
P
,
r
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
f
,
a
,
b_{1},\dots,b_{r}
, respectively, and any
\gamma\in\Delta

with
\gamma>v(\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}),v(\operatorname{Re}h_{1}),\dots,v(\operatorname{Re}h_{s})
.
This gives
a,b\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}
and
b_{1},\dots,b_{s}\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
such that
\dot{a},\dot{b}\neq 0
in

\dot{H}
and such that for the linear part
\widetilde{A}\in\dot{H}[\der]
of
\dot{Q}_{+\dot{b},\times\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}
we have

\dot{b}\ -\dot{\widehat{b}}\ \prec\ \dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n},\qquad\widetilde{A}\ \sim\ A\dot{{\mathfrak{n}}}^{n},\qquad\operatorname{order}\widetilde{A}\ =\ s,\qquad{\mathfrak{w}}\ :=\ {\mathfrak{v}}(\widetilde{A})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}}(A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}),

and such that for
w:=\operatorname{wt}(Q)
and with
\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})\subseteq\Delta
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}\ =\ \widetilde{B}+\widetilde{E},

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{B}\ =\ \dot{a}(\der-\dot{b}_{1})\cdots(\der-\dot{b}_{s})\in\dot{H}[\der],\quad\widetilde{E}\in\dot{H}[\der],

\displaystyle v(\dot{b}_{1}-h_{1}),

\displaystyle\dots,v(\dot{b}_{s}-h_{s})\ >\ \gamma,\quad\widetilde{E}\ \prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}\ {\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}\widetilde{A},

and
(\dot{b}_{1},\dots,\dot{b}_{s})
is a real splitting of
\widetilde{B}
over
\dot{K}
. This real splitting over
\dot{K}
has a consequence that will be crucial at the end of the proof: by changing
b_{1},\dots,b_{s}
if necessary, without changing
\dot{b}_{1},\dots,\dot{b}_{s}
we arrange that
B:=a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{s})
lies in
\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]\subseteq H[\der]
and that

(b_{1},\dots,b_{s})
is a real splitting of
B
over
K
. (Lemma
I.1.6
.)

Since
\operatorname{Re}\dot{b}_{1}\sim\operatorname{Re}h_{1},\dots,\operatorname{Re}\dot{b}_{s}\sim\operatorname{Re}h_{s}
,
the implication just before Lemma
IV.5.2
gives that
(\dot{b}_{1},\dots,\dot{b}_{s})
is a
\Delta
-repulsive splitting of
\widetilde{B}

over
\dot{K}
.
Now
\widehat{b}-b\prec{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}\prec 1
, so
(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
is a refinement of the normal slot

(Q,1,\widehat{b})
in
H
, hence
