(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
is normal by Proposition
III.3.25
.
We claim that the refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}}^{n},\widehat{b}-b)
of
(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
is also normal.
If
[{\mathfrak{n}}]\leqslant\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b}})\big]
, this claim holds by Corollary
III.3.27
.
From Lemma
III.1.28
and
III.1.7
we obtain:

\displaystyle\operatorname{order}L_{Q_{+b}}\

\displaystyle=\ \operatorname{order}L_{Q}\ =\ \operatorname{order}L_{Q_{+\widehat{b}}}\ =\ s,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b}})\ \sim\ {\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q})\

\displaystyle\sim\ {\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+\widehat{b}}}),\qquad v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+\widehat{b}}})\big)\ =\ v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big),

so
v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b}})\big)=v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A)\big)
. Moreover, by Lemma
III.1.7
and the facts about
\widetilde{A}
,

v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}})\big)\ =\ v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(\widetilde{A})\big)\ =\ v\big({\mathfrak{v}}(A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n})\big)\ =\ v({\mathfrak{w}}).

Suppose
\big[{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b}})\big]<[{\mathfrak{n}}]
. Then
[{\mathfrak{v}}(A)]<[\dot{{\mathfrak{n}}}]
, so
{\mathfrak{v}}(A\dot{\mathfrak{n}}^{n})\preccurlyeq{\mathfrak{v}}(A)
using
n>m
. Now the asymptotic relations among the various
{\mathfrak{v}}(\dots)
above
give

{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}})\ \preccurlyeq\ {\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b}}),

hence
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}}^{n},\widehat{b}-b)
is normal by Corollary
III.3.29
applied to
H
and the normal slot
(Q_{+b},1,\widehat{b}-b)
in
H
in the role of
K
and
(P,1,\widehat{a})
, respectively.
Put
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}})
, so
{\mathfrak{v}}\asymp{\mathfrak{w}}
. Note that
Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}\{Y\}
, so the image of
L_{Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}}\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]
in

\dot{H}[\der]
is
\widetilde{A}
. Thus in
H[\der]
we have:

L_{Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}}\ =\ B+E\qquad\text{where $E\in\dot{\mathcal{O}}[\der]$, $\ E\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}L_{Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}}}$.}

Now
\dot{b}_{1},\dots,\dot{b}_{s}
are
\Delta
-repulsive, so
b_{1},\dots,b_{s}
are
\Delta
-repulsive, hence

B\ =\ a(\der-b_{1})\cdots(\der-b_{s})

splits
\Delta
-repulsively, and thus
(\widehat{b}-b)/{\mathfrak{n}}^{n}
-repulsively.
Therefore
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}}^{n},\widehat{b}-b)
is repulsive-normal.
∎

Instead of assuming in the above proposition that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is special and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is deep and normal, we can assume, as with Corollary
IV.3.13
, that
\deg P>1
:

Corollary IV.5.30
.

Suppose
\deg P>1
and
v({\widehat{b}}-H)\subseteq v({\widehat{c}}-H)
. Let
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
have minimal complexity.
Then the
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has a special refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
such that

(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal.

Proof.

The beginning of the subsection
Achieving split-normality

of Section
IV.3
and
\deg P>1
give that
K
is
r
-linearly newtonian.
Lemmas
III.2.26
and
III.3.23
yield a quasilinear refinement
{(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)}
of
our hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
.
Set
b:=\operatorname{Re}a
. By Lemma
IV.1.3
we have

v\big((\widehat{a}-a)-K\big)\ =\ v({\widehat{a}}-K)\ =\ v\big({\widehat{b}}-H\big)\ =\ {v\big((\widehat{b}-b)-H\big)}.

Replacing
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by

(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
and
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
, respectively, we arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is quasilinear.
Then
by Proposition
I.5.12
and
K
being
r
-linearly newtonian,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is special;
hence so is
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
. Proposition
III.3.36
gives a refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
and an active
\phi_{0}\in H^{>}
such that
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is deep and normal.
Refinements of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
remain quasilinear by Corollary
III.2.23
. Since
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
, Lemma
IV.1.3
(ii) gives
a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
with
\operatorname{Re}a=b
.
By Lemma
III.2.35
the minimal hole
(P^{\phi_{0}}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
in
K^{\phi_{0}}
is special. Proposition
IV.5.29
applied to
(P^{\phi_{0}}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
,
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
in place of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, respectively, gives us
{b_{0}\in H}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{0}\in H^{\times}
and a repulsive-normal
refinement
\big(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
of
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
.
This refinement is steep and hence deep by Corollary
III.3.6
, since
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is deep. Thus
by Corollary
IV.5.23
,
\big(Q_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
is a refinement
of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
such that that

\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal. As a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, it is special.
∎

In the same way that Corollary
IV.3.13
gave rise to Corollary
IV.3.14
,
Corollary
IV.5.30
gives rise to the following:

Corollary IV.5.31
.

If
\deg P>1
,
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
, and
R\in Z(H,\widehat{c})
has minimal complexity, then the
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a special refinement
(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{c}-c})
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal.

By Lemma
IV.1.3
we have
v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
or
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
, hence
the two corollaries above yield Theorem
IV.5.28
, completing its proof. ∎

Strengthening repulsive-normality

In this subsection we adopt the setting of the subsection
Strengthening split-normality
of Section
IV.3
.
Thus
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is a slot in
H
of order
r\geqslant 1
and weight
w:=\operatorname{wt}(P)
, and
L:=L_{P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}}
. If
\operatorname{order}L=r
,
we set
{\mathfrak{v}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L)
. We let
a
,
b
range over
H
and
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
{\mathfrak{n}}
over
H^{\times}
.

Definition IV.5.32
.

We say that

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
almost strongly repulsive-normal
if
\operatorname{order}L=r
,

{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
, and there are
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
such that

(RN2as)

(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\geqslant 1}=Q+R
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q}
has a strong
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsive splitting over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

We say that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
strongly repulsive-normal

if
\operatorname{order}L=r
,
{\mathfrak{v}}\prec^{\flat}1
, and there are
Q,R\in H\{Y\}
such that:

(RN2s)

P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}=Q+R
,
Q
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,

L_{Q}
has a strong
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsive splitting over
K
, and
R\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}})_{1}
.

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal, then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly split-normal; likewise without “almost”.
Thus we can augment our diagram from Section
IV.3
as follows, the implications holding for slots of order
\geqslant 1

in real closed
H
-fields with small derivation and asymptotic integration:

strongly repulsive-normal

almost strongly repulsive-normal

repulsive-normal

strongly split-normal

almost strongly split-normal

split-normal

strictly normal

normal

Adapting the proof of Lemma
IV.3.23
gives:

Lemma IV.5.33
.

The following are equivalent:

(i)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly repulsive-normal;

(ii)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal and strictly normal;

(iii)

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal and
P(0)\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{w+1}(P_{1})_{\times{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

Corollary IV.5.34
.

If
L
has a strong
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsive splitting over
K
, then:

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is normal,}

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly repulsive-normal

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is strictly normal.}

If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal, then so are

(bP,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
for
b\neq 0
and
(P_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}},{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}})
, and likewise with “strongly”
in place of “almost strongly”.
The proof of the next lemma is like that of Lemma
IV.3.25
,
using Lemmas
IV.5.25
and
IV.5.33
in place of
Lemmas
IV.3.18
and
IV.3.23
, respectively.

Lemma IV.5.35
.

Suppose
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
refines
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal, then so is
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strongly repulsive-normal,
Z
-minimal, and

\widehat{a}-a\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}^{r+w+1}{\mathfrak{m}}
, then
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)
is strongly repulsive-normal.

Here is the key to achieving almost strong repulsive-normality; its proof is similar to that of Lemma
IV.3.26
:

Lemma IV.5.36
.

Suppose that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is repulsive-normal and
\widehat{a}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then for all sufficiently small
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
,
any
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}^{q}{\mathfrak{m}}
yields an almost strongly repulsive-normal refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.

Proof.

First arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
. Take
Q
,
R
as in (RN2) for
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
.
Then Lemma
IV.5.17
gives
q_{0}\in{\mathbb{Q}}^{>}
such that
\widehat{a}\prec{\mathfrak{v}}^{q_{0}}
and
for all
q\in{\mathbb{Q}}
with
{0<q\leqslant q_{0}}
and
{\mathfrak{n}}\asymp{\mathfrak{v}}^{q}
,

L_{Q_{\times{\mathfrak{n}}}}=L_{Q}{\mathfrak{n}}
has a strong
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{n}}
-repulsive splitting over
K
.
Now Lemma
IV.5.26
yields that
(P,{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal for such
{\mathfrak{n}}
.
∎

Using this lemma we now adapt the proof of Corollary
IV.3.27
to obtain:

Corollary IV.5.37
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal, deep, and repulsive-normal.
Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has a deep and almost strongly repulsive-normal refinement.

Proof.

Lemma
III.3.13
gives
a
such that
\widehat{a}-a\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{m}}
. By Corollary
III.3.8
, the refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{a}-a})
of

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is deep with
{\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P_{+a,\times{\mathfrak{m}}}})\asymp_{\Delta({\mathfrak{v}})}{\mathfrak{v}}
, and by Lemma
IV.5.25
it is also
repulsive-normal.
Now apply Lemma
IV.5.36
to
{(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a}-a)}
in place of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and again use Corollary
III.3.8
to preserve being deep.
∎

Next we adapt the proof of Lemma
IV.3.28

to obtain a result about the behavior of (almost) repulsive-normality under compositional conjugation:

Lemma IV.5.38
.

Suppose
\phi
is active in
H
with
0<\phi\prec 1
, and there exists
a
with
\widehat{a}-a\prec^{\flat}{\mathfrak{m}}
. If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal, then so is the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
. Likewise with “strongly” in place of “almost strongly”.

Proof.

We arrange
{\mathfrak{m}}=1
,
assume
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal, and take
Q
,
R
as in (RN2as).
The proof of Lemma
IV.3.5
shows that with
{\mathfrak{w}}:={\mathfrak{v}}(L_{P^{\phi}})

we have
{\mathfrak{w}}\prec^{\flat}_{\phi}1
and
(P^{\phi})_{\geqslant 1}=Q^{\phi}+R^{\phi}
where

Q^{\phi}\in H^{\phi}\{Y\}
is homogeneous of degree
1
and order
r
,
L_{Q^{\phi}}
splits over
K^{\phi}
, and

R^{\phi}\prec_{\Delta({\mathfrak{w}})}{\mathfrak{w}}^{w+1}(P^{\phi})_{1}
. By Lemma
IV.5.16
,
L_{Q^{\phi}}=L_{Q}^{\phi}
has even a strong
\widehat{a}
-repulsive splitting over
K
.
Hence
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal.
For the rest we use Lemma
IV.5.33
and
the fact that if
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is strictly normal, then so is
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
.
∎

Lemma
III.3.13
, the remark preceding Corollary
IV.5.23
, and Lemma
IV.5.38
yield:

Corollary IV.5.39
.

Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is

Z
-minimal and deep, and
\phi
is active in
H
with
0<\phi\prec 1
.
If
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal, then so
is the slot
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
H^{\phi}
. Likewise with “strongly” in place of “almost strongly”.

In the case
r=1
, ultimateness yields almost strong repulsive-normality, under suitable assumptions;
more precisely:

Lemma IV.5.40
.

Suppose
H
is Liouville closed and of Hardy type, and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Assume also that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is normal and special, of order
r=1
. Then

\text{$(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})$ is ultimate}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{$L$ has a strong $\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}$-repulsive splitting over $K$,}

in which case
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is almost strongly repulsive-normal.

Proof.

By Lemma
IV.4.12
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is ultimate iff

{\mathscr{E}}^{\operatorname{u}}(L)\cap v\big((\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}})-H\big)\leqslant 0
, and the latter is
equivalent to
L
having a strong
\widehat{a}/{\mathfrak{m}}
-repulsive splitting over
K
, by Corollary
IV.5.20
.
For the rest use Corollary
IV.5.34
.
∎

Liouville closed
H
-fields are
1
-linearly newtonian by Corollary
I.7.29
, so in view of Lemma
III.2.36
and Corollary
III.3.21
we may replace the hypothesis “
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is special” in the previous lemma
by “
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal or a hole in
H
”.
This leads to repulsive-normal analogues of Lemma
IV.3.29
and Corollary
IV.3.30
for
r=1
:

Lemma IV.5.41
.

Assume
H
is Liouville closed and of Hardy type, and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Suppose
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is
Z
-minimal and quasilinear of order
r=1
. Then there is a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
\phi
in
H

with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
such that
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
is deep, strictly normal, and ultimate
(
so
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is strongly repulsive-normal by Lemmas
IV.5.40
and
IV.5.33
)
.

Proof.

For any active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
we may replace
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
. We may
also replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by any of its refinements.
Since
H
is
1
-linearly newtonian,
Corollary
III.3.35
gives a refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
\phi
in
H
such that
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
and
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
is normal.
Replacing
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
,
we arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
itself is normal.
Then
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
has an ultimate refinement by Proposition
IV.4.14
, and
applying Corollary
III.3.35
to this refinement and using Lemma
IV.4.10
, we obtain an ultimate refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and an active
\phi
in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1

such that the
Z
-minimal slot
(P_{+a}^{\phi},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
in
H^{\phi}
is deep, normal, and ultimate.
Again replacing
H
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
by
H^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi}_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{a}-a})
,
we arrange that
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is deep, normal, and ultimate.
Corollary
III.3.47
yields a deep and strictly normal refinement
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{a}-a})
of

(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
; this refinement is still ultimate by Lemma
IV.4.10
.
Hence
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{m}},{\widehat{a}-a})
is a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
as required, with
\phi=1
.
∎

Combining Lemmas
III.2.26
and
IV.5.41
with Corollary
IV.5.39
yields:

Corollary IV.5.42
.

Assume
H
is Liouville closed,
\upomega
-free, and of Hardy type, and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
.
Then every
Z
-minimal slot in
H
of order
r=1
has a refinement
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
such that
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
is eventually deep, ultimate, and strongly repulsive-normal.

In the next subsection we show how minimal holes of degree
>1
in
K
give rise to deep, ultimate, strongly repulsive-normal,
Z
-minimal slots in
H
.

Achieving strong repulsive-normality

Let
H
be an
\upomega
-free Liouville closed
H
-field with small derivation and constant field
C
, and
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
a minimal hole of order
r\geqslant 1
in
K:=H[\imag]
. Other conventions are as in the subsection
Achieving repulsive-normality.

Our goal is to prove a version of Theorem
IV.5.28
with “repulsive-normal” improved to
“strongly repulsive-normal + ultimate”:

Theorem IV.5.43
.

Suppose
C
is archimedean,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
, and
\deg P>1
. Then one of the following conditions is satisfied:

\mathrm{(i)}

\widehat{b}\notin H
and some
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has a special refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate;

\mathrm{(ii)}

\widehat{c}\notin H
and some
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a special refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate.

The proof of this theorem rests on the following two lemmas, where the standing assumption that
H
is Liouville closed can be dropped.

Lemma IV.5.44
.

Suppose
\widehat{b}\notin H
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
with a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal.
Then
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
has a refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep and almost strongly repulsive-normal.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of Lemma
IV.3.34
.
Let
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
be a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
and
let
\phi_{0}
be active in
H
such that
0<\phi_{0}\preccurlyeq 1

and
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is deep and repulsive-normal.
Then Corollary
IV.5.37

yields a refinement

\big((Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)

of
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
which is deep and
almost strongly repulsive-normal. Hence

\big((Q_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)\ =\ \big(Q_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})\big)

is a refinement of
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
, and
\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}}_{0},\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})\big)
is eventually deep and almost strongly repulsive-normal by Corollary
IV.5.39
.
∎

In the same way we obtain:

Lemma IV.5.45
.

Suppose
\widehat{c}\notin H
and
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
is a
Z
-minimal slot in
H
with a refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and repulsive-normal.
Then
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
has a refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep and almost strongly repulsive-normal.

Theorem
IV.5.28
and the two lemmas above give Theorem
IV.5.28
with “repulsive-normal” improved to “almost strongly repulsive-normal”.
We now upgrade this further to “strongly repulsive-normal + ultimate” (under an extra assumption).

Recall from Lemma
IV.1.3
that

v(\widehat{b}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{c}-H)
or
v(\widehat{c}-H)\subseteq v(\widehat{b}-H)
.
Thus the next two lemmas finish the proof of Theorem
IV.5.43
.

Lemma IV.5.46
.

Suppose
C
is archimedean,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
,
\deg P>1
, and

v(\widehat{b}-H)\ \subseteq\ v({\widehat{c}-H}).

Let
Q\in Z(H,\widehat{b})
have minimal complexity.
Then the
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has a special refinement
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate.

Proof.

Here are two ways of modifying
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
. First, let
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
be a refinement of

(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
. Lemma
IV.1.3
gives
c\in H
with
v(\widehat{a}-a)=v(\widehat{b}-b)
with
a:=b+c\imag
, and so the minimal
hole
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)
in
K
is a refinement of
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
that relates to
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)

as
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
relates to
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
. So we can replace
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
and
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by
(P_{+a},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{a}-a)

and
(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
, whenever convenient. Second, let
\phi
be active in
H
with
0<\phi\preccurlyeq 1
.
Then we can likewise replace
H
,
K
,
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
by
H^{\phi}
,
K^{\phi}
,
(P^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
,
(Q^{\phi},{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
.

In this way we first arrange as in the proof of Corollary
IV.5.30
that
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is special. Next, we use Proposition
III.3.36
likewise to arrange that

(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is also normal. By Propositions
IV.4.14
(where the assumption
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
comes into play) and
III.3.25
we arrange that
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
is
ultimate as well. The properties “special” and “ultimate” persist under further refinements and compositional conjugations.

Now Corollary
IV.5.30
and Lemma
IV.5.44

give a refinement

(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
of the slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
and an active
\phi_{0}
in
H
with
0<\phi_{0}\preccurlyeq 1

such that the slot
(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b})
in
H^{\phi_{0}}
is deep and almost strongly repulsive-normal.
Corollary
III.3.47
then yields a deep and strictly normal refinement

\big((Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b})_{+b_{0}},{\mathfrak{n}},(\widehat{b}-b)-b_{0}\big)

of
\big(Q^{\phi_{0}}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-b}\big)
.
This refinement is still almost strongly repulsive-normal by
Lemma
IV.5.35
, and therefore strongly repulsive-normal by
Lemma
IV.5.33
.
Corollary
IV.5.39
then gives that
\big(Q_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)

is a special refinement of our slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
such that
\big(Q^{\phi}_{+(b+b_{0})},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{b}-(b+b_{0})}\big)
is eventually
deep and strongly repulsive-normal.
∎

Likewise:

Lemma IV.5.47
.

Suppose
C
is archimedean,
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
,
\deg P>1
, and

v(\widehat{c}-H)\ \subseteq\ v(\widehat{b}-H).

Let
R\in Z(H,\widehat{c})
have minimal complexity. Then the
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a special refinement
(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},{\widehat{c}-c})
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate.

IV.6.
The Main Theorem

We prove here the Normalization Theorem from the Introduction, as a corollary of Theorem
IV.5.43
. It is accordingly less detailed than the latter, but more user-friendly. It is what will get used at a key stage in
[
6
]
.

Corollary IV.6.1
.

Let
H
be an

\upomega
-free Liouville closed
H
-field with small derivation, archimedean ordered constant field
C
, and
1
-linearly newtonian algebraic closure
H[\imag]
. Suppose
H
is not newtonian. Then
for some
Z
-minimal special hole
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
in
H
with
\operatorname{order}Q\geqslant 1
and some active
\phi>0
in
H
with
\phi\preccurlyeq 1
, the hole

(Q^{\phi},1,\widehat{b})
in
H^{\phi}
is deep, strongly repulsive-normal,
and ultimate.

Proof.

By Proposition
I.7.28
,
K:=H[\imag]
is
1
-linearly surjective and
\operatorname{I}(K)\subseteq K^{\dagger}
. As
H
is not newtonian,
neither is
K
, by (
0.13
), so Lemma
III.2.1
and subsequent remarks
give a minimal hole
(P,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{a})
in
K
of order
r\geqslant 1
, where
{\mathfrak{m}}\in H^{\times}
. Then
\deg P>1
by Corollary
III.2.8
.
By Lemma
IV.2.15
we arrange that
{\widehat{a}\in\widehat{K}:=\widehat{H}[\imag]}
where

\widehat{H}
is an immediate
\upomega
-free newtonian
H
-field extension of
H
.
Now
\widehat{a}=\widehat{b}+\widehat{c}\imag
with
\widehat{b},\widehat{c}\in\widehat{H}
.
By Theorem
IV.5.43
there are two cases:

(1)

\widehat{b}\notin H
and some
Z
-minimal slot
(Q,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{b})
in
H
has a special refinement
{(Q_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)}
such that
(Q^{\phi}_{+b},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{b}-b)
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate;

(2)

\widehat{c}\notin H
and some
Z
-minimal slot
(R,{\mathfrak{m}},\widehat{c})
in
H
has a special refinement
{(R_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)}
such that
(R^{\phi}_{+c},{\mathfrak{n}},\widehat{c}-c)
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate.

Assume
Q
,
{\mathfrak{m}}
,
\widehat{b}
are as in Case (1). (Case (2) goes the same way.) Lemma
IV.3.7
gives
1\leqslant\operatorname{order}Q\leqslant 2r
.
Multiplicatively conjugating by
{\mathfrak{n}}
and renaming
Q_{+b,\times{\mathfrak{n}}}
and
\frac{\widehat{b}-b}{{\mathfrak{n}}}
as
Q
and
\widehat{b}
we arrange that
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
is a
Z
-minimal special slot such that

(Q^{\phi},1,\widehat{b})
is eventually deep, strongly repulsive-normal, and ultimate (using Lemma
IV.4.11
to preserve
ultimate
).
With Lemma
III.2.14
, changing
\widehat{b}
if necessary, we arrange that
(Q,1,\widehat{b})
is a hole in
H
, not just a slot in
H
.
∎

.

III

Errata and Comments to [ADH]

The changes below apply to the edition published by Princeton University Press, and are already reflected in the versions posted on the arXiv and on our personal web pages (as of February 2024). We thank Allen Gehret for pointing out most of the errors left in that edition. Linguistic slips like missing commas or articles are not listed below unless they might mislead. Citations are to the bibliography of [ADH].

Acknowledgments:

The date of September 2015 on p. xiv indicates when the manuscript
was first submitted to Princeton University Press. The published version incorporates some changes and additions made since then.

Dramatis Personae:

In the item for “
\upomega
-free” under the heading “Asymptotic Fields”,

f-\omega(g^{\dagger\dagger})\succcurlyeq g^{\dagger}
should be
f-\omega(g^{\dagger\dagger})\succcurlyeq(g^{\dagger})^{2}
.

Chapter 1:

(1)

The first sentence of the subsection
Irreducibility
in 1.1 should be:

Let
X
and
Y
be topological spaces
.

(2)

In the subsection
Localization of modules
in 1.4 the formula for addition should have
s_{2}x_{1}+s_{1}x_{2}
in the numerator.

(3)

In the subsection
Tensor products
in 1.7, the
H
in the 4th line should be a
B
.

(4)

In the subsection
Rational rank
in 1.7, in the line following the display:
{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}N
should be
{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}}M
.

(5)

In the 4th line of the proof of 1.8.12, the second “
:=
” should be “
=
”.

(6)

In the 6th line of the proof of 1.8.13, “
(a,b)\to
” should be “
(a,b)\mapsto
”.

(7)

In 1.9.6 one should add the assumption that
L
is separably generated over
K
, that is,
L
is separably algebraic over an intermediate field
K(B)
with
B\subseteq L
algebraically independent over
K
. This assumption is satisfied if
\operatorname{char}K=0
.
Corollary 1.9.7 is still correct as stated, but its proof requires for positive characteristic a variant of 1.9.6, namely:
L
is separably algebraic over
K
iff every derivation on
L
extending the trivial derivation on
K
is trivial
. (This variant with a proof, as in [249, pp. 370–371] is now included in the arXiv version.)
Lemma 1.9.8 should be restricted to the case
\operatorname{char}K=0
.

Chapter 2:

(1)

In the fourth paragraph of 2.3, replace “valued subgroup of
(G,S,v)
” by “valued subgroup of
(G^{\prime},S^{\prime},v^{\prime})
”.

Chapter 3:

(1)

In the second sentence of the proof of 3.1.21, one can omit
“with
\mathfrak{q}\cap A=\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\cap A=\mathfrak{m}
” since this condition is automatically satisfied.

(2)

F.-V. Kuhlmann pointed out that in the “Notes and comments” to 3.2 we misattribute 3.2.26 to Krull [229]. An
early source for a result of this kind is Theorem 11 in O. Schilling’s book,

The Theory of Valuations,

Mathematical Surveys, no. 4, American Mathematical Society, New York, 1950.

This book refers for this theorem to I. Kaplansky’s unpublished Ph.D. thesis

Maximal Fields with Valuations,
Harvard University, 1941.

(3)

Replace “theorem” by “proposition” in the sentence following the statement of 3.4.22.

(4)

Marcus Tressl alerted us to an error in the proof of 3.6.11: replace the condition
\bf{K}\preccurlyeq\bf{F}
in the first sentence of the proof by
\bf{K}\subseteq\bf{F}
, so that Zorn’s lemma can be applied as indicated in the next sentence.

(5)

Right after 3.7.6, replace

“open ball of the form
\{y:v(y-f)>vf\}
where
f\in K^{\times}
” by

“open ball of the form
\{y:v(y-f)>vg\}
where
f,g\in K^{\times}
,
f\succcurlyeq g
”.

(1)

In the first sentence of the proof of 4.1.10, omit
be.

(2)

The last three sentences of the proof of 4.6.12 can be shortened to:

Then by Lemma 1.3.10,
a
is algebraic over
K
, so
a
is algebraic over
C
by Lemma 4.1.2.

”.
